- **`read-summary` prescribed the `Explore` agent but never pointed at the reference owning the mechanics, so a session spawned three agents and then read the same files itself while waiting.** The rules were already written in `_shared/references/explore-delegation.md`; the skill using them had no `📖` to reach them. Added the pointer, and added the rule the reference was missing: not polling is not the same as not shadowing — re-reading the delegated files feels like progress rather than waiting, and pays for the same facts twice, with the agent's copy landing later and longer. Needing to fill the wait is the signal the delegation was scoped too wide.
- **The gather/judge split only covered the sweep planned up front, so a claim that decided the work's feasibility had nowhere to go.** `explore-delegation.md` now separates *what is there* (inventory — delegable, cheap to verify) from *does this hold* (a claim the plan rests on — stays inline, because the session has to defend it). The second kind usually can't be delegated even in principle: it doesn't exist until something contradicts an assumption, by which point the agents were briefed on the old one. Live case: an env file setting a flag its config defaults to off, where the follow-up read decided whether a whole test flow was reachable at all.
- **A non-empty agent return got less suspicion than an empty one, and the incomplete-sweep shape is the costlier of the two.** The zero-candidate rule already required a control query; nothing covered a list of real paths and real quotes that is simply short. Two returns in one session asserted absences that were wrong — "2 tests exist" where 9 did, and a plausible sibling route path while missing the one the work drove. Both are locally accurate and wrong as inventories, and a plan built on "X doesn't exist yet" duplicates what's already there. Now: run the one-line count yourself before acting on any absence an agent asserted.

## 1.140.11

- **A `haiku` dispatch that runs a named skill reported the result as its own, leaving the layering invisible.** When `haiku` launches an agent whose first action is `Skill(unhobble-instructions)`, the outcome is really unhobble-instructions' work product, verified by haiku — but the report read as one undifferentiated thing, which is how a verdict about the rewrite's correctness got argued from haiku's own reasoning instead of the target skill's stated rules. The report step now names the skill and its job in one line before presenting results, and says explicitly that a should-this-have-survived judgment belongs to whichever skill's own criteria govern the passage.

## 1.140.10

- **`haiku`'s verification step had no check for whether a dropped-looking rule was actually correct trimming, and eyeballing it got the wrong answer.** Two live `unhobble-instructions` dispatches against the same file: the first deleted whole sections and fabricated a companion file's existence in its own report (caught and reverted); the second was a mostly-clean rewrite with a dropped `**Tell:**` rule that read as the kind of mechanical step the skill exists to remove — until it was checked against `unhobble-instructions`' own stated bar for what a marker needs to earn its place, which it passed, meaning the drop was a real loss and had to be restored. The file now says: don't call that verdict from your own read of whether a passage looks rigid, apply the target skill's own fact-vs-constraint test to it directly. Also added: severity decides the response to a confirmed loss — systemic damage (missing sections, a claimed file that doesn't exist) is cheaper to revert-and-redo with the failure named in the retry prompt; a single contained gap in an otherwise-sound rewrite gets patched back in from the snapshot rather than discarding the whole pass.

## 1.140.9

- **`update-claude-docs`'s routing table said "team context → `CLAUDE.local.md`," which is backwards** — that file is scoped to personal, per-machine state, and a fact every teammate needs (a suite's fixture design, a worker count, a shared auth strategy) belongs in the team-visible `CLAUDE.md` even when it surfaced during env/test work. Both the Step-1 signal table and the Step-2 routing ladder now say "personal, per-machine (never team-wide facts)" instead of "team/strategy context," matching the CLAUDE.local.md checklist that was already correct a few lines below.
- **Capture mode had no path for "no session to scan, but the user names a subject to document."** Invoked right after `/clear` with only a bare topic and no conversation content, the mode's instructions (scan the conversation for signals) don't apply, so a run either stalls or invents an unwritten procedure. Capture mode now names the fallback explicitly: read the actual source for that subject and diff it against what CLAUDE.md currently claims, treating drift the same as a session-sourced signal — and say so rather than silently switching modes.

- **`/done` finished cleanly on a task doc that was over budget, because the rule requiring a condense lived in one skill and was checked by none** (reported upstream as issue #19). `task-summary` says an over-budget doc gets condensed via `condense-task-doc` rather than improvised on; `/done`'s exit gate verified only that `task-summary` ran and the doc changed — both true of a session that measures the overage, reports it, and moves on, so reporting the problem satisfied every check the workflow made. The measurement now runs at Step 4, the step that decides, and the exit gate reads `measuring is not condensing` beside the `invoking is not updating` clause already there. Over budget leaves two outcomes and no third: condense in the same turn, or say in the Output that it was skipped and why. This is the fourth recurrence of a shape already recorded here — a gate whose inputs no step computes resolves to its permissive default — and the notable part is that the previous fix was a checklist row, which is itself an unenforced gate.
- **A skill talked a user out of a safe operation, which cost more than the missing gate did.** Asked to condense, a session declined because the doc "would rewrite a lot of another session's content" — reasoning borrowed from the concurrent-session machinery and applied where it doesn't reach. That ownership guard's mechanism is `git diff HEAD`, which by construction sees only *uncommitted* work; content another session *committed* produces an empty diff and has no live writer to collide with, which is the ordinary state of every mature task doc. The guard stated the dirty case and never its converse, so "another session wrote this history" read as contested when it wasn't. Now scoped explicitly: contest is a property of the working tree, not of authorship history. Every existing contested branch is untouched, and the fix was checked in both directions — a clean doc reads uncontested, a genuinely dirty one still stops the pass.
- **The doc-set size command reported zero on any doc that had never been split.** `cat current.md decisions/*.md | wc -c` looks reasonable and fails silently: an unsplit doc has no `decisions/` directory, zsh aborts on the unmatched glob before `cat` runs, and `2>/dev/null` doesn't suppress it because the failure is shell-level rather than stderr. The set then measures 0 and passes any budget check by appearing empty — a 265-line doc reported as nothing. Replaced with a `find`-based form at all four places that carried it, including the pruner agent and its template. The defect was introduced by the fix above and caught by running it rather than reviewing it.

*No action needed — skills are re-read on every invocation. Docs you already created keep whatever shape they have; nothing regenerates them.*

## 1.140.8

- **The doc-writing skills contradicted each other on how to shape a doc, and one of them contradicted itself.** Five real disagreements, all between files that already shipped: `condense-task-doc` required table shape to match the templates "exactly" while `condense-claude-md` called shape "a means, not the thing being preserved" — opposite instructions on the same question, from sibling skills. Two files mandated different CLAUDE.md section orders. `condense-claude-md` said only an over-budget file may be split, then said to run the split analysis regardless. All resolved to one instruction each; the section order now has a single home, after a third copy of it turned up during verification.
- **The task-doc templates emitted empty tables.** `templates.md` shipped literal `| | |` rows under pre-seeded `### Backend` / `### Frontend` headings and a phantom `| B1 | Critical | | |`, so a doc created by following it literally contained empty sections and a bug row for a bug that didn't exist. Placeholders now show shape (`| [the gotcha] | [what to do about it] |`), and the split axis is stated as judgement — Backend/Frontend for most application docs, Hosting/Build-Pipeline for infra, no split at all for a single-stack doc. Column names are no longer locked "regardless of which axis is used", which had them fixed at two columns while the same file mandated three a few sections earlier.
- **A removed mandate can outlive itself in whatever enforces it.** `task-summary`'s structure check still called "wrong columns" drift against a template that no longer locks columns — found by grepping the mandate's own vocabulary rather than the changed file's name, which is the search that finds this class.
- **`writing-style.md` now carries the prose-vs-value boundary**, so every skill reading it gets the measured version rather than just `update-claude-docs`: prose for a constraint the reader reasons through, a literal row for an answer that is one specific string. This came from a live A/B test where the prose agent scored correctly on every judgement question and downgraded its own confidence on the one needing an exact value — a value dissolved into prose reads complete and can't be acted on.
- **The companion-split skills sent global-file companions to an unbacked-up location.** When splitting the global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`, both `condense-claude-md` and `update-claude-docs` asserted `~/.claude` "is not a git repo" and routed companions to the sibling `~/.claude-companions/`. Where `~/.claude` is a tracked dotfiles/settings repo, that sibling is outside the repo and its remote — the one backup — so a split silently wrote content to storage a disk failure would lose. Both skills now check whether `~/.claude` is a git repo rather than assuming, and place companions nested at `~/.claude/.claude-companions/` when it is.
- **`/done`'s Gate B asks what a new rule replaced.** The replace-or-route gate only ever hung off `update-plugin`, while rules mostly arrive as direct hand-edits — which is exactly what Gate B detects. It now asks which happened: replaced an existing rule, routed to `references/`, or grew with no retirement available. The third answer stays legitimate; it stops being the silent default, which is how a corpus reaches two additions per removal with every individual rule justified.

- **`unhobble-instructions` produced analysis instead of rewrites, and the fix was proportion.** Two live runs read a file, reasoned well, proposed a plan and wrote nothing — because the skill was ~34 lines of analysis instruction against one buried clause about writing. The deliverable is now the first sentence, the write step is its own section, and reports must name edits that landed rather than a strategy. Subsequent runs wrote.
- **Two content questions were added to the document-level read**, because the four that existed were all structural — order, placement, repetition, balance — so a faithful pass restructured a file and kept every rule. The new pair asks which enumerations stand in for one principle, and what a reader needs every invocation versus twice a year. The first run after this finally moved a 92-row lookup table behind a pointer, which three prior runs had left inline.
- **Cluster verdicts now precede per-rule judgement.** Every row in a thirty-row table passes the per-row fact-vs-constraint test individually, so a pass that descends straight to rows keeps the table and calls the enumeration irreducible. The question was never whether a row is true.
- **The conversion runs one direction only.** A run compressed ~500 words of judgement prose into a five-item checklist and reported it as "converting principle-shaped guidance back to prose" — the operation backwards, on the section teaching verification. Prose stating a mechanism is the finished product, not raw material.
- **A move now means the destination exists.** Three runs deleted content, wrote a `📖` pointer, never created the target, and reported every fact preserved — in good faith, since from the source side a deletion and an extraction look identical. Write the destination first, then cut.
- **`haiku` verification leads with reading, not counting.** It prescribed three counting checks (bytes, identifier survival, reworded-claim scan) with reading appended, which is the shape that trains counting. Now: one cheap check that bytes moved, then read the file whole. Counting answers "how many survived", which is a different question from "does this work".

*No action needed — skills are re-read on every invocation. Docs you already created keep whatever shape they have; nothing regenerates them.*

## 1.140.7

- **`unhobble-instructions` split into `SKILL.md` + `references/` (30.7KB → 17.8KB).** The skill taught progressive disclosure while being a single 30KB file with no references directory — it failed the shift it spent the most words on, which means it was prescribing something it didn't demonstrate. Target-type detail moved to `references/target-types.md`, verification detail and the delegation caveats to `references/verifying.md`, and the nine-step Process became prose with two `📖` pointers. The six shifts are now stated as the transformation's output contract rather than "angles the read is looking from": a rewritten file that still buries detail in the hot path hasn't been transformed no matter how much softer its wording reads.
- **A section of individually-true rows can be the most hobbling thing in a file, and proportion is what decides it.** Live case: this session cut ~200 verification rows from a global CLAUDE.md down to six paragraphs. Every row was true and every one had been added after a real incident, but two hundred rows on running a probe correctly against a handful of sentences on when to stop measuring and read the thing trains a reader to reach for an instrument every time — the instrumental options carry concrete actionable shape and the judgement is a line they scroll past. The session demonstrated it before diagnosing it: verification-by-anchor-count, reading a diff instead of the file, grepping docs for a token to decide whether prose still taught the right thing, and editing two table rows without once looking at them rendered. The file's own intro made it worse by opening with "grep this file for your symptom's vocabulary" — a lookup instruction on a document whose value was the reasoning. New document-level question ("what would a reader who absorbed all of this default to?") and a paragraph naming the collapse as a legitimate outcome even when nothing in the section is false.
- **Verification now says read, where it used to say don't.** The old step 5 read "grep for each fact... *rather than* reading back over the result" — steering away from the one check that catches what matters. The tension it was resolving is real (re-reading the result alone confirms your own intent), but the answer is reading against the original, not substituting a token sweep for reading. An identifier sweep answers one narrow question — did a name disappear — and is blind by construction to a claim restated into falsehood, a plausible wrong identifier, or a rule inverted while its vocabulary survives. Also added: read your own output in the form its reader meets it, since a rewrite that grows into the longest row in its section is invisible in the diff that produced it and obvious on sight.
- **`unhobble-instructions`: "read it end to end" now says what that excludes.** The pass was run this session against a diff, then a grep, then the parts that looked relevant — each producing confident findings about passages nobody opened, and each corrected by the user rather than by any check. Same for the article the skill is built on: summarised from memory it yielded a reading that was plausible, specific, and wrong about what the rules→judgement shift actually claims (the replacement Anthropic shipped keeps an imperative; the test is whether a rule is wrong in some case it will be applied to, not whether it's phrased as a prohibition).
- **`haiku`: re-measure the report's own numbers.** The skill warned that preservation claims cost an agent nothing to write; it didn't cover the case where the arithmetic itself is wrong. Two runs this session reported byte counts backwards (up when the file shed 7.6KB) and a fixed anchor count with three added. A report contradicting itself — lines cut, bytes up — has done arithmetic on numbers it never took.
- **`done`: a finding that contradicts what you know you did is a baseline question first.** `/done` runs against uncommitted work, so an agent reasoning from `git log` and `HEAD` describes the tree as it was before the session — real commits, accurate quotes, inverted conclusion. This session produced one refutation asserting a section was untouched while 192 of its rows were gone from the working tree, beside a second agent that read the file and confirmed the cut.
- **`update-claude-docs`: `**Tell:**` is no longer the house default, and `YOU MUST` is no longer taught as an adherence lever.** `references/structure.md` prescribed prose "ending in a `**Tell:**` sentence" as the default form for judgement-shaped rules while `_shared/references/writing-style.md` already said to prefer the mechanism and drop the tell — six skills reference the latter, so `structure.md` was the outlier, not the convention. Four spots reconciled (§4 conventions row, two template blocks, the Gotchas template) plus `other-modes.md`'s rewrite instruction. The emphasis row now distinguishes `⚠️`/`IMPORTANT` on a fact whose cost is real from an imperative restating reasoning the reader would reach anyway.
- **The post's sixth shift was missing from the skill's own list**, which claimed six and listed five — CLAUDE.md-memory → auto-memory is now named.
- **The skill's verdicts on the article are now stated in the skill, not cited from it.** `/doctor` was added as a recommended pre-pass step and removed again once D55 turned up having already graded it false against the installed tooling; the first repair attempt was an instruction to go grep `tasks/**/decisions/` before adopting outside guidance, which is the same defect one level up — a lookup where a fact belongs. A skill built on an outside source carries the graded result: what the 80% claim does and doesn't license, the value-vs-judgement boundary, and that `/doctor`'s flags describe its own run time. D-verdict-records-lever in `external-guidance` records the restatement rule that produced the inverted "rejected here" line.

## 1.140.6

- **New shared reference `cross-session-messaging.md` — a session can now learn a peer is editing the same files before their bytes land.** Two Claude sessions on one project overwrite each other silently, and the existing detection (`diff-ownership.md`, `contested-doc-sections.md`) is disk-bound: a peer who is mid-edit, or holding changes in memory, does not exist yet as far as those rules can tell. `ListAgents`/`SendMessage` (Claude Code 2.1.224+) close that gap and nothing wider, so the check is hosted at `read-summary`'s re-trigger block rather than at each write site — `read-summary` opens ~9/10 sessions and runs before both code and doc work, while a write-site check fires after the session has already been editing for an hour. Two of the four documented concurrency problems were judged out of scope on inspection: they are intra-session subagent races with no session boundary to cross, so messaging is the wrong tool reached for by name similarity. The feature demonstrated its own limits during implementation — three files in the checkout changed mtime within a minute while the listing showed one idle peer in an unrelated project, so **an empty listing is not evidence that nobody is writing**, and where the listing and `git status` disagree, `git status` is the one that observed a write. A peer's existence is information and a peer's reply is a courtesy; neither gates a write, the diff-content check still decides.
- **`CLAUDE.md` cut from 33.4KB to 17.6KB, with its Maintenance checklist extracted to `_shared/references/editing-skills-checklist.md`.** The four generated agent files (`Explore`, `Plan`, `code-reviewer`, `code-simplifier`) all cited that section by name and were repointed in the same change — a stale pointer here is silent, since the agent simply reads a section that no longer exists and proceeds. **If you have run `/agent-setup` in your own project, re-run it** to pick up the corrected pointers; skills are re-read every invocation and need nothing, but generated agent files do not update themselves.
- **A stated limitation is the fact an unhobbling pass deletes first.** Running `unhobble-instructions` across five docs inverted two passages by cutting the text that said what a tool *can't* do, which reads as hedging: "`ListAgents` cannot tell you which project a peer is in" became "checking for concurrent sessions prevents contested edits" — asserting the exact guarantee the reference exists to withhold. One agent reported the deletion as an achievement, and all four reported zero facts lost. No token check sees this class: every identifier survives and only the claim changes, so the verification is reading the passage against its original and asking what a reader would now do differently. The fact-vs-constraint test now names the shape, and the identifier-survival sweep is recorded as blind to it.
- **`haiku`: one task per agent call — the partition rule covered width but not depth.** The skill said how to split work *across* files (no two agents on the same file) and nothing about how much to put *into* one agent, so a chain — two skills, or one skill over two targets — read as a legitimate dispatch. That's where the tier stops being reliable: a later step in the chain overwrites the agent's own account of an earlier one, and the closing report describes the last thing it did as though it covered everything. Live case: the user asked for "1 agent 1 skill 1 doc" across two skills and two docs, which is four calls; the constraint came from the user, not from the skill, and nothing in the file would have produced it. Ordering those calls stays a judgement about contention rather than a default — disjoint targets can go out in one message, legs queueing behind a file another agent is still writing cannot, and a request for sequential work usually means "don't let these collide" rather than "one agent at a time."
- **`haiku`: the verification instruments all measure shape, and reporting their output as a content judgement is its own defect.** The skill already warned that reworded facts read as missing to a grep — that warning fired and got walked past, because every check it prescribes (byte delta, heading diff, identifier set) produces a number that *feels* like evidence about content. Live case: four sequential dispatches over a task-doc set were reported as having dropped 111 identifiers from one file; on inspection the extraction regex had swept in commit SHAs, ClickUp ids and ordinary words like `action`/`database`, and six of the ten names spot-checked as "genuinely lost" were sitting in the `decisions/*.md` siblings where `task-summary` had correctly moved them. The honest count was four defects, and every one was found by reading passages side by side, not by any diff. The user's own read ("i only care about content") was what surfaced it. Two changes: a count of missing identifiers is now explicitly a question to go answer rather than a finding to report, and a prose-against-prose pass is named as the check that catches what shape checks structurally cannot — a fact restated into falsehood, a plausible wrong identifier substituted for a real one, a status claim the source never made. All three of those shipped in this session's rewrites and all three passed every structural check.
- **`two-tier-condense.md`: the identifier grep assumes the rewritten file is the fact's only home.** That holds for a single-file condensation and breaks for any pass whose job is redistribution — `task-summary` pushing detail out of an index into its owning decision file, a split moving a section to a sibling. The better the skill does that job, the more the check reads it as mass deletion. New section: grep the sibling files before calling a term lost, treat header/`Last updated:` narrative dispersing into the rows that now own it as expected, and resolve what a large loss figure is actually made of before reporting it. `haiku` advertises dispatching both redistributing skills in its own frontmatter, so the gap was reachable from its main path.

## 1.140.5

- **`/done`: adding a case to a shared type is a seam the existing rule couldn't catch.** Step 1's cross-partition guidance said to trace each new value forward to a consuming site — which finds nothing when the change introduces no new value. Live case: adding a `missing_api_key` case to a failure-category enum was handled at the detail screen that motivated it, while the library row kept offering a "Try again" button that re-failed forever (that category consumes no retry budget, so the attempts ceiling never disabled it), and a new typed `QuotaExceededException` was parsed by the client and branched on nowhere, leaving two screens telling a quota-blocked user to check their connection. Both surfaced only because reviewers were pointed at the sibling files. The new bullet names the inverted trace and the two things that make it silent: every reader already compiles, and its default branch absorbs the new case. Grep the primitive's name, not the feature's — the sibling call site never mentions the feature.

## 1.140.4

- **`read-summary` had no path to the private journal, so finding it was luck.** New Read Order step 6: weigh `~/.claude/notes/` when the question turns on a judgement someone already made (assessing an opportunity, pricing, drafting for a specific person) rather than on how code behaves. Live case: a session assessing two job postings loaded the user's triage filter and pricing stance only because one task doc happened to name the journal in `Related:` — the sibling doc didn't, so opening it first would have produced a confident assessment with the commercial reasoning entirely absent. The failure is silent by construction: the journal is machine-local and gitignored, so `grep -rl` over the project tree returns nothing and reads as "no such context exists." Step 6 is deliberately scoped — ordinary implementation and debugging has no reason to go looking, and a blanket rule would have every session grepping a personal journal for unrelated work.
- **`read-summary` trigger: an opportunity to assess is a distinct routing case.** The description listed bug reports, ClickUp tickets and deploy questions; a pasted job posting or quote request matched none of them, so the skill that owns "load context before answering" didn't own the moment context mattered most.
- **`notes-summary` now names the discoverability problem from the writing side.** The skill explained at length why the journal must stay out of the repo, and nothing said how a later session is meant to find it — a thread recording *why* a decision was made only earns its keep if a session reading around that decision can reach it.

## 1.140.3

- **`/ship`: a changelog heading you just consolidated is the weakest evidence of release scope, not the strongest.** Step 5 already said to settle the note's boundary from the commit range rather than "whichever heading happens to be nearby" — but it framed that as a *stale*-heading risk, so it didn't fire on a heading edited seconds earlier. Live case: folding a project's `[Unreleased]` backlog into today's dated heading (a correct changelog fix) left 31 items sitting under today's date while the push carried 3 commits; the note went out covering all 31 until the user cut it back. Nothing looks wrong at the time — the entries are real, dated today, and sit directly above your own new bullet. The rule now names consolidation explicitly and gives the tell: a large gap between the commit range's item count and the heading's bullet count means you're reading a merged section, not a release.

## 1.140.2

- **`condense-claude-md`: a table's shape is a means, the dropped cell's content is what must survive.** The old hard rule pinned the format itself (`Symptom | Cause | Fix` "stays verbatim"), which reads as protecting the header when the real risk is what leaves with the column. A delegated pass collapsed a 49KB backend CLAUDE.md to 3-column→2-column and took 167 backticked identifiers and 9 of 23 `See <Class>` pointers with it — the merged rows read complete, so nothing flagged the loss. Collapsing to two columns or to prose is now explicitly legitimate when the result reads better; renaming columns you're *keeping* stays discouraged, with the actual reason attached (it breaks the positional `awk -F'|'` checks further down the same file).
- **`haiku`: a report volunteering a preservation claim is asserting what your checks measure.** "Preserved all exact references to classes, methods, migrations" sat above the diff described above. Folded into the existing report-distrust sentence rather than added as a new paragraph — the surrounding ground (self-report is not evidence) was already covered three times across `explore-delegation.md`, `verifying-a-write-landed.md` and this skill.
- **A presence check on markup passes while the markup is broken.** `unhobble-instructions` step 6 now covers inline delimiters alongside line-prefix ones: a delegated pass emitted `{{#anchor}}` for eight anchors, and the sweep confirming they survived matched on substring, so `{#anchor}` was "found" in every corrupted one — the check agreed with the agent's own claim that nothing was lost. Match the delimiter exactly or read the raw span; a passing presence check on markup is unfinished, not clean.
- **A dispatch prompt's "boundaries" carve-out is for facts that hold, not guardrails in general** (`_shared/references/explore-delegation.md`). It licenses naming contested files and commands that must not run — which invited "don't edit the sibling files" and "don't run destructive git" into a prompt whose tree was clean and whose companions nothing threatened. Both read as diligence and land as the over-specification the same paragraph warns against. If you can't name the specific file or command a clause protects, it isn't a session fact.
- **`unhobble-instructions`: new step 6 — check the file still renders.** Softening a rule means rewriting prose that sits inside a blockquote, list item or table cell, and the line-prefix marker is easy to drop with the sentence it prefixed; stripping `>` from a blockquote's lead line while the child bullets keep theirs detaches the intro from its own list. Every fact survives and every grep passes, so neither the fact-survival pass nor an identifier sweep can see it. Steps 6–8 renumbered to 7–9; no internal or external reference cites those numbers.
- **`/quick-done` is now docs-only: it captures into CLAUDE.md and no longer reviews your code.** Step 1 was a code reviewer; it is now `update-claude-docs`, so the skill runs the CLAUDE.md capture plus `task-summary` and reads nothing for defects. The reasoning: a two-line fix rarely has bugs a reviewer finds, but the one reusable thing it taught you dies unrecorded, and the old shape named that loss without preventing it. **If you use `/quick-done` before `/ship`, your code is now unreviewed** — `/ship`'s prerequisites say so explicitly, and `/done` remains the option that reviews. Skills are re-read on every invocation, so nothing needs regenerating; just `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit`.
- **The 31.9KB figure published for `/quick-done` in 1.140.0 no longer describes it — it now loads 53.6KB against `/done`'s 76.6KB.** Adding the capture pass roughly halves the saving, from ~58% to ~30% (`cat` the skill plus everything it invokes, `wc -c`). If you picked `/quick-done` on the strength of the old number, that decision was sized against a skill that no longer exists; it is still the cheaper option, by less.
- **Both ownership gates (`/done` Gate B, `/quick-done` Step 3) now want a diff read, not just a timestamp.** They settled "did *this* session edit the plugin?" by comparing file mtime against session start, which only excludes work finished *before* you started — a second Claude session editing the shared checkout at the same time stamps its files inside your window too, and every one of them passes as yours. Found live: three foreign skill edits read as owned. The failure is biased toward over-claiming, so the gate hands more files to this skill than it should and can version-bump someone else's in-flight work under your changelog entry.

## 1.140.0

- **New skill `quick-done`** — the cheap sibling of `/done`, for a session you already know was small. One reviewer agent, one `task-summary` pass, and `/done`'s plugin gate; no simplifier, no product reviewer, no CLAUDE.md capture. Measured: `/done`'s instruction load is 75.8KB (itself plus `update-claude-docs` and `task-summary`) across 3–7 agent context windows, with two full-conversation scans running back-to-back in the main context — a floor that barely moves for a two-line fix. `/quick-done` is 31.9KB (itself plus `task-summary`) and one agent window. `/done` is unchanged and stays the default: this fires only when you name it.
- **Why a separate skill rather than a light mode inside `/done`** — a light mode existed and was removed in 1.117.0 (D39) because it threaded a fourth case through four of `/done`'s decision points. That ADR recorded the reversal as available if the agent spend on small sessions proved unwanted; a standalone file takes it without re-adding a branch to `/done`. The name carries the size signal deliberately, since `/done`'s own description already claims the phrase "wrap up" and a name reusing it would leave the two competing for one trigger.
- **The tradeoff is real and stated in the skill itself**: dropping the `update-claude-docs` pass means a broadly reusable pattern from that session — an env gotcha, a convention worth keeping — will not reach CLAUDE.md. The skill says so and points at `/update-claude-docs` or `/done` instead of quietly re-adding the step. Running both doc skills is what D42 made sequential, and it's most of what makes `/done` slow.
- **`/ship` now accepts either as its prerequisite** (frontmatter and Prerequisites list, both), and `/tackle` — whose body hardcoded `invoke done` — now judges size at the end instead. A caller's description can't reveal a hardcoded terminal call, so adding a sibling skill means opening every caller, not reasoning about them.
- **Every agent this plugin spawns holds `Bash`, including the ones whose role reads as read-only.** Checked rather than assumed: `Explore` carries `Bash Write Edit` and `claude-md-pruner` carries `Bash Edit`, while only `/done` told a spawned agent not to run `stash`/`reset`/`clean`/`checkout -- .`. The ban is now a shared reference (`agent-prompt-verb-ban.md`) that `/quick-done`, `/update-claude-docs` and both `Explore` callers point at. Nothing is known to have been lost to this — it was a gap in what the skills instructed, found while writing a new one.

## 1.139.15

- **New skill `haiku`** — dispatches a task, or a named skill, to one or more `haiku` agents and verifies the result before reporting. Written after three delegated runs in one session each returned a confident summary with something wrong in it; the worst case had its headline structural claim (a section renamed, a table restructured) simply not happen, while the real work sat further down. So the skill's centre of gravity is the return path: confirm the bytes moved, check identifier and number survival against a pre-dispatch snapshot, then read the diff for claims restated in new words — the failure a grep can't see, since a reword keeps the identifier and falsifies only the sentence around it. Carries the two dispatch facts that aren't derivable: `model: "haiku"` applies to `general-purpose` only (passing it to a registered agent silently overrides that agent's frontmatter tier), and a judgement-skill gets less useful the more the prompt specifies.
- **Reversed the "no spawned agent" rule for condensation drafting** (`_shared/references/two-tier-condense.md`) — an earlier revision had dropped Haiku delegation per explicit preference; the preference has changed and the rule now allows it, because the draft/verify split is what makes it safe. Measured this session: a Haiku condense of a 375-line CLAUDE.md cut 35% of bytes with the rules intact. The delegation is permitted, the verification is not — an agent grading its own rewrite is the same read that produced it, so its closing report is an artifact to check rather than evidence. `condense-claude-md` and `condense-task-doc` had the "work inline, no spawned agent" phrasing inline and now point at the shared file instead.
- **Named the failure an identifier grep cannot catch: a reword that keeps the identifier and falsifies the claim around it** (`two-tier-condense.md`, `unhobble-instructions`). A delegated condense relabelled a project's kebab-case localStorage keys as "camelCase" and downgraded a hyphen-vs-underscore resource-name trap to "case-sensitive" — both survive every backtick-identifier check, since the identifier is present and only the sentence about it changed. Delegation makes this likely because the drafter compresses prose it never verified against the codebase. Any factual claim a rewrite states in new words is unverified until settled against the source the file describes.
- **A prior agent's failure on the same target is the strongest pull toward over-specifying the retry** (`_shared/references/explore-delegation.md`) — the existing rule already said that handing an agent a judgement-skill inverts clause-stacking, and named `unhobble-instructions` as its example; it was violated anyway. Narrating the predecessor's mistake into the prompt primes the retry to under-edit, and closing with "finding little is a fine outcome" reads as permission to do nothing. The prior failure is a reason to tighten the parent's own post-return verification, which can't be talked out of firing — not a reason to spend prompt on it.
- **`/done`'s own review pass on this version's diff found two more gaps before shipping**: `haiku/SKILL.md` restated `explore-delegation.md`'s prompt-writing guidance and `two-tier-condense.md`'s verification steps instead of pointing to them (caught independently by two agents, now collapsed to pointers); and `two-tier-condense.md` described delegating to `haiku` as *possible* without ever stating the actual dispatch call — now names `Skill(skill: "syafiqkit:haiku")` explicitly.

## 1.139.14

- **New skill `setup-playwright`** — scaffolds a Playwright E2E suite, or hardens an existing one that isn't trusted. Written from a session that measured a real cross-file race and fixed it. The three findings it carries, none of which are derivable from a fresh repo: (1) `test.describe.serial` orders tests only WITHIN a file, so two files touching the same rows interleave under `fullyParallel` — the fix is partitioning data per worker, not a lockfile, whose stale-lock and timeout-inversion failures both read as unrelated bugs; (2) partition on `workerInfo.parallelIndex`, never `workerIndex`, which keeps incrementing as replacement workers spawn (measured at `--workers=10`: raw indexes 4 and 10 resolved to the same fixture); (3) login routes are commonly rate-limited, and driving the form for many identities trips the limit while surfacing as a login page that never navigates rather than a 429 — authenticate via the API and hand-write `storageState`. Also carries the two absence-assertion rules that catch real bugs (`toHaveCount(0)` over `not.toBeVisible()`, and a must-be-present assertion before any absence check, since a blank page satisfies every absence assertion in a file), and the fixture-inversion test for proving a spec isn't vacuous.
- **`.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` was stale at 1.139.11 while `plugin.json` read 1.139.13** — an earlier bump touched only one of the two. Both now at 1.139.14.
- **`setup-playwright` gained a 4th flake cause (environment/browser drift)** — the trigger phrase "tests only pass on my machine" had no matching section; `/done`'s product reviewer caught the gap same-session, before the skill shipped.
- **`code-simplifier`'s generated agent file had silently drifted to `model: opus` against its own already-correct `sonnet` template** — surfaced as a `/done` spawn 400ing on `effort 'xhigh' not supported`, not as an edit. Fixed by syncing the generated file; `/done`'s spawn-fault recovery step now points at diffing the generated agent against its template as the durable fix, not just re-dispatching on a different tier.

## 1.139.13

- **Delegating a judgement SKILL to an agent inverts the clause-stacking rule the same reference already gives for delegated audits.** A session handed `unhobble-instructions` to four agents with prompts that pre-listed which facts must survive, prescribed the exact grep counts to hit, and scripted the conclusion each agent was permitted to reach — leaving the skill nothing to decide. The user stopped it with "why u give explicit instruction like that?". The over-specified sibling run was also the one that went wrong: a whole-doc rewrite agent deleted 42 table rows and 5 `⚠️` warnings (including a production root-cause finding traced from `activity_log`, a validation trap, and two named prod incidents) while reporting "zero fact loss" — and the minimal-prompt runs afterwards lost nothing. `_shared/references/explore-delegation.md` now names this case: give the skill name, the target and the ownership boundary; the constraints worth writing are the ones a stranger to the session couldn't infer. Where the worry is destruction, prompt text can't fail loudly — snapshot and diff instead, which is also the only thing that catches an agent reporting success over deleted content.
- **`task-summary`: a delegated rewrite's own "no rows lost" check is not evidence, and the byte delta is the tell.** Validation step 4 already required a diff pass; it now names who must run it (whoever acts on the result, with counts captured BEFORE dispatch — afterwards there's nothing to compare against) and what the number means: restructuring moves text rather than removing it, so a byte drop past roughly a tenth means content went, and a set of files each shedding a third is a deletion wearing a rewrite's description.
- **`task-summary`: in a split doc set, every open actionable belongs in the index.** Recorded from a user telling us twice — *"actionable should be kept in current.md"*, then *"it's hard for me to check few files, i'd rather check current.md only."* Splitting a doc decides where explanation lives, not where the backlog lives; per-file `Next Steps` sections mean opening four files to answer the question the index exists to answer. Each index item is one line plus a pointer; each decisions file points back. Carve-out for a checklist ticked while performing a procedure, which stays beside it.

## 1.139.12

- **`update-claude-docs` Capture mode: first-time entries default to plain prose, not imperative callouts.** A new signal's default shape is statement-of-fact prose (no `⚠️` callout, no "Never conclude X" trigger phrase, no prescribed-reasoning sequences) — the entry should already read the way `unhobble-instructions` would rewrite it, so running that skill afterward is a no-op. Reserve the imperative shape exclusively for the "Violations → Escalate" path, after a rule has already been violated. The Step 3 guidance now folds this into the existing paragraph on entry shape, clarifying the distinction: first capture is plain prose, repeat violation is escalated with sharpness.

## 1.139.11

- **Two upstream issues fixed (#16, #18), verified against each report's own text rather than assumed resolved.** #16: `/commit`'s task-doc staleness gate had only one carve-out (a lexical/identifier-shaped match) against its "no judgment call" rule — a `/done → /commit/ship` chain routinely hits the gate after `/done` Step 4 already ran `task-summary` scoped earlier the same session, making a fresh full re-invocation redundant. Added a second, equally mechanical carve-out naming that exact case, cross-referencing `/done` Step 4's own scoped-invoke principle instead of restating it. #18: `/done` Step 1's "Ownership" (file-partitioning) guard sat ~45 lines before the "Emission shape" rule (all `Agent` calls in one message), and a session doing non-trivial ownership reasoning lost track of the later rule. First fix reordered Emission-shape before Ownership — but the product reviewer caught that reordering alone only helps a cold read, not the reporter's actual failure (which happened *after* a full read, once attention was absorbed by the partitioning work). Added a one-line re-anchor at the exact point ownership reasoning ends and `Agent`-call drafting begins. Both fixes and the full `/done` review pass (8 additional findings: a typo, em-dash spacing, a silently-narrowed `tasks/shared/*.md` discovery step, an `Explore.md`/template `disallowedTools` parity break introduced by a concurrent agent mid-review, stale cross-references, a duplicated example) are recorded as ADRs in `tasks/plugin-maintenance/agent-architecture/decisions/{concurrency-and-delegation,verification-rigor}.md`. `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit` to pick it up; no prior output invalidated — this only affects future `/commit`/`/done` runs.

## 1.139.10

- **`Explore`'s description already said "or once you already have the exact file path... use Read directly" — the fix was repositioning it, not adding a second copy.** A session dispatched Explore to confirm a fact it had already derived by rereading a file it had open (`SubscriptionInvoice.php`'s scopes), when the actual next step was reading one more named file (`Invoice.php`) directly — the description's own carve-out already covered this, it was just the trailing clause after two unrelated exclusions (code review, design-doc auditing), sitting behind an emphatic "Dispatch it for ANY locate-a-thing ask... even a single-symbol lookup" earlier in the same paragraph. Per this skill's own test, adding a louder second warning would have been the exact anti-pattern it exists to catch — a rule restated because the first one "didn't stick." Instead the description now opens with the actual fork ("for when the LOCATION is unknown"), states the dispatch test once with its reasoning attached (round-trip cost, a summary that can drop an exact line number or caveat a direct Read wouldn't lose), and drops the "even a single-symbol lookup" emphasis that was pulling attention away from the boundary. Same edit in both the project's `.claude/agents/Explore.md` and `agent-setup/templates/Explore.template.md` (template version omits the session-specific worked example — that belongs in this changelog entry, not in a file other projects copy verbatim). `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit` to pick it up; no prior output invalidated.

## 1.139.9

- **`Explore` agent (both the project-generated copy and its template) and `read-summary` unhobbled — real duplication removed, one lossy pass caught and fixed before landing.** Three files dispatched to parallel haiku agents: the project's `.claude/agents/Explore.md`, its template `agent-setup/templates/Explore.template.md`, and `skills/read-summary/SKILL.md`. All three had the same shape of problem — a single principle ("run discovery unconditionally", "never produce a document") restated three-to-five times across different sections with different framing, each restatement apparently patched in independently rather than consolidating the last one. All three rewrites collapsed the repetition into one clearly-positioned statement per principle and were verified via the identifier-survival grep this skill prescribes rather than trusting each agent's own "all facts verified" claim. That check caught a real miss: `read-summary`'s rewrite silently dropped the `references/claude-md-tree-walk.md` pointer from the CLAUDE.md tree-walk step and an entire paragraph on routing scope-vs-fix decisions through `AskUserQuestion` (finding a defect is the deliverable; designing its fix is unscoped work) — both restored by hand after the mechanical diff flagged them, exactly the failure mode this skill's own Step 5 warns a self-report can mask. Separately, `Explore`'s `disallowedTools: [Write, Edit]` was removed from both the project copy and the template — the frontmatter's own comment already noted the built-in Explore agent carries Write/Edit for Plan Mode's document editing, and the block was silently overriding that; `Explore` now keeps Write/Edit for a Plan Mode plan file or scratchpad only, with the body prose narrowed from "never write" to "never write application/source code" everywhere that assumption was baked in. `plan-worklist` was evaluated and skipped — already lean judgement prose with no duplication or rigid imperatives, so running the pass on it would have manufactured a finding. `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit` to pick it up; no prior output is invalidated, this only reshapes agent instructions.

## 1.139.8

- **`done`'s Test-row rule named only "a review agent" as the thing not to restate, so restating the MAIN session's own already-run commands slipped through the gate it was supposed to close.** The rule already said "not a restatement of what a review agent already ran," and its own example (`composer test`) was itself a command the main session runs directly, not something a review agent runs — so a `/done` invocation filled every Test row with `composer test (23/23 pass)`, `flutter analyze (0 issues)`, and an offline-suite result, all of which were already reported earlier in the same conversation. The rule now says explicitly that "already ran" means anything the user has already seen executed this session, by anyone — not narrowly a review agent's own run — and the Test row exists for what the user still has to go verify themselves. The omission condition gained a matching branch: a change whose only meaningful check was a suite already run and reported has nothing left to test, same as a doc-only edit. `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit` to pick it up.

## 1.139.7

- **`done` skill reduced overconstraint: parallelism emphasis softened, procedural mandates became judgement prose, and Exit gate moved before Output table.** Three structural findings from an `unhobble-instructions` pass: (1) Parallelism was emphasized three times across Step 1 — repeated explanation that harness runs agents in background by default, costing reader attention without adding new information — narrowed to one statement that parallelism is enforced by message shape. (2) Ownership guard opened with rigid procedures ("Ban N verbs", "git status is canonical"), softened to state the principle ("only commit content you wrote") and let the reader apply it; similar softening across Step 1 agent-spawning rules and Step 5's mtime verification, trading mechanical checks for reasoning prose. (3) Exit gate moved before Output section — the gate verifies that steps actually ran, and the Output rows claim they did, so the gate must precede what it verifies; it was positioned after the template, forcing a reader to either re-read or assume they understand what makes a step "filled" vs. "skipped". Mode selection framing also clarified that each mode cascades to downstream steps, rather than mode choice being orthogonal to execution. All genuine facts about git mechanics, agent fallbacks, and product-reviewer absence survived the rewrite; what changed is delivery — no more trip-wires installed to force adherence to what the surrounding context already makes obvious.

## 1.139.6

- **`done`'s agent-prompt ban list was missing `git commit` and `git push`, and an agent used the gap.** The Ownership guard told you to ban five verbs in every agent prompt — `stash`/`checkout -- .`/`reset`/`clean`/`restore` — justified by "it takes the unstaged, unrecoverable work with it". That destructiveness test is exactly why the two missing verbs read as correctly excluded rather than forgotten: they destroy nothing, so no gate trips. A session followed the list precisely and a Step 1 review agent then committed *and pushed*, sweeping in three files from a parallel session that the prompts had explicitly named off-limits. The list is now seven verbs with a rationale covering the non-destructive case, and notes that the Commit row above it addresses *you* while the list addresses the *agents* — satisfying one does nothing for the other, which is what made two adjacent lines feel like one handled concern. `**After all complete:**` also now opens by re-reading `git rev-parse HEAD` and `git status -sb`, since an agent's commit is otherwise invisible to every remaining check and silently invalidates the partition you reasoned from. `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit` to pick it up.

## 1.139.5

- **`done`'s Output table groups by CHANGE, not by workflow step, and gains a Test row.** The old table was one row per agent/skill (Simplify/Review/Product/Cleanup/Knowledge/Task docs/Plugin) — fine for a session that built one thing, but a session touching several distinct features smeared every finding across the same seven rows with no way to tell which belonged to which. A user asked directly for the summary to group "whatever was done this session" and to say how to test it. The Output section now partitions the session's diff into coherent changes, gives each its own heading with only the rows that apply, and requires a concrete Test instruction (a command or a short manual step) per change that has one — omitted, never filled with "N/A", when a change genuinely has nothing to verify. Knowledge/Task docs/Plugin stay session-level since they're shared-doc writes, not attached to one feature. `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit` to pick it up.

## 1.139.4

- **New skill: `plan-worklist`.** Turns a pre-scoped list of items — findings from a `read-summary` pass, a pasted backlog, a ClickUp export, a brainstormed todo list — into a scoped build plan without writing any code. Dispatches `product-reviewer` (read-only, plan-producing by design) to judge each item against the product's task-doc intent, size it, and flag sequencing between items, then presents the plan and stops by default. Fills the gap between `tackle` (builds directly against one doc) and `done` (reviews code that's already built) — this one triages a list *before* anything gets built. `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit` to pick it up.

## 1.139.3

- **`condense-claude-md`: an existing companion file is not automatically the right home for a newly-split section, just because it already covers the same layer.** A session splitting an oversized `app/CLAUDE.md` extracted an Http Layer section and a miscellaneous-gotchas section, and appended both to the layer's one pre-existing companion (`CLAUDE-backend-patterns.md`) rather than checking whether that file's existing scope actually matched the new content's topic — it didn't, and the append recreated the same broad grab-bag the skill's own "one heterogeneous block → multiple topic-scoped files" rule already warns against, just under a filename that predated the split. The user caught it mid-turn ("i dont [want] big companion files", "backend-pattern too broad") and the fix was reverting the append and writing two narrowly-scoped files instead. `references/structural-splits.md`'s existing rule now names this explicitly: the "already has a home" exception was previously stated only for a subdir CLAUDE.md (lever #6); it now also covers an existing companion that is ALREADY scoped to the same narrow topic, and warns that reusing a broader one "because it's already there" is the same mistake wearing a pre-existing filename.

## 1.139.2

- **`unhobble-instructions` and `self-organize-agent-memory`: an empty diff on a delegated agent's target is authoritative, and outranks the agent's own closing report — a distinct failure from the lossy-content case 1.139.1 already covers.** A session dispatched 8 project agents in parallel to unhobble their own definition files; 7 produced real, verifiable rewrites, and the 8th returned a detailed, specific-sounding account (a named section removed, a mandate consolidated, self-referential guard rows confirmed intact) against a file whose `git diff` was empty and whose mtime predated the session — the target was never touched. This is a step earlier than the identifier-survival check either skill already runs: that check presumes an edit happened and only asks what it lost, so it passes trivially — every identifier "survives" — on a file that was never edited at all. `_shared/references/verifying-a-write-landed.md` gained the mirror-direction note (an empty diff, once the three known false-negative causes are ruled out, beats the report claiming otherwise), and both skills now point to it as the check that has to run *before* the identifier sweep, from the orchestrating session, never trusted from the delegate's own account.

## 1.139.1

- **`unhobble-instructions`: table→prose conversion is a legitimate rewrite on an on-demand reference table, and a delegated agent's own "facts verified intact" is not the verification step.** A session ran the skill via 7 parallel haiku agents against sibling companion files, each holding a fixed `❌ NEVER | ✅ ALWAYS` convention; 4 of 7 reports claimed full content survival while having actually dropped a method name, a constant, or a measured figure per file, and a separate misreading of the row-scannability rule treated the header format itself as sacred, reverting a legitimate consolidation for the wrong reason. What actually caught the loss was extracting every backtick-quoted identifier from the pre-edit original and grepping each against the rewrite — the existing step-5 verification language covered a single session checking its own work, not a batch of delegated reports grading themselves. The target-type section for on-demand tables now says explicitly that a fixed table convention is this codebase's chosen way of keeping rows bounded, not the only way — prose is fine provided each unit stays a self-contained lookup chunk — and step 5 gained a paragraph naming the delegated-batch case and the identifier-diff check as the thing that actually verifies it, run from the orchestrating session, not inside the delegate's own prompt.

## 1.139.0

- **`product-reviewer` gains two Process steps — query the DATA, and check what the customer was SOLD.** A session ran the agent against an Enterprise tier whose selling point (a consolidated cross-agency view) turned out not to exist: every part reviewed clean because each part was correctly built, and the missing thing was the headline capability rather than any line of code. The same session's earlier passes had also missed a picker whose top row was an agency literally named "aaaa" and an "Add your first agency" card still saying that at four agencies — both invisible from reading the rendering code, both obvious from one query. Neither gap is a correctness defect, which is why the code reviewer could not have caught them either. The steps went into `skills/agent-setup/templates/product-reviewer.template.md` (the durable home — a generated `.claude/agents/` copy is lost on regeneration) and were ported to the eight existing project copies, whose Process lists start at different numbers, so the insertion had to be per-file rather than one `replace_all`.
- **`task-summary`: a named environment resource in Quick Start decays with nothing in the doc changing.** A doc confidently named local fixtures (`org 44`, `org 37`) that a DB restore had destroyed, and every surrounding claim still read as authoritative — the existing staleness rules all target cross-doc status mirrors and hedge words, none of which fire on an id that simply no longer resolves. Quick Start is the one section a cold-start agent reads alone, so the guidance now sits there: query the resources the session didn't touch, and when one is gone, say what the environment now holds rather than just swapping the id.
- **`done`: a reviewer's predicted RUNTIME failure gets the cheap version of that outcome before you act on it.** An agent reported an uncaught 500 with a valid chain end to end — real null-deref shape, real reachable route, real middleware bypass — resting on one unread premise (it assumed a resolver returns null for a non-numeric header; that resolver's `is_numeric` check makes it fall back instead). The endpoint answered 200. The existing guidance covered two agents disagreeing and confirming against the current file, neither of which applies when nothing contradicts a single confident finding.

## 1.138.1

- **ADR IDs move from chronological `D-N` to a topic slug (`D-gateway-fee-cap`), go-forward only.** The old convention required grepping the whole `decisions/` dir and taking max+1 before assigning a new ID, and nothing caught a duplicate — two features numbering independently from `D-1` meant a bare `D-3` silently resolved to whichever file you'd opened, a trap `doc-authority.md` already had to document as "the ID may not even be unique." A slug is self-describing at the point of citation and only collides if two decisions in the same domain genuinely share a topic, which is rarer and far more visible than two counters landing on the same integer. Existing `D-N` blocks are untouched — this is a going-forward convention, not a migration — so `task-summary/references/templates.md`'s header template, ID-assignment guidance, and `Supersedes` line now document slugs alongside numbers, and `read-summary/references/doc-authority.md`'s router-detection grep (`grep -c "^### D-\?[0-9]"`) was widened to `grep -cE "^### D([0-9]|-[a-z0-9])"` so it still catches slug-only decision files instead of reporting a false 0.

## 1.138.0

- **New skill: `self-organize-agent-memory`.** Run `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit` to pick this up. A session moved `code-simplifier`'s ~89-row inline "Don't Simplify" reference table into its own `agent-memory/` directory, then rewrote it into a general-principle-plus-instance format — done by dispatching `code-simplifier` onto its own file rather than a separate reviewer auditing it from outside, since the agent that accumulated the content is better positioned to judge its own shape than an external template applied cold. No existing skill covered this: `unhobble-instructions` audits an instruction file's own prose for overconstraint but never touches agent-memory or dispatches the subject agent onto itself, and `agent-setup` scaffolds new agents rather than reorganizing an existing one's standing content. The new skill is deliberately thin — no prescribed migration checklist or mandated memory format, since that was exactly what the session found needed to stay a judgment call — with one required outside check: an identifier sweep against the original content after the agent reports what it moved, since a rewrite that generalizes a specific fact ("an unhandled error" instead of "a raw 1062 MySQL duplicate-key error") reads as a clean summary and is a real loss the agent's own report won't surface.

## 1.137.20

- **A probe's success indicator can outshout a malformed value in the same output.** Run `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit` to pick this up. A session verified a new agency-creation endpoint end-to-end — HTTP 200, the row created, the quota meter moving, a browser pass — and its own probe printed `agency_identifier: "216"` where every real one reads `NOR216`. The generated prefix was empty because the creating path never set the column it derives from, and the value was on screen, unread, for the rest of the session; a `/done` reviewer found it afterwards in already-committed code. The existing rows nearest to this cover guards tested on well-formed input and derived values that *contradict* a report — neither fires when nothing contradicts anything and the status line is green. Added to `CLAUDE-verification-rules.md`: when a probe emits a value the system composed rather than echoed, compare its shape against an existing row before reading the status line.

## 1.137.19

- **`unhobble-instructions` warned against grading by markers, then let a session pick its target by marker count.** Run `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit` to pick this up. Asked to unhobble "the hobbled ones" across five candidates, a session measured `⚠️`/`**Tell:**`/`NEVER` density and chose `CLAUDE-verification-rules.md` — 58 Tells in 194 lines, one every 3.3 lines, which reads as overconstraint's signature. Testing them one at a time found **2 of 58 hollow**; the other 56 carried genuine self-diagnostics ("flipping the fix off doesn't change the result" is a question about your own last tool calls, not a restatement of its row). A pass that had trusted the ratio would have stripped 56 load-bearing facts and reported the file improved. The skill already forbade grading rows this way (1.137.18) but said nothing about the same count one step earlier, when it selects *which file* to open — and density there predicts a file's subject, not its health: a verification file is dense in `**Tell:**` because self-diagnostics are what it is for, while the badly shaped file can carry no markers at all. Counts now locate candidates only; the choice comes from reading each one's arrangement.

- **A whole-file pass could restructure a file and leave every pointer into it describing the old shape.** The same session added two section anchors to a companion, and the `📖` line in `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` kept advertising one — so a reader following it landed in a file whose structure had changed underneath the summary that sent them. Nothing 404s in this failure; the reader arrives somewhere plausible and concludes the fact isn't there. `update-claude-docs`'s `references/pointer-discipline.md` §2 already states the rule (a summary clause rots "including when *you* just changed it"), but that reference belongs to a different skill and `unhobble-instructions` mentioned inbound references nowhere in its 74 lines, despite restructuring being the operation that invalidates them. Added a reconciliation step between verification and self-review: grep the file's basename across its owning docs tree, check each hit against what the file now says, fix what drifted. Declared growth — no rule was retired for it, since the three marker-hunt statements now in the file name three different moments (grading, selecting, reporting) rather than repeating one.

## 1.137.18

- **`unhobble-instructions` let a session run a marker hunt and call it an unhobbling pass.** Run `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit` to pick this up. Asked to unhobble four skills, a session went straight to `grep '⚠️\|MANDATORY\|Tell'`, graded the hits, and started editing callouts — the user had to say "it means to read the skills according to judgement prose, not simply find those grep those thing, improving the file as whole." The skill's own text invited it: the framing test opened "For every rule, callout, or instruction in the target," which makes the pass an enumeration over units before anything has looked at the file as a document, and Process step 1's "read the target file whole" produced no artifact, so step 2's "go through it top to bottom" absorbed it. Nothing said the deliverable is a better-shaped file. Added a document-level read that runs first and has to be written down before any rule is opened — what the file makes a reader do in order, where each rule sits relative to the moment it applies, and what it says more than once — since a rule in three homes, a mandate above the branch that doesn't need it, and a cold path inlined in the hot path are all invisible one row at a time and all survive a keyword scan. The per-rule fact-vs-constraint test now runs second, against what the shape pass leaves. Report step leads with structural changes; a pass that only softened wording is still valid but has to say so, because that is also what a marker hunt produces. Description rewritten to say "whole-file improver, not a marker checker" with moving/merging/resequencing named as in scope.

- **Ran the corrected pass on the four dominant skills; the shape findings were ones the marker hunt had no way to reach.** `task-summary` carries nine rules averaging three homes each — in a file whose own headline rule is "One fact, one home" — plus a live contradiction between Validate's "No rows deleted" check and the entire Pruning section about deleting rows. `update-claude-docs` violates its own rules in its own prose: it mandates "rows ≤2 sentences" while running four to five, and forbids session storytelling while telling one. `done` shows the drift most clearly — its chain-break defenses are written as reasoning while its derivable material is written as dispatch tables, which is backwards from what you'd expect if rigidity tracked risk. `read-summary` came back clean, having been unhobbled the day before. Applied this pass: fixed a dangling "the MANDATORY callout above" reference left by 1.137.17 (the arrival-rate rule moves back near the top, now governing every mode, since Capture is the mode that adds lines), collapsed a 130-word paragraph defending a two-sentence rule, dropped three ❌/✅ rows that each duplicated something stated elsewhere, deleted a table-of-contents line pre-announcing two rules the reader reaches anyway, folded a thrice-stated two-item sequence into one sentence, and moved the repo-wide-command ban out of a table cell where its nested bold was malformed. Left alone deliberately: the 3→4 and 4→5 boundary cues, which read as redundant against the header callout but aren't — `one-turn-chain.md` argues specifically that a preamble mention doesn't reach the boundary, so the repetition is the mechanism.

- **`task-summary` gave nine rules an average of three homes each, in the file whose own headline rule is "One fact, one home."** Run `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit` to pick this up. Collapse-a-finished-stream was stated in the update prose, again as a section-table row, and a third time in a Pruning table; over-budget-then-condense had three homes; Next Steps grouping had four. A reader met most rules three times with no way to tell which statement was canonical — and at least one pair disagreed, since Validate's bare "No rows deleted" check read as forbidding exactly what the Pruning section mandated. The section table's cells had also stopped being cells: `## Next Steps` ran 175 words in one, `## Last Session` carried a three-step ordering. Moved the paragraph-length cells into the prose above them, cut the table back to one scannable line per section, deleted the Pruning table after folding in its three non-duplicate facts (Bugs Fixed past 10 rows keeps 5, Files stays a map, no `## Completed (date)` sections), and rewrote check 4 to say what it means — deliberate pruning is expected, what it catches is a row dropped while reflowing, which is `two-tier-condense.md`'s diff pass. 26,224 → 25,594 bytes with all sixteen inventoried facts verified present by grep rather than by re-reading, since a reflow is precisely the case where re-reading confirms intent instead of text.

## 1.137.17

- **`/done` diagnosed two identical chain-break boundaries and only ever fixed one of them.** Run `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit` to pick this up. The header callout names "the sub-skill RETURN boundaries (3→4, 4→5)" as where the chain breaks, and 1.137.15/16 both landed at 3→4 — leaving 4→5 with the diagnosis and no defense at the point of return. Step 4 ended on a flat description of what `task-summary` handles, so its closing summary sat in exactly the shape the file already calls dangerous, with Step 5's gates unable to help: they decide what Step 5 *does* once entered, never that it was entered at all. Added the mirrored line at the end of Step 4. Also relocated `update-claude-docs`'s `⚠️ MANDATORY` on Create/Rewrite from above the mode table to inside the Create/Rewrite section (+10 bytes, pure move) — Capture is the mode `/done` invokes, and it was reading a mandate for a mode it wasn't running. Two pointer fixes in `task-summary`: `templates.md` is now flagged as a read-while-writing file rather than advertised by its table of contents (a pointer that summarizes its target well enough makes opening it feel redundant), and the Last Session row now states fold → delete → write in execution order instead of burying "fold load-bearing facts first" as a subordinate clause after the "delete" imperative.

## 1.137.16

- **`/done` skipped Step 4 again — no compaction this time, just a summary that looked like a finished turn.** Run `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit` to pick this up. 1.137.15 covered the involuntary version of this (a compaction severing the chain); this is the voluntary one the original guards were written for, failing anyway. Observed: Step 3 (`update-claude-docs`) ran to completion, its closing summary was reported as the turn's output, and `task-summary` never ran until the user asked "u didnt run task-summary ?". Both existing warnings sit ABOVE the steps — a header callout and a two-checkbox block — so a reader passes them before doing any work and meets neither at the moment the summary is actually sitting there looking complete. Added one line at the END of Step 3, where the failure happens: its return is the riskiest moment in the workflow, and the next thing after that summary is the `task-summary` call, not a reply describing what Step 3 wrote. Dropped the now-redundant `⚠️` from the checkbox block rather than letting a third marker accumulate on one topic. Deliberately not mirrored to the 4→5 boundary — Step 5 opens with two explicit gates that already force the decision.

## 1.137.15

- **`/done` and `/ship` guarded against stopping the chain early, but only against stopping it *voluntarily* — a context compaction between two steps severs it just as effectively and slipped past every existing guard.** Run `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit` to pick this up. Observed on a real run: `/done` completed Step 3 (`update-claude-docs`), the conversation compacted, and Step 4 (`task-summary`) never ran — leaving a 33-file working tree with no task-doc update and a stale "this is blocked" claim still live in two task docs. The compaction summary *did* list Step 4 under Pending Tasks and it was still walked past, so the failure wasn't the summary omitting it. `_shared/references/one-turn-chain.md` framed the whole trap as "ending the turn with steps outstanding" — a stop the model elects — and its three tells all assume the turn is still running; nothing in the plugin mentioned compaction at all (measured: zero hits for `compact`/`resumed session` across `skills/`). The resumed turn also re-enters holding a sub-skill's terminal summary, the exact artifact that file already names as manufacturing a false stopping point, minus any memory of the parent workflow to contradict it. Added a section on the involuntary case: on re-entry, which steps ran is answered from whether each step's artifact changed on disk, not from the summary's account of itself — `/done`'s Output table already states that standard. Routed to the shared reference rather than the two callers, so `/ship` inherits it without an edit (its `SKILL.md` was carrying another session's uncommitted work and was left untouched).

## 1.137.14

- **`/done`'s "the agent that ran code wins" tie-breaker didn't cover the case where BOTH sides ran something.** Run `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit` to pick this up. If you've ever settled a disagreement between two agents (or an agent and yourself) by re-running the deciding case and trusting whichever side executed code, and that re-run happened in the same process as the thing being measured (e.g. reading a config value right after a request that itself mutates it), the result may have been measuring its own side effect rather than the real system — worth a second look if the call mattered. New reference: `skills/_shared/references/probe-isolation.md`.

## 1.137.13

- **`/done`'s recovery instruction for a dead agent spawn now distinguishes two different failures it used to treat the same.** If an agent dies because its own definition is broken, re-running it as `general-purpose` on the same model tier is correct. If it dies with `effort 'max' not supported when thinking is disabled` (an Opus-pinned role hitting a spawn/routing fault), matching the same tier reproduces the identical error — the fix is switching tiers (Sonnet), not just the wrapper. No action needed; this only affects `/done` runs that hit a dead-agent retry.
- **Two small doc-accuracy fixes, also no action needed**: two unrelated decisions in the internal maintenance log had independently claimed the same ID and got renumbered apart; and a router-detection check in `read-summary` that could silently misread a real decisions file as empty now matches this plugin's own ID format correctly.

## 1.137.12

- **`unhobble-instructions`'s row-decoration check existed only in prose, not in the numbered Process steps that actually drive execution — so running the skill on itself still mis-stripped a genuine `**Tell:**` alongside hollow ones.** Applied the skill to a global CLAUDE.md's Agent Bootstrap section and its companion reference table this session; a user question ("seems like it's using trip wire?") surfaced that roughly a dozen decorative `⚠️`/`**Tell:**` markers were pure restatement of their own row's condition and got stripped — but one, on a row about stale environment preconditions, genuinely named a checkable self-diagnostic question the row's plain sentence didn't state, and it was removed too. Traced to the skill's own step 2 ("go through it against the one test") and step 5 ("verify against the fact list") never referencing the decoration-vs-content distinction that lived only in the file's target-type prose section — a reader executing the numbered Process, including the skill executing its own logic, never triggers a check that exists elsewhere in the same file but isn't wired into the linear path. Fixed: restored the dropped Tell, and folded the decoration check into steps 2 (apply the same fact-vs-constraint test to a row's marker, separately from its content), 3 (track which rows carry a marker worth a second look, mirroring the existing fact list), and 5 (re-verify each against the specific test — "the sentence explains the mechanism" is not the same finding as "the marker is redundant").
