- **A second, distinct fix landed the same session: an agent's cited facts can each independently verify while the claimed scope/baseline is still wrong.** A `/done` simplifier agent reported a companion reference table's edit as adding "10 new rows," and cross-checked every cited row against the file's real content, table heading, and a sibling pointer's summary — all matched. But the actual session diff (`git diff HEAD`) was pure removal, zero additions; the agent had silently diffed against the wrong baseline (a prior commit's state, not the session's actual `HEAD` diff) and attributed pre-existing content to this session's edit. Every individual citation was accurate; the attribution wrapping them wasn't. Added a row to the agent-bootstrap companion table naming this as a distinct failure shape from forged provenance (which is deliberate) — this is honest, internally-consistent confabulation about scope, caught only by checking the agent's stated baseline against `git diff HEAD` directly rather than spot-verifying its citations.
- **`done`'s multi-repo agent-count rule assumed a project's sub-repo list was the exhaustive way to find every git tree in scope — it doesn't cover a global dotfiles checkout or a separately-cloned plugin repo, both of which sit entirely outside the project directory.** A session editing `~/.claude` and `~/.claude/plugins/syafiqkit` alongside a project repo had to manually discover the extra repos rather than have `/done`'s partition logic surface them, since no `Glob`/directory walk from the project root reaches either location. Added the fact to the existing multi-repo row. Caught scope-creep in the first draft of this same addition — it stated the fact correctly but tacked on a prescriptive "the only reliable way is X, not Y" procedural clause the file's other genuine facts don't carry; trimmed to the bare structural fact once flagged.

## 1.137.11

- **`unhobble-instructions` step 5 offered "grep or re-read" as equivalent ways to verify a fact survived, and the two aren't.** Same sentence then warns against concluding the file "feels complete" — which is what re-reading produces, so the step contradicted itself and an agent picking the permitted option complied while producing an unfounded claim. Observed on a real run against a project CLAUDE.md: the agent deduplicated a section, reported "no facts were lost — verified by re-reading the surviving section against the deleted one," and was right by luck; a grep across the twelve deleted markers (run separately, not by the agent) is what actually established it. Re-reading confirms whatever the reader is already holding in mind, which right after a rewrite is their own intent rather than the text. Step 5 now prescribes grep alone and says why the re-read can't support the claim. Checked the siblings rather than assuming a shared gap — `condense-task-doc` and `condense-claude-md` already prescribe `grep`/`grep -c` with no re-read option, so this was one skill out of step, not a plugin-wide pattern needing a `_shared/` reference.

## 1.137.10

- **`update-claude-docs` still mandated the trip-wire shape the campaign is removing everywhere else.** Its Step 3 entry-shape rule told the writer to end every judgement entry with a `**Tell:**` naming the concrete signal — and demonstrated the form by ending in one itself. Meanwhile `_shared/references/writing-style.md`, which this skill points at, had gained the opposite rule ("state the mechanism, not the trip-wire"), so a session following the skill body produced entries a later unhobble pass had to undo. Caught from the output side: four CLAUDE.md rows written through this skill in one session each came out with a `**Tell:**` pinned to that session's specific incident ("an agent that read the body disagrees") rather than the recurring signal, and needed rewriting immediately after. The step now says to state the mechanism and defers to the base rules on when a trailing tell earns its place; its own closing sentence was converted from a tell to plain prose. Audited the other doc/instruction writers rather than assuming the same gap: `task-summary` was already clean (⚠️-density rule, a no-narrative sentence-style rule, and an explicit mechanism-not-trip-wire route for gotcha rows), and `agent-setup`/`skill-creator`/`update-plugin` each already carry their own statement of the unhobbled shape — this was the one surviving contradiction, not a plugin-wide gap.

## 1.137.9

- **`agent-setup`'s template-drift verification was itself unverified when delegated.** A real session ran the full 8-agent drift check across three parallel background agents, got three clean "no drift found" reports, and repeated that verdict to the user as fact. Asked "u sure?", it turned out two agents (`Explore`/`Plan`) really were missing a trailing anti-loop-guard row each — the exact sub-heading drift shape the checklist already names — and the "clean" verdict on them had never been independently re-read, only relayed. The checklist item already said to run the check directly rather than delegate it; what it didn't say is that a *clean* delegated result is the one most likely to go unchecked, since nothing about it looks worth a second look. Added that a delegated pass is a first draft to spot-check, not a verdict, with a concrete minimum (personally re-open at least the thinnest report or shortest generated file before repeating its conclusion).
- **Version-bump races between parallel agents patching the plugin concurrently had no documented protocol.** The same session's follow-up `unhobble-instructions` run fanned out to 4 background agents, each independently reading and bumping `plugin.json`/`marketplace.json` — three separate version collisions happened over the course of the run, each caught only because the individual agent happened to re-check before finalizing. `CLAUDE.md`'s Version Bumping convention covered a prior-session's uncommitted bump but not same-window concurrent bumps from sibling agents, and said nothing about who should run `claude plugin update` when several agents are mid-batch. Added: re-read the version immediately before writing a bump rather than trusting an earlier read, and treat `claude plugin update` as the coordinating session's job once the whole batch lands, not each agent's.

## 1.137.8

- **Ran `unhobble-instructions` against `agent-setup`'s `product-reviewer.template.md` and `claude-md-pruner.template.md`.** `product-reviewer`'s opening "Zoom OUT" paragraph restated its own point twice — a bolded imperative callout followed by a second bolded sentence making the same claim — rewritten as two flowing paragraphs stating the reasoning once; the generated Autorentic copy had already independently drifted toward the softer phrasing, which was itself the signal the rigid template version wasn't holding up. Also trimmed the generated copy's Process step 8, which repeated the zoom-out point a second time with its own "do NOT skip" trip-wire. `claude-md-pruner`'s Gotcha Condensation section had already been converted to flowing prose in the template by an earlier pass, but the generated Autorentic copy still carried the older bulleted "Delete gotcha rows ONLY if:" list — brought the generated copy in line with the template (pure drift-sync, not a new judgement call). `browser-verifier.template.md` was reviewed in full and left unchanged — every rule in it is either one of its three protected mandates (Prime Rule table, never-fabricate-user-approval, USER-TRIGGERED-ONLY dispatch language) or a genuine environment fact with a measured, checkable consequence (a real prod-S3 orphaned-file incident, a real Dourr logout race condition, the `matchMedia` hard gate); a genuine "nothing to unhobble" result, not a skipped file. Applied the product-reviewer/claude-md-pruner changes identically to their generated `.claude/agents/*.md` copies in Autorentic for template/generated parity.

## 1.137.7

- **Ran `unhobble-instructions` against `agent-setup`'s `code-reviewer.template.md`/`code-simplifier.template.md`.** Both were largely already lean — most of their bulk is on-demand lookup tables (High-Frequency Mistakes, Known False Positives, Don't Simplify) that this pass leaves in table form by design, and the surrounding prose (Bootstrap, Process, Review Categories) is mostly concrete non-derivable facts (real tool names, real gotchas with named consequences), not rigid scaffolding. Found two genuine restatement spots: the Confidence Calibration threshold was stated plainly once, then repeated with "non-negotiable" framing in the Constraints table/bullet — trimmed to a cross-reference. And code-simplifier's Component-vs-Utility table was followed by two ❌/✅ lines echoing the same table plus a third line restating the Rule of Three already stated one paragraph up — the generated Autorentic copy had already independently dropped both restatements, so the template just needed to catch up to what its own generated output had converged on. Also softened a project-specific "500+ lines is itself a finding" mechanical gate on the *generated* `code-simplifier.md` (project-local addition, not present in the template) into judgement prose that keeps the same reasoning and the 500-line anchor number without the hard trigger-on-any-500-line-file framing — flagged as a real trade-off, not a pure improvement: the mechanical version fires reliably regardless of whether a given file's size actually feels wrong, judgement might not. Applied identically to the generated `.claude/agents/code-reviewer.md`/`code-simplifier.md` copies in Autorentic for template/generated parity, except the 500-line callout which only exists generated-side.

## 1.137.6

- **Ran `unhobble-instructions` against `agent-setup`'s `task-builder.template.md`.** The Scope Rules section's core prohibitions (file-partition-only scope, the sub-agent scope-passing requirement, and the `NEVER run a REPO-WIDE git write` callout naming the `git checkout master -- .` incident) are the genuine mandate — task-builder deliberately omits `tools:` to get the full toolset including `Agent`, so that prose is the *only* thing keeping parallel builders from clobbering each other or the user's uncommitted work; left all of it untouched at full force. Trimmed three doubled-restatement spots instead: the clobber-risk callout repeated the adjacent table row's "stop and report" almost verbatim; the inline-comment rule stacked "1 line" / "no multi-line budget" / "never judge by ratio" as three separate defensive phrasings of one cap; and the closing Output Format callout restated a constraint the `**Not done**` field already structurally enforces. Folded each into a single prose statement of the reasoning. Applied identically to the generated `.claude/agents/task-builder.md` copy in Autorentic for template/generated parity.

## 1.137.5

- **Ran `unhobble-instructions` against `agent-setup`'s `Explore.template.md`/`Plan.template.md`.** Both carried a duplicated-emphasis pattern in the "always run `/read-summary` discovery" callouts: a trailing sentence ("There is no prompt shape that exempts this step" / "Treat 'no task doc found' as a checked box, not an assumption") that restated the paragraph's own preceding sentence rather than adding new reasoning. Grepped `tasks/plugin-maintenance/agent-architecture/decisions/injection-and-delegation.md` before touching the mandate itself — it documents a real session that skipped discovery on a fully-scoped OAuth prompt, treating detail as proof a task doc wasn't needed — so the absolutism ("MANDATORY, no exceptions", prompt specificity is not an exemption) is the surviving fix to a recurred failure, not unexamined scaffolding, and stayed in full force. Trimmed only the redundant trailing sentence and folded the incident into the "detailed prompt" paragraph's own reasoning so the fact travels with the rule instead of sitting as an unexplained absolute. Applied identically to the generated `.claude/agents/Explore.md`/`Plan.md` copies in Autorentic for template/generated parity. Left the two anti-loop guard rows ("You ARE the search/design step — never hand it back") untouched in both — those encode a real recurring failure mode (the agent recommending its own re-dispatch instead of doing its job), not overconstraint.

## 1.137.3

- **`agent-setup`'s Step 5 memory-readback check only ever grepped generated `.claude/agents/*.md` copies, never the `templates/*.template.md` they're generated from.** Found via the prior session's unhobble pass on `claude-md-pruner.template.md`: its fact-survival check went looking for the `⚠️ Read your own memory first` line and found the *template* missing it, even though every consuming project's generated copy already had it (a prior hand-fix had survived regeneration by coincidence, not because the check caught the source). Traced to the template's artifact-branch restructure (`3179e68`) landing before the memory-readback requirement was added to the checklist — the rollout that added the line to every other template missed this one mid-restructure, and nothing since has re-checked template sources against their own rule. Step 5's checklist item now says to run the same grep against `templates/*.template.md` directly, not just its output.

## 1.137.2

- **`agent-setup`'s `claude-md-pruner.template.md` carried overconstraint an unhobble pass would flag in any other skill.** Ran `unhobble-instructions` against it directly: Philosophy's "not two lanes of one job" meta-taxonomy, three separate `⚠️`+bold restatements of "this agent's lane vs. the condense skills," a bolded trip-wire for a point its own preceding sentence already made (mixed-batch classification), and a 4-bullet "delete gotcha rows ONLY if" checklist standing in for one judgement call ("is this row provably closed"). Rewrote all of it as prose stating the reasoning once; left both NEVER-remove lists, the 2a/2b classification tables, and the frontmatter untouched — those are the genuine facts/lookup-tables/mandate-boundary this pass exists to protect, not scaffolding.
- **Same file was also missing the `⚠️ Read your own memory first` Bootstrap line** that `agent-setup`'s own Step 5 checklist requires on every `memory: project` agent — a pre-existing template gap, found via the unhobble pass's fact-survival check (looking for the line in the rewrite and finding it absent from the *original* too), not introduced by it. Added.

## 1.137.1

- **`condense-task-doc` cleared its own line/byte target while individual sentences stayed bloated.** A real run on Dourr's `blog-automation/current.md` did a full row-existence pass, landed a 21% byte cut, and reported done — the user came back with "each line also bloated," and several Task Status/Bugs Fixed rows were still 500-1000+ character run-ons carrying full measurement narratives inline. Step 9's tripwire is aggregate (bytes-per-line, total size), which a doc can clear while only its worst few lines carry the bloat — most lines are short, so the average hides it. Added step 11: `awk '{print length, NR}' <file> | sort -rn | head -15` after row-existence, and tighten those specifically, mirroring the row-existence tripwire's "zero deletions means the step was skipped" logic at sentence granularity instead of row granularity.

## 1.137.0

- **`read-summary`'s mirror trap only fired on `AskUserQuestion`, so prose options walked straight past it.** The rule said presenting a settled fork "via `AskUserQuestion`" reads as not having read the doc — but laying out options in prose re-opens the fork just as surely, and neither feels like asking. It also fires hardest on a question phrased as *behaviour* ("why did it still charge me?"), which is answerable from the ADR that chose to charge but doesn't read like a decision question, so the reflex is to go reason about the code. Rewrote the trap to cover both shapes with a `**Tell:**` at the point options get enumerated. `references/doc-authority.md`.
- **A `decisions/` file can be a pure router holding zero ADRs, and appearance doesn't discriminate.** Read Order step 2 already warned routers nest, but its tell — "only a Quick Start and a `## Sub-Files` table" — missed an 11,826-byte router carrying prose and a full-looking routing table with no `### D-` blocks in it. Replaced the appearance test with a mechanical one: `grep -c "^### D-"` returning 0 means you're on a router whatever it looks like. Verified against both cases (0 on the router, 5 on a real ADR file).
- **A bare `D-N` isn't unique once a doc set is split by feature** — each feature renumbers from D-1, so `D-3` named three different decisions across `subscription/{core,enterprise-org,discounts}`. Extended the existing shorthand-ID rule rather than adding a second one.
- **Evidence**: a session offered three pricing options on an org-downgrade question that committed ADR D-20 had already settled ("the PM pays the pro-rated first month at the gateway first"). The user's reply was "ithought we already have decision for this ?". The doc was loaded; the router in between was read and its summary mistaken for the decision.
- **Left unpatched, deliberately**: `read-summary/SKILL.md` carries another session's uncommitted edit, so the finding was routed to the clean `references/` file that already owned the mirror trap. Its line-44 pointer still describes this file as relevant "when a question you're about to ask cites a doc's internal ID" — now stale, since the failure involved neither a question nor an ID. One-line fix for whoever owns that file next.

- **New skill: `skill-creator`.** Creates a skill end to end — judges whether the thing should be a skill at all (a project fact belongs in CLAUDE.md; an always-on behavior isn't a skill; an adjacent existing skill should be extended, since two skills covering one area both trigger weakly), picks the location, drafts the SKILL.md, registers it in the `CLAUDE.md`/`README.md` tables, and pressure-tests the trigger against phrasings the user would really reach for. `update-plugin` gained a matching exclusion — it patches skills that exist, this one creates them.
- **Frontmatter descriptions were carrying enforcement, not just trigger vocabulary.** `update-plugin` (1379 → 769 chars, 3 `Fire it` imperatives + 2 `Do NOT use` clauses removed) and `unhobble-instructions` (2148 → 1213, 3 `Do NOT use` removed) both restated in their descriptions what their bodies already explain. Every trigger signal was verified surviving — 11 and 14 respectively — so what went was scaffolding, not routing. The authoring rule now covers descriptions explicitly: a boundary belongs there as the one clause that routes a near-miss, with the reasoning in the body. Stacked `Do NOT use` clauses are the tell, each one a real routing fix that stopped earning its length once the body explained it.
- **This gap was found by the user, twice, after the pass reported clean.** The first check looked for the *vocabulary* of overconstraint (`NEVER`, `MUST`, `⚠️`) and found none, while the *shape* survived — a prescribed ask-the-user procedure in `skill-creator`, then a third negation stacked onto `update-plugin`'s description in the same session that added the rule against exactly that. The rule hadn't fired because it was written about body rules and the offending text was frontmatter.
- **Unhobbling became an authoring default rather than only a separate pass.** `update-plugin`'s Step 3 "Workflow rule" bullet and `agent-setup`'s Step 4 now say to write new rules in the shape `unhobble-instructions` audits for — reasoning stated once, markers reserved for costs that are genuinely silent or irreversible. The standalone skill stays for auditing existing files, including non-plugin CLAUDE.md and agent files that no authoring skill reaches. User's framing: "the skills also should follow unhobble by default, so no need to run like this."
- **Swept the four skills holding the most constraint markers.** Corpus-wide `⚠️` 54 → 39, `**Tell:**` 11 → 7, `MANDATORY` unchanged at 5. Per-file: `agent-setup` 13 → 6, `update-claude-docs` 11 → 8, `condense-claude-md` 6 → 0, `done` unchanged.
- **`done/SKILL.md` was deliberately left alone** — the pass returned a negative finding rather than a manufactured diff. It sits at its documented post-condense steady state (247 lines, 134.2 B/L), and its markers trace to decisions that already rejected loosening them (D50 rejected "remove 80% of rules, let judgement handle it"; D46 rejected giving Gate B a significance floor).
- **Closed the D64 gap in `unhobble-instructions` itself.** Its verification step checked that facts *survived* a rewrite but never that an absolute rule's *strength* survived — which is how a prior run softened a rule D57 had deliberately hardened, caught downstream by a product reviewer rather than by the pass. Step 3 now marks absolutist rules while listing facts; step 5 checks they still bind, since a softened rule passes a presence check while reopening the failure the absolutism closed.
- **Five rules were kept hard after grepping their incident history**, which is that check working rather than a failure to unhobble: `Explore`/`Plan`'s unconditional `/read-summary` (a live test showed the gated version missing a real documented bug), the sub-heading/table-row drift check, both pruner migration items, and the D46 budget gate.
- **No version drift**: the working copies read 1.136.29 vs 1.136.33, but committed-to-committed both sat at 1.136.27 — the gap was another session's uncommitted bumps, which is the exact case the Version Bumping convention says to check `git show HEAD:` for before calling either file stale.

## 1.136.33

- **`read-summary`'s remediation-fork rule told you to offer options without saying to check they're buildable.** The exit-gate rule correctly routes "which findings to act on" through `AskUserQuestion` as separate forks, but an option is a commitment, and nothing in the rule closed the gap between offering one and being able to perform it. Added the check at the point the options are drafted, framed as the consequence — walking back an answer the user already gave — rather than as another prohibition.
- **Evidence**: a session offered "delete the comment and repost a rewrite" as the recommended option for fixing a ClickUp card written for the wrong audience. The user picked it; the MCP server turned out to expose `create`/`get`/`get_threaded` for comments and no delete or update, so the chosen option was unbuildable and the fallback (edit the description, reply in-thread) had to be improvised after the fact. One `ToolSearch` before drafting the question would have settled it.
- **Not routed to `_shared/references/`** despite touching a convention several skills use: `condense-task-doc` and `merge-task-docs` prescribe `AskUserQuestion` for *restructure* forks where the options are always performable by the skill itself, so the failure mode doesn't reach them. The rule belongs where remediation of an external system is being offered.

## 1.136.32

- **The skills that WRITE task-doc gotcha rows had no idea the fact-vs-trip-wire test applied to them.** 1.136.30 settled that a `| Symptom | Rule |` table inside a `current.md` is in scope for unhobbling, but that ruling lived only in `unhobble-instructions` — the skills that author those rows (`task-summary`, `condense-task-doc`) never mentioned trip-wires, imperatives, or overconstraint at all. The rule was reachable only by someone already invoking the audit skill, never by the write that creates the problem.
- **Routed to `_shared/references/writing-style.md` rather than either caller**, since both already cite it as their base rules — `condense-task-doc` inherits the new row with no edit of its own. The rule states the mechanism-over-trip-wire preference and names why the failure is uneven: a `| Symptom | Rule |` table resists it by construction (the symptom column already occupies the slot a `**Tell:**` would fill), while prose rows and free-form callouts are where it creeps back.
- **`task-summary`'s `## Critical Gotchas` row now carries the ADR boundary explicitly** — those rows instruct a reader, the MADR blocks below them record a decision, and the rule reaches the first and not the second. Without that line the shared rule is ambiguous at exactly the file where both section types coexist.
- **Evidence from the session that prompted it**: the same sitting wrote three `**Tell:**` trip-wires into two CLAUDE.md files (a third of each file's total in one go, all three later removed as restatements of their own opening clauses) while the five gotcha rows written into a `current.md` came out clean. Same author, same hour, opposite outcomes — which is what identified the table shape as the protective factor rather than the author's care.

## 1.136.31

- **`unhobble-instructions` carried the exact defect it names — two callouts making the same point, one appended below the other as a repair seam.** 1.136.30 fixed the decision-log scope contradiction by adding a correcting paragraph *below* the wrong one rather than fixing it in place, leaving the job-not-path discriminator stated twice in the body (plus once in `description:`) and a "the paragraph above" seam that reads as repair history rather than instruction. Merged into one paragraph: the exclusion, its job-not-path test, and the clarifying-question guidance now sit together, and the clarifying question covers both which-section and whether-this-pass-at-all. 57 → 55 lines.
- **`/done`'s turn-boundary rule was stated three times** (header, after Step 3, after Step 4) — the file's own named tell for overconstraint. The header already declared it fires at the 3→4→5 seams, making the two site-copies restatements at the exact spots the header pointed to. Collapsed to one statement that names both sub-skills and both seams explicitly, so removing the copies didn't weaken it — `update-claude-docs` and `task-summary` each close with a summary of their own writes, and that summary is what makes the boundary feel terminal. 251 → 247 lines; `⚠️` density 10 → 8.
- **Bounded by three prior decisions, deliberately left alone**: D64 (this skill previously softened an absolutist rule that D57 had hardened — its Process step 2 grep-before-softening and step 5 verify-against-fact-list are the surviving fix and were not touched), D46 (Gate B fires on clean sessions *by design*; a significance floor was an explicitly rejected alternative), and D50's rejection of "remove 80%+ of the rules, let the model use judgement" wholesale — measured as a regression on this plugin specifically, because it is an incident-driven accumulator where the lever is arrival rate, not stock. Both passes were therefore duplicate-removal, not rule-loosening: every mechanically-checkable fact (`git status --short` vs `git diff --name-only`, the agent/fallback `subagent_type` table, the ~90 B/L gate, the emission-shape rule, browser-verifier's opt-in) survives at full strength, verified by grep against a pre-written fact list.

## 1.136.30

- **`unhobble-instructions` contradicted itself on whether a symptom table inside a `tasks/**/current.md` is in scope, and the contradiction only surfaces on the file where it matters.** Its fifth target type explicitly lists "a task doc's symptom index" as in scope, while the paragraph directly below it — and the `description:` frontmatter — excluded `tasks/**/current.md` wholesale by path. A `current.md` that carries an ADR set *and* a `| Symptom | Rule |` gotcha table satisfies both readings at once, so the skill gave opposite answers about the same file depending on which paragraph you reached first. Surfaced this session: a user asked to unhobble the Critical Gotchas table in a subscription `current.md`, which is squarely the fifth type (it instructs a reader who arrived holding a symptom) living inside a path the exclusion names.
- **Fixed by making the discriminator the section's JOB rather than the file's PATH** — ADR blocks record decisions and stay out of scope; a symptom-indexed table in the same file instructs a reader and is fair game. Both the body paragraph and the `description:` now say so, and a request naming the file without naming a section is called out as worth one clarifying question rather than a guess. The exclusion itself is unchanged in strength; only its test moved.
- **`/done`'s Gate B treated a dirty `git status` in the plugin checkout as sufficient evidence the session hand-edited a skill file.** That checkout is shared by every project, so it routinely carries another session's uncommitted work — and a file merely *read* this session is indistinguishable from one written. Measured this session: Gate B listed five files, all of which predated the session (03:04–12:43 against a ~13:00 start) and one of which had only been read. Gate B now names mtime-vs-session-start as the settling step, with the BSD `stat -f '%Sm' -t '%m-%d %H:%M'` spelling inline, since the GNU `ls --time-style=…` form prints nothing on macOS and reads as "no output, therefore nothing to check."
- **Declared growth, no retirement available**: both files sit above the ~90 B/L density threshold and both edits netted positive lines. Each is a *replace* in kind (correcting a rule that was actively wrong, not adding a new one), so no superseded rule existed to delete. `unhobble-instructions`' 317-word `description:` does restate body scope and is the obvious retirement candidate, but that shape is common across the plugin's skills and reworking it is out of scope for a defect fix.

## 1.136.28

- **`condense-claude-md`, its shared `declared-budget.md`, and `update-claude-docs/references/structure.md` all treated a `.claude-companions/*.md` file as a destination content gets moved TO, never as a target that itself needs the condense process run against it.** Surfaced this session: an `unhobble-instructions` pass correctly swept all seven companion files under a project's `.claude-companions/`, but a later bare `/condense-claude-md` on the same project explicitly excluded them, reasoning "already looked at these under the unhobble lens" — conflating a different skill's check (rigid-mandate-vs-judgement) with this skill's own (atomic-file gate, longest-row scan, Restructuring #4). The user caught it: "claude-companion supposed should be seen as claude.md too." Re-running the condense process against the companions on the project where this was found surfaced two real defects the exclusion had hidden — a malformed 3-cell row inside a 2-column table, and a 10-fact Cloudways paragraph that should have been a lookup table like every sibling section in the same file. (Neither example lives in this plugin's own repo, which has no `.claude-companions/` directory — this fix is to the skill logic, verified against that other project's files, not against anything tracked here.)
- **Fixed at the shared root**: `declared-budget.md` (referenced by all three size-judging consumers — `update-claude-docs`, `condense-claude-md`, `claude-md-pruner`) now states explicitly that a companion is a CLAUDE.md for every rule on the page, with the concrete `Glob .claude-companions/**/*.md` action. `condense-claude-md/SKILL.md` cross-references it at the point scope gets decided (its opening "source of truth" paragraph). `update-claude-docs/references/structure.md` gets a companion note right after its auto-load hierarchy table — the note explains WHY a companion looks excluded (it genuinely never auto-loads, unlike every row in that table) before correcting the inference that "not in the hierarchy" means "not in scope for capture/routing/density."
- **`claude-md-pruner`'s own template already had this right** (`agent-setup/templates/claude-md-pruner.template.md` line 54 already classifies a `.claude-companions/` file as counting as CLAUDE.md) — the gap was specific to the two skills that decide condense SCOPE, not the one that decides pruning ELIGIBILITY, so the pruner agent needed no change.
- **No consumer action beyond the usual `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit`.** Anyone who ran a bare `condense-claude-md` against a project with pre-existing companion files before this fix likely reviewed the same subset I did — root/layer files only — and should re-run it including `.claude-companions/**` if those files haven't been checked under this skill's own process since.

## 1.136.27

- **Every `_shared/references/<topic>.md` pointer across the plugin was a bare, non-literal path — one level short of resolving from wherever it was cited.** `skills/_shared/references/` sits one level under `skills/`, so a citation written inside `skills/<name>/SKILL.md` (depth 1) needed `../_shared/references/...` to actually resolve, and one inside `skills/<name>/references/*.md` or `templates/*.md` (depth 2) needed `../../_shared/references/...`. Every citing file instead wrote the bare form, which silently 404s on a literal `cat`/`Read` — it only ever "worked" because every prior reader recovered by locating the target file some other way (grep, memory) rather than treating the miss as a defect. Surfaced this session when `condense-claude-md`'s own referenced files (`two-tier-condense.md`, `declared-budget.md`, `strip-tool-output-tags.md`, `writing-style.md`) came back not-found via a literal read.
- **Fixed all 17 affected files** (11 `SKILL.md`s at depth 1, 6 `references/`/`templates/` files at depth 2) plus one bare instance inside `CLAUDE.md` itself (depth 0, needed the `skills/`-prefixed full form to match its own sibling row). 47 insertions / 47 deletions — pure path-string substitution, verified independently afterward by resolving every citation against its actual filesystem target (all now resolve; none over- or under-prefixed).
- **Deliberately left untouched**: `CHANGELOG.md` and the `tasks/plugin-maintenance/**/decisions/*.md` / `current.md` files, plus generic mentions of the `_shared/references/` convention in this plugin's own `.claude/agents/*.md` checklists (`code-reviewer`, `code-simplifier`, `Plan`, `product-reviewer`). All of these are historical narration or conceptual references to the convention, not live navigable pointers — editing them would rewrite history rather than fix a broken path.
- **Added the depth rule to `CLAUDE.md`'s Conventions table**, next to the row that establishes the `_shared/references/` extraction convention, so the next new pointer gets written at the correct depth instead of reintroducing the bare form.
- **No consumer action beyond the usual `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit`.** Nothing about any skill's behavior changed — every affected pointer already resolved in practice because readers worked around the broken path by searching instead of reading it literally. This closes that gap so a literal read/cat works too.

## 1.136.26

- **Ran `unhobble-instructions` against `tackle` and `pull-db`.** `tackle/SKILL.md` needed no changes — at 8 lines with a single instruction, there was no accumulated scaffolding to remove. `pull-db/SKILL.md` was the real target: rewrote its five-step workflow to fold each rigid callout ("Always...", "Never...") into judgement prose stating the underlying reason (why `remote` output corrupts binary pipes, why bcrypt breaks raw-SQL password resets, why FK 6125 happens on MariaDB→MySQL imports), and deleted the closing Gotchas table outright — every row in it restated a step already covered above verbatim, so it was pure duplication rather than a genuine symptom-index a reader would jump to independently.
- **No incident history found for `pull-db` in `tasks/`** (only `plugin-maintenance` exists as a domain), so none of its rules were protected by a documented "judgement was tried here and failed" case — every callout in the file was open to the pass's default lean toward loosening, unlike the `commit/SKILL.md` case in 1.136.25 where a Rejected-alternatives entry overrode that default.
- **No consumer action beyond the usual `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit`.** Nothing about `pull-db`'s actual procedure changed — same commands, same order, same recovery steps for FK errors and chroot-jail transfer failures — only how the reasoning behind them is stated, so no prior run of the skill is invalidated.

## 1.136.25

- **`unhobble-instructions`'s 2nd real run (`task-summary`, `condense-task-doc`, `merge-task-docs`, `sweep-doc-overlaps`) found a genuine correctness regression on a sibling file the 1st run had already touched — `commit/SKILL.md`'s task-doc staleness gate was softened from "the grep hitting is the ENTIRE trigger, no judgment permitted" to "a real hit means...", which invites exactly the case-by-case judgment call D37/D57 (`tasks/plugin-maintenance/doc-condensation/decisions/structural-splits.md`) deliberately closed after it caused an undocumented deviation (issue #14).** Caught independently by two `/done` review agents (code-reviewer, product-reviewer), the second of which traced it to D57's own Rejected-alternatives entry naming this precise softening as the thing not to do. Fixed by restoring the absolute no-judgment trigger as flowing judgement prose (not reverted to the original's bolded `⚠️ MANDATORY` formatting) — the constraint's strength was the load-bearing part, not its shape.
- **`unhobble-instructions/SKILL.md` gained the fix that prevents a 3rd recurrence**: Process step 2 now instructs grepping `decisions/*.md` for an absolutist-looking rule's keywords before softening it — a Rejected-alternatives entry matching the exact softening about to happen means the rigidity is a deliberate, tested fix, not unexamined scaffolding, and outranks the skill's own default lean toward loosening rigid-reading rules.
- **Same review pass found and fixed two smaller instances of the identical root cause (a rewrite trusting a rule's old position/shape instead of re-deriving what needed to survive there) in `merge-task-docs/SKILL.md`**: a "don't proceed past an `AskUserQuestion` fork without an answer, even if the recommendation seems obviously right" rule had been silently dropped, overwritten by an unrelated sentence occupying the same paragraph position — restored as its own sentence. Separately, the code-simplifier pass caught that the file's closing `❌ Never / ✅ Always` recap table had been deleted outright on the reasoning that it duplicated the numbered Steps above — missing that this repo's own CLAUDE.md ("Prompting Techniques for Commands" § Constitutional) prescribes exactly that table shape for write-decision skills. Restored, folding a since-dropped `git mv`-for-renames rule in as its own row.
- **`ship/SKILL.md` Step 3 had a numbered 1/2/3 checklist (which branch deploys / is there a gate / what rides along) flattened into one semicolon-joined run-on sentence during the same rewrite** — each item is independently substantial enough to check off on a real push, so the enumeration was load-bearing structure, not decoration. Restored by the code-simplifier pass.
- **Three pre-existing stale `templates.md` line-number citations** in `condense-task-doc/SKILL.md` and `merge-task-docs/SKILL.md` (citing `L134`/`L136`, actually resolving 2-8 lines off after unrelated prose condensing in `templates.md`) were caught while this session had the cross-referenced content open, and converted to the section name (`"Splitting a whole-doc MADR further"`) instead — matches this repo's own CLAUDE.md maintenance table, which already names stale line-number cross-refs as a known-bad pattern.
- **Recorded as D64** in `tasks/plugin-maintenance/doc-condensation/decisions/structural-splits.md` — the doc-condensation feature's `unhobble-instructions` lever (Task Status row 2c) now tracks 8 files across its two real runs, not 4.
- **No consumer action beyond the usual `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit`.** If anyone ran `unhobble-instructions` against a rule they knew was deliberately rigid (not just rigid-*looking*) since 1.136.21 and it read differently after, the same class of loss is possible there too — worth a re-check, but this session found only the one instance.

## 1.136.24

- **`/done`'s Step 5 accounting caught an undocumented unhobbling pass on `read-summary/SKILL.md` + `references/doc-authority.md` — same treatment as `update-plugin` (1.136.20) and `done` (1.136.22/23), but never recorded.** Every `⚠️`, bolded-ALL-CAPS opener, and `Tell:` trip-wire in both files converted to plain prose stating the same reasoning (confirmed: 0 remaining hits in either file, was several per file before). ## 1.136.23

Re-verification here was limited to confirming the line still reads as claimed and that `task-summary/SKILL.md` wasn't contested by the other in-flight session's edits before patching (`git status --short` on the file returned clean).

Scoped #16 out (belongs to `commit/SKILL.md`); used #15 and #17 as signal about which parts of `done` were load-bearing rather than as a fix queue — a deliberate user choice this session, since content-completeness work belongs to `update-plugin`, not this skill.

This directly closes #15's gap: a fourth case is now explicit, and the remedy table's Commit row no longer claims a pathspec always separates two writers — only when the split is file-level; when it isn't, the fix is to stop and ask the user, not commit any slice of the mixed file.

This directly closes #17's gap: "whatever the fix/build loop itself produces is unreviewed new content" now falls out as the same kind of instance as the other four, rather than needing a bolted-on fifth rule — issue #17's three concrete repro bugs (a suffix dropped on one of four exit paths, a permanently-stale label lookup, a type-clearing invariant broken by two handlers) were all in exactly this never-covered slice.

What moved: `Tell:` trip-wires 6→3, `⚠️` callouts 12→10 — fewer standalone rules asserted in isolation, which is the actual target of this pass, not byte count.

## 1.136.21

- **New skill: `unhobble-instructions`.** Two prior 1.136.x entries applied Anthropic's "Unhobbling Claude" framing to `update-plugin/SKILL.md` by hand — overconstraint via conflicting system-prompt/CLAUDE.md/skill directives, when a model can already reason about the same case from context. A Dourr session asked to generalize that pass into a standalone skill covering anything instructions/agent/skill/rule-shaped, rather than a one-off manual rewrite.
- **Scoped to four target classes with genuinely different judgement calls, not one flat "cut constraints" rule**: SKILL.md (constraint is almost always fair game, since a skill is only read in the moment a competent agent needs it); agent definitions (body prose gets the same scrutiny, but `tools:` frontmatter is a real mandate boundary, not overconstraint — removing a reviewer's `Edit` access isn't unhobbling, it changes what the agent is); CLAUDE.md/CLAUDE.local.md (loaded every turn unconditionally, so the cost profile is the opposite of a skill — if anything a stricter bar, not a looser one, even though the target pattern is the same); slash commands (usually too thin to need this, flagged mainly as a smell if one has grown a SKILL.md-shaped body of caveats it should instead delegate to a real skill).
- **Checked the installed official `skill-creator@claude-plugins-official` before writing anything** — confirmed active (not just cached) via `installed_plugins.json`, then deliberately didn't build on it. It's a generic, eval/benchmark-driven skill-authoring workflow (test prompts, quantitative variance analysis, novice-friendly jargon-checking) with no notion of syafiqkit's own conventions (frontmatter shape, `_shared/references/`, the version-bump protocol, `agent-setup`'s own explicit warning against "skill-creator baseline contamination"). Hand-authored against sibling skills' actual conventions instead — `agent-setup`'s frontmatter naming this exact anti-pattern is the reason it wasn't used as a starting point.
- **Self-checked the new skill's own body against its own test before registering it** — `grep -c "⚠️\|Tell:"` came back with 2 "Tell:" hits, both confirmed to be the skill *describing* the trip-wire pattern as the thing it looks for in a target file, not deploying one against its own reader; zero bolded-ALL-CAPS directive openers in the body. The frontmatter's two `Do NOT use for...` lines are the one exception left in place — that register is correct for a `description:` field (terse anti-triggers for keyword matching), which every sibling skill in the plugin does identically; the principle is scoped to the body prose, not the frontmatter.
- **Registered in both `CLAUDE.md`'s skill table and `README.md`'s** (the routing rule `update-plugin` itself states for "new skill created this session"). Verified with the plugin's own `comm -13` drift-check script for `CLAUDE.md`, and a corrected version of the same check for `README.md` — the first pass on README came back showing every single skill as simultaneously missing-and-stale, a `tr -d` pattern bug (not stripping a literal space) rather than a real drift; fixed the extraction and re-ran clean. Found three pre-existing, unrelated stale rows in README (`read-notes`, `update-notes`, `read-summary` naming mismatches against the skills-folder list) — flagged, not silently fixed, since they predate and are out of scope for this change.

## 1.136.20

- **A Dourr session pointed at Anthropic's "Unhobbling Claude" note — overconstraining through conflicting system-prompt/CLAUDE.md/skill directives when the model can already reason from context — and asked to apply it to `update-plugin/SKILL.md` itself as a real test, not just a discussion.** Re-read cold: nine `⚠️` callouts, most ending in a bolded imperative or a "Tell:" trip-wire naming a specific way to have failed; a mandatory line-by-line message-enumeration ritual in Step 1; an entire density sub-procedure (Step 3a) computing a byte/line ratio via `bc` against a fixed 90 threshold with its own forbidden-pattern table. The file was close to a worked example of the pattern being described — including the previous entry's own addition (1.136.19's Step 2 callout), added the same session as a fifth rigid trap instead of trusting a competent reader to ask the question unprompted.
- **Rewrote the file end to end** rather than patching individual lines — 164→109 lines, 24.1KB→12.0KB. Zero `⚠️` callouts, zero "Tell:" lines, zero bolded ALL-CAPS directives remain. Every fact with a real, silent-failure consequence survived verbatim: the OWNER/CONSUMER shell command byte-for-byte unchanged, all six Step 2 routing targets, the harness-behavior-needs-checking warning, the contested-file/diff-ownership hazard, the templated-agent-source rule, all reference-file pointers (`consumer-portability.md`, `upstream-consumer-finding.md`), the version-bump-skip convention. What didn't survive: the numeric B/L ratio and its `bc` formula (replaced with "does this read as bloated" as an explicit judgement call), the mandatory message-enumeration procedure (replaced with "read the whole session" as one sentence), and every "Tell:" self-catching mechanism (the underlying instinct folded into plain prose instead — e.g. Step 4's self-contradiction check is now one sentence with no bolded directive, ironically the shape the removed version was itself violating).
- **This is a real bet, not a risk-free tightening** — the deleted numeric threshold was a mechanical trigger that would reliably fire on bloat regardless of how the moment's read felt; removing it means "is this file bloated" now depends entirely on judgement in the moment, which is exactly what the source concept argues for but is a genuine trade-off, not a strict improvement. Worth revisiting if a future session's dense-file bloat goes unnoticed where the old ratio check would have caught it.
- **Verified via fact-survival grep, not just a read-through**: extracted every load-bearing fact from the pre-rewrite version (ownership commands, all 6 routing targets, all reference pointers, the version-skip convention) and confirmed each survived post-rewrite before reporting done — a full-file `Write` rewrite is exactly the shape where a real fact quietly drops out while the prose still reads plausibly.

- **`/done`'s own review agent then caught a real, pre-existing miscitation this grep didn't**: `other-modes.md`'s Rewrite-mode steps 4 and 8 (added in 1.136.13, per that entry below) both said "apply SKILL.md's prose-vs-table test" but cited `condense-claude-md/references/prose-vs-value-split.md` — a different skill's file, documenting a narrower, differently-scoped technique (splitting one dense row during condensation), not "SKILL.md's" test at all. Replaced the unconditional table default with the same judgement-vs-value test the prior entry validated: response is "it depends, reason about it" → prose ending in a `**Tell:**` sentence; response is "the answer is this specific string" → table row; a signal mixing both → prose with a `📖 <companion> {#anchor}` pointer carrying the exact value. ## 1.136.12

- **A condense/format-conversion pass can shed a rule's load-bearing appendages while the rule's core claim survives — verified as A/B in a Dourr session, not just theorized.** Two isolated Sonnet agents each answered 6 real scenarios using only one CLAUDE.local.md excerpt: an existing gotcha-table format vs. the same facts converted to judgement-style prose (per an Anthropic blog post's "rigid rules → let Claude use judgement" framing). Both scored correctly on every judgement-shaped question — but the prose agent explicitly downgraded confidence on the one question with a literal-value answer (an exact IP to bind), since prose had nowhere to carry a concrete value without becoming a table again. Executing the finding on the real file (prose for judgement + a companion for exact values, joined by per-paragraph `📖` pointers) then hit the SAME failure mode twice more: the experimental excerpt's own "trim" cut a real cross-reference pointer and a precedent example, and the live prose conversion softened away a specific error-code detail ("1146s") that had to be caught and restored. Three independent instances of the same shape: a pointer, a precedent example, or an exact value reads as a short trailing clause, not a rule of its own, so the sentence around it looks complete without it and it gets cut as connective tissue.
- **Shared-mechanism grep run before choosing a home** (`grep -rln "prose.*table\|table.*prose\|Prefer bullet lists over tables\|Compress, don't delete" skills/*/SKILL.md`, narrowed from a first pass on the bare word "table" which false-positived on nearly every skill): two real owners of the trim-vs-lose-information concern — `condense-claude-md` and `condense-task-doc` — both already delegating their verify step to `_shared/references/two-tier-condense.md`. Added the appendages-survive check there (item 7, renumbering 7→9) as the canonical, once-only fix for both callers, rather than duplicating it in either skill.
- **Placement**: `condense-claude-md/SKILL.md` was already over the ~90 B/L gate (165.5) with no retirable rule found (checked — nothing in the file names a removed tool/format), so the split-technique documentation is a **declared growth**: one sentence added to Restructuring #7 (165.5 → 167.6 B/L) naming the new lever and pointing to a new reference, `references/prose-vs-value-split.md` (2.9KB, under the 6KB prose ceiling) — a within-row content-type split (judgement stays inline as prose, attached exact values move to a companion) as an alternative to #7's existing whole-row-by-frequency move. Kept narrowly scoped per the session's own framing: a diagnostic + one documented option, not a mandate to always split content this way.

**Action for readers**: `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit`. No re-run needed on past condense/rewrite output — this doesn't invalidate prior work, it adds a check for the NEXT one. If a past session hand-converted a CLAUDE.md section from table to prose (or ran a heavy trim pass) without this check, worth a spot-verify that pointers/precedents/exact values from the original survived somewhere.

## 1.136.11

- **`update-claude-docs` had a rule for pointer-delegated content being invisible to grep, but only in the direction where the rule is MISSING — not where it is present and STALE.** In a remote-cli session `--scp` was reimplemented as an rsync alias (directories now allowed, `--dry-run` no longer connects). The routing pass grepped `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` and the project `CLAUDE.md`, and the global file returned exactly **one** hit for `scp`: its own `> 📖` pointer line advertising the companion's contents. That read as "the global file doesn't describe `--scp` behaviour, nothing to update," so `.claude-companions/shared/CLAUDE-remote-cli.md` was never opened — it still said `--scp` was single-file-only, and the global pointer still advertised "`--scp` silent failure," the exact bug the work had deleted. The existing rule's `Tell:` ("you can quote the rule verbatim yet grep finds it nowhere") cannot fire here, because the grep *succeeded*; nothing signals. The user had to find and fix it, then ask twice ("inspect why the update-claude-docs fail to notice" → "tell me why u fail to notice this"). Their fix also carried a wrong claim — `Sent <size> -> user@host:<path>` — where the real output is `Sent <local-path> -> …` with ` (<size>)` only for a single file via `--scp`, so a directory or any `--sync` prints no size and a reader greps for a string that never appears.
- **Second, independent gap in the same miss: nothing scoped the routing pass by the artifact's blast radius.** `remote` is a global CLI on `PATH`; changing it invalidates instructions in the global corpus that every other project loads, while the in-repo docs that *were* fixed read as complete. Added as its own rule — the trigger is "the thing you changed is invoked from projects other than this one," not "a companion exists."
- **Shared-mechanism grep run before choosing a home** (`grep -rn "claude-companions\|companion files\|companion targets" skills/`): the *write* path has one owner (`update-claude-docs`); `read-summary` owns the *read* path (tree walk + companion pointers) and already handles it correctly. One owner → patch there, not `_shared/`.
- **Placement**: target was over the ~90 B/L gate (94.1), so this is a **route + replace**, not an addition. Created `references/pointer-discipline.md` (2.5KB, under the 6KB prose ceiling) consolidating four scattered pointer rules — following a pointer, a pointer's own `Covers:` summary going stale, inlining facts beside one, and picking a target by content not folder name. **Retired** two rows restating the memory-write callout already at the top of the file, and one row superseded by the new reference. Net 94.1 → 94.3 B/L (+142B) with two new rules absorbed. Also added a `Match only inside a > 📖 pointer line → Not a match — descend` row to the classification table, which is what would have caught this.

**Action for readers**: `claude plugin update syafiqkit@syafiqkit`. If a past session documented a change to a globally-installed tool (a CLI on `PATH`, a shared script), re-check that tool's companion file and the global pointer's `Covers:` summary — the previous rule would not have flagged either as stale. Skills are re-read per invocation, so no regeneration is needed.

- **`/done`'s exit gate told sessions to verify a doc write with `git diff` and named two causes of a false empty, but not the one that is permanent: the target may be gitignored.** In a Dourr session `update-claude-docs` wrote to `CLAUDE.local.md` (gitignored at `.gitignore:23`, and one of that skill's two primary targets), so `git diff HEAD --stat -- CLAUDE.local.md` returned empty with exit 0. The two documented causes — a staged target, a CWD-relative pathspec — are both incidental, fixed by a different invocation; an untracked target can never be shown by any git command, so escalating to a better git command loops. The prescribed recovery (grep for the text) was right but framed as a fallback for an inconclusive result, so the natural reading is to try harder with git first, and a session would report a landed write as a skipped step. Caught only by an unprompted `git ls-files --error-unmatch`. The user then asked *"but i already make respect gitignore false ?"* — a real and separate trap now recorded: that setting governs Claude Code's own search tools, not git's plumbing, and it genuinely does make the file readable, which is exactly why the grep works and the diff still doesn't.
- **Shared-mechanism grep run before choosing a home** (`grep -rn "show-toplevel\|diff HEAD --stat" skills/`): found a **second owner** — `_shared/references/two-tier-condense.md`'s Diff step carried the same empty-diff-is-inconclusive rule with the same two causes and the same gap. Two owners → canonical home is `_shared/`, not either caller. `update-claude-docs`' own Validate step is a third consumer.
- **Placement**: both callers were over the ~90 B/L gate and carrying another writer's uncommitted work, so this is a **route**, not an addition. Created `_shared/references/verifying-a-write-landed.md` (2.0KB, under the 6KB prose ceiling) holding all three causes as a table with the command that settles each; replaced the inline prose in `done/SKILL.md` (133.9 → 133.2 B/L, **−181 bytes**) and `two-tier-condense.md` (178.1 → 173.9 B/L, **−135 bytes**) with pointers. `update-claude-docs/SKILL.md` gained a pointer on its existing re-grep step (95.6 → 96.4 B/L, +191B) — the one declared growth, since that step is where the grep already lives and splitting it would separate the check from its reason. Retirement checked and unavailable: all three causes are live, and `diff-ownership.md` is a different mechanism (whose a change is, not whether it exists).

## 1.136.9

- **`/ship` Step 4 was titled "Verify CI/CD Deploy" and framed the deploy as something to OBSERVE, with no concept of the operator PERFORMING post-deploy actions.** In a halalgig session the backend deployed cleanly (renumber command, 2 migrations, caches) and the ship reported done, while the React half never moved — `git pull` ships frontend *source*, and prod has no `node_modules` to build it. The gap is silent by construction: every check on the deployed half passed, prod HEAD matched local, and the deployed bundle was 10 days stale. Compounding it, the session first declined the build citing halalgig's `CLAUDE.md` row *"User will build manually; never run production builds"* — reading a default-workflow rule as absolute after an explicit override, which reads as procedural correctness and is the harder error to see. Section retitled **"Deploy and Verify"**; two rules added — enumerate the artifacts a change spans from the DIFF's file types (a bundled frontend, container image, compiled asset, mobile binary is untouched by a repo push), and an explicit instruction to carry the whole ship overrides a project rule reserving a step for the user.
- **Second defect, same session: a backgrounded `remote --scp` returned control without transferring, and the next step assumed the file was there.** The wrapper had rejected an absolute path (`Invalid local path`), the call was backgrounded, and nothing reported a failure — the destination `ls` came back empty only because the user asked "is it running properly what ? if not, try scp directly". Added a rule: verify a transfer at the DESTINATION, never from the sending command's exit, and drop to the plain tool when a wrapper rejects its own arguments.
- **Placement**: `ship/SKILL.md` was at 121.0 B/L, over the ~90 gate → **route + declared growth**. Created `ship/references/ci-provider-polling.md` (1.5KB) and moved the CI-provider detail out — provider commands plus three traps (empty list read as no-deploy, one push fanning out to several runs, a retried run reusing its id) — genuinely cold-path, since a repo with no CI config skips it entirely. Net 121.0 → 126.1 B/L (+1005B): the route recovered most of the growth, not all. Retirement checked and unavailable — `gh` is still installed and the `reset --hard` orphaning rules guard a live destructive trap.
- **Shared-mechanism grep run before choosing a home**: `grep -rln "deploy" skills/*/SKILL.md` returns 10 files, but reading each shows no sibling owns the deploy-EXECUTION step — `commit` only cross-references ship's staleness override, `done` handles infra/ops *review* modes, `read-summary` covers deploy *questions*. Single-file patch, not `_shared/`.
- **Also fixed silent version drift**: `marketplace.json` sat at 1.136.7 while `plugin.json` was 1.136.8. Both now 1.136.9.

## 1.136.8

- **`/done` Step 1 had no handling for an agent that FAILS to run, and the exit gate's wording let the failure pass as success.** In a halalgig session the project `code-simplifier` died instantly with `There's an issue with the selected model (sonnet  # project override: …)` — a trailing YAML comment on the `model:` key, which parses as part of the model string. `grep -inE "fail|died|terminated|API error"` over `done/SKILL.md` returned only rules about *code* failing; nothing about an *agent* failing. The exit gate table compounds it: its Simplify row is fillable if you "spawned simplifier agent(s)", and the agent WAS spawned — so a role that produced zero findings because it never ran is indistinguishable in the Output table from a role that ran and found nothing, and `✅ none needed` is the natural, wrong thing to write. `/done` is the one skill that manufactures evidence of completion, so a false `✅` here is later read back and believed. Recovery also had to be hand-derived: fixing the agent file does **not** help mid-session (the registry is read at session start, so the re-run failed identically), and the working move is to re-run the ROLE as `general-purpose` with an explicit `model:` matching what the agent file pins.

Consistent with the documented pass that found 0 of ~7 candidates actually dead — no retirement was available, so the rule was appended to the exit gate it repairs rather than manufacturing an offset. Rule body is abstracted (fires on any agent failure, not this YAML cause); the concrete incident lives here.
- **Shared-mechanism grep run before choosing a home**: five skills spawn agents (`agent-setup`, `done`, `merge-task-docs`, `read-summary`, `update-claude-docs`), but `grep -c 'Output row\|substantiate\|✅ none needed'` returns 0 for all four siblings — the completion-claim mechanism is unique to `/done`, so `_shared/` would be over-routing. Single-file patch.
- **Gate B correctly did not fire.** The checkout still carries a prior session's uncommitted edits across 13+ skill files; diff-content check found 0 markers from this session in `done`/`task-summary`/`update-claude-docs`/`agent-setup`, so their rules were left untouched and unaudited. The project-specific half (the YAML trap + cache + `general-purpose` recovery) went to halalgig's root `CLAUDE.md`, not here.

## 1.136.2

- **A second full test-suite run was launched while an identical one was still in flight, and the corruption presented as a code defect.** Two `RefreshDatabase` suites sharing one database truncate each other's tables, so the failure lands on whichever test happens to be mid-flight — a single-file run later reported 1 of 4 failing purely from a killed-but-still-running suite, and was nearly diagnosed as a real bug in the feature under test. What made the re-launch likely: the background command piped through `tail -4`, which buffers until exit, so an empty output file read as "failed or never started" rather than "still running". The user had to interrupt ("why u run the same shell, tha's in the background ?").
- **Routed to `_shared/references/long-running-commands.md`** (new, 2.0KB): check `pgrep -f` before re-launching a minutes-long *mutating* command (read-only probes exempt); an empty background-output file means buffering, never failure; block with a `kill -0` loop instead of polling; and never report a duration without its pass/fail count beside it — a suite that errors during setup finishes fast and looks like a speedup.
- **Arrival-rate accounting (Step 3a): route, not addition.** `done/SKILL.md` 131.1 → 132.1 B/L (already above the ~90 gate, so a bare addition was not available) — the substance went to the new reference and only a one-line symptom-naming pointer landed inline, on the Step 1 bullet that mandates re-running a verification command. No retirement applied: nothing in that file's re-run guidance is superseded by this. Shared-mechanism grep run across all `skills/*/SKILL.md` and `_shared/references/` — the five files mentioning background commands all concern *agent/file* contention (`diff-ownership`, contested docs), a different axis; no existing rule covers command-level concurrency and `pgrep -f` appears in no SKILL.md.

## 1.136.1

- **`task-builder`'s own instruction was licensing the violation it was blamed for.** The user reported agents writing long code comments "disregarding our rules" — but the template said *"match surrounding code's naming, idiom, and comment density"*, so a heavily-commented file authorised more heavy commenting. The agent was complying. Measured across three recent Autorentic commits: comment ratios of 20%, 20% and 24% all passed the global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` ~25% bar while the same diffs held **ten comment blocks over the 3-line cap**, one of 13 lines and two of 11. A ratio always passes on a large diff, which is how a violating diff reads as clean to the agent writing it. Dropped `and comment density`; added a `Do NOT` row with the ratio explicitly banned as a self-check.
- **The cap was then corrected downward: the default is ZERO inline comments, not three lines.** The first fix shipped a 3-line cap, which the user rejected on sight — a numeric cap reads as a budget to spend, so an agent writes three lines because three are allowed. Since the task doc already holds the rationale, the correct default is none, with **1 line** as the exception when a comment prevents a specific mistake at the code's own level. `@param`/`@return` docblocks are scoped OUT (confirmed with the user): they're a typed signature contract consumed at the call site by intelephense/tsc, not prose duplicating a task doc.
- **Arrival-rate accounting (Step 3a): declared growth, no retirement available.** 66.7 → 68.9 B/L, well under the ~90 gate. The `density` clause was a **replace** (deleted, not merely superseded); the cap row is net +1 rule with nothing to retire — no existing row stated a per-block limit, which is precisely the gap that let the defect ship. Shared-mechanism grep run across all `agent-setup/templates/`: no sibling carries a code-comment ratio instruction (the `claude-md-pruner` `density` hit is doc byte-density, an unrelated mechanism).
- **Scope limited on the user's instruction.** The live `.claude/agents/task-builder.md` copies were enumerated (Autorentic, Dourr, plus 70+ immutable version-scoped `plugins/cache/` snapshots that are never patch targets). Autorentic's copy was fixed earlier in the session; **Dourr's copy still carries the old `comment density` line** and was deliberately left — template parity is restored at the source, so the next `agent-setup` regeneration corrects it.

## 1.136.0

- **A real consumer ran `/audit-instructions`, produced a complete 25-skill audit, then stopped at the last step to ask where to write.** Step 5 named `tasks/plugin-maintenance/external-guidance/current.md` relatively, which resolves against *their* project. Everything upstream had worked — a write instruction is simply the one shape with no safe default, so the session asked rather than degrading. Recorded as D61.
- **The first fix was wrong on every axis, and the user caught it: consumers install via the marketplace, on macOS/Windows/WSL.** Measured afterward rather than assumed: `tasks/` **does not ship at all**, installs are **version-scoped** (`plugins/cache/<marketplace>/<plugin>/<version>/` — 90 versions deep on this machine), `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` does not expand in markdown ([#9354](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/9354)), `~` does not resolve on native Windows, and a `~/.claude` shared with WSL stores paths broken on the other side ([#36575](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/36575)). There is no absolute path that works — so Step 5 now names none, handing verdicts to `update-plugin`, which owns the ownership gate. Three research passes independently agreed: delegate rather than add a second probe.
- **The ownership probe was returning the right answer for the wrong reason.** `git -C ~/.claude/plugins/syafiqkit remote get-url origin` **walks up** to the enclosing `~/.claude` dotfiles repo and resolves *its* remote (`my-claude-settings.git`); the grep missed, so `CONSUMER` came back by luck and would invert for anyone who forked that settings repo. Rewritten as a CWD probe (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`) and verified in three environments: OWNER from the checkout, CONSUMER from a non-git dir and from `~/.claude` itself. `done`'s Gate B carried the identical `git -C` defect and was fixed in the same pass — found by grep, not by memory.
- **The audit's headline finding about itself was a measurement artifact.** `audit-instructions` reported itself the fleet's top grower at **+168L** — its exact total length, one day after creation. `git log --since --numstat` counts an in-window *creation* as growth, so every new skill ranks #1 on its first audit. Step 1 now disqualifies in-window creations via `--diff-filter=A`; the corrected ranking surfaced the real top grower (`done`, +43L) that the artifact had masked. A `%+d` format string was also defeating `sort -rn` (`+9, +8, +43`) — bare `%d` restored the ordering the command exists to produce.
- **Both `/done` reviewers independently flagged the new receiving branch as unreachable.** `update-plugin` Step 1's arrival-rate branch skipped Steps 1–2 whenever a handoff carried *any* arrival-rate file — and arrival rate is measured every run, so the row recording verdicts was dead on the common path. Narrowed to *solely* arrival-rate, with an explicit mixed-handoff rule. Same N-owners shape as D59, one layer down. The consumer's upstream flow was likewise singular-defect shaped against a 22-finding payload; a fleet audit now files as ONE issue with the table as body.
- **Arrival-rate accounting (Step 3a) — the portability rules were a ROUTE, not growth.** Five skills embed consumer-facing shell commands, past the 3-owner threshold, so the new material went to `_shared/references/consumer-portability.md` (2.6KB, single-topic, under the ~6KB ceiling) with symptom-naming pointers from `update-plugin` and `audit-instructions` rather than prose in either. `update-plugin` 136.9 → 144.3 B/L — the Step 0 rewrite **replaced** its old probe and its four-clause path warning collapsed into the reference; net +1 rule (the harness-facts research gate), **declared**: no further retirement available. `audit-instructions` 98.3 → 102.2 B/L — Step 5's path text was **replaced** by the handoff sentence, Step 1 grew by the disqualification block, **declared**, same reason D59 declared its last overage. `done` 132.1 → **131.1** B/L — a pure **replace** (the `git -C` line), the one file whose density improved.
- **Scope came in narrower than approved, on evidence rather than effort.** A full sweep found 7 further sites; all 7 were correct as written. The 6 `.claude/agents/*.md` hits have templates that are already generic (`tasks/<domain>/<feature>/current.md`), so the hardcoded domain belongs in this repo's own specialized copies — rewriting them would have broken them, and the parity rule was satisfied, not violated. `task-summary/references/templates.md` cites the path as split history, not as a path to read.

## 1.135.2

- **`Explore` kept calling `Write` against the plan file in Plan Mode — the guard held, so the fix was never a permission.** `disallowedTools: [Write, Edit]` blocked every attempt, surfacing an `Error writing file` and a wasted turn. The asked question was "should we grant it Edit?"; the answer is no — granting Write would let a `haiku` search agent clobber the plan file the lead session is actively building, trading a visible harmless error for a silent one. Root cause is name shadowing: the built-in `Explore` *is* spawned with `Write` in Plan Mode, and the harness's "build your plan incrementally" instruction is addressed to the **lead**, not the subagent that reads it.
- **Fixed by role correction, not more emphasis.** The existing prohibition was already ⚠️, bold, and first in `## Bootstrap (Do This First)` — restating it louder is the arrival-rate treadmill D50 measured and rejected. Instead extended the pattern already proven in the same file (`Constraints` → "You ARE the search step — never hand it back"), which fixes the identical lead-instruction-absorbed-by-subagent misattribution: line 25 now leads with the deliverable ("your findings ARE the deliverable") and reattributes the caller-directed text, `## Output Format` closes the loop ("this response text IS what the caller receives"), and a new `Constraints` row carries the `Tell:`. Landed in both `.claude/agents/Explore.md` and `skills/agent-setup/templates/Explore.template.md` per the parity mandate, preserving each file's own vocabulary (the template stays generic; the generated agent names its concrete siblings).
- **De-primed on review: the first draft reproduced the trigger phrase it was redirecting.** The product review flagged that quoting the harness's own wording verbatim — twice per invocation, including the ~99% of dispatches not in Plan Mode — reproduces the exact string whose first-person absorption *is* the root cause. Both mentions now describe the instruction structurally instead of quoting it. The only surviving mention is a YAML comment in frontmatter explaining why `disallowedTools` exists — maintainer-facing, not instruction text.
- **Then re-anchored, because pure abstraction traded a loud failure for a silent one.** The second product review made the decisive point: de-priming's risk is not symmetrical with priming's. Quoting the trigger fails *rarely* (Plan Mode only) and *announces itself* ("Error writing file"); an abstraction that fails to bind fails on *every* Plan-Mode dispatch and is *indistinguishable* from the fix never having applied — same error, no signal it regressed. `model: haiku` is also the tier least able to complete an unanchored three-way match (this is onboarding text → it targets my caller → withhold). Final wording names the target category — "the harness's own Plan Mode framing" — giving a concrete thing to match against while keeping zero occurrences of the trigger string. Applied at both intercepts in both files.
- **Arrival-rate accounting (Step 3a), both files already above the ~90 B/L gate at HEAD.** `.claude/agents/Explore.md` 96.0 → 103.0 B/L, `Explore.template.md` 90.9 → 96.7 B/L; +625 bytes and **+1 line** each. The change is a **replace**, not an addition — the Bootstrap prohibition was rewritten in place rather than appended to, and two clauses were retired as redundant (a hand-off restatement already carried by Bootstrap, and a sentence duplicating its own row header four words earlier). Residual growth is the one new `Constraints` row, **declared**: no further retirement was available. The adjacent `Read-only | Never Edit/Write` row was assessed as a retirement candidate and kept — it guards all writes, where the new row guards one misattribution; cutting it would widen the gap it covers.
- **Scope verified by grep, not assumption.** The warning existed in exactly 2 files, not the 11-file copy-paste spread this repo has hit before. `browser-verifier`/`product-reviewer` are read-only by design but shadow no built-in name, so they carry no lineage impulse — correctly out of scope. The 4 literal `subagent_type: "Explore"` dispatch sites inject no Plan-Mode text, confirming the priming reaches the subagent from the harness, which is why the fix belongs in the agent file. Rename was costed and rejected; it stays the **final** escalation if the symptom recurs. Not proven, and cannot be — the repo has no harness to replay a Plan-Mode `Explore` spawn, so confidence rests on analogy to a rule that worked in this same file, not on this rule having been tested. Watch for the absence of "Error writing file" over subsequent Plan-Mode sessions.

## 1.135.1

- **The one-turn-chain rule was read, correctly placed, and violated twice in the same session — so the gap was in what the cue is addressed to.** Both stops came at a sub-skill return (`update-claude-docs` → Step 4, then `task-summary` → Step 5), and both times the closing text *named* the next step: "Next in the /done chain is Step 4, task-summary." `done/SKILL.md`'s boundary cues at Steps 3 and 4 fire on the act of invoking, which a sentence naming the step feels like it has already satisfied. Added the missing half to `_shared/references/one-turn-chain.md`: naming the next step is itself the stop, and the name is the trigger to call it in that same message. **Routed to the shared reference, not `done/SKILL.md`** — that file sits at 132 B/L, far above the ~90 gate, and a fourth callout restating the same rule is the "two or more ⚠️ re-justifying one rule" anti-pattern Step 3a forbids. `ship` shares the mechanism and points at the same reference, so both consumers pick it up. 2362B → 2927B, single-topic, under the ~6KB prose ceiling.
- **Declined to patch a second signal: inventing a storage convention when `.claude-companions/<shared|local>/` already existed.** `update-claude-docs/references/structure.md:173` already prescribes it exactly, down to the tracked-vs-gitignored split and the `~/.claude` special case. The rule was never consulted because the work was framed as secret extraction rather than a companion split — a routing miss in judgment, not a defect in the skill. Captured as a hot-path callout in the user's global `CLAUDE.md` instead; duplicating it here would have put one rule in two artifacts.

## 1.135.0

- **New skill `audit-instructions` — the plugin could grade one skill but never its corpus.** `update-plugin` Step 3a grades a file when a session touches it; `/done` Gate B measures only that session's edits. Both are per-file by construction, so corpus-wide drift was invisible and "audit all our skills" had no method behind it. The new skill mirrors `sweep-doc-overlaps`: measure first, fan out graders **partitioned by file**, verify inline, hand flagged files to Step 3a. It carries **no threshold** — the B/L gate and density checklist stay with Step 3a, cited not restated. Its one new measurement is the **fleet arrival ratio**, genuinely absent elsewhere. 112 lines at 84.9 B/L, under the gate on arrival.
- **Graded all 24 skills against D55's four verdicts: 14 came back clean.** Measured first: emphasis 235 corpus-wide, 10 of 24 with a `references/` dir, and a 7-day arrival of **added 833 / removed 346 / net +487 — 2.41:1 across 22 commits**, worse than the +418 that made D50 reject re-condensing. The request implied the prose needed work; the measurement said the arrival rate does.
- **Three grader findings were disproved by re-reading their cited lines, and all three had correct line numbers.** A claimed absence of inbound pointers to `_shared/references/` (all 8 have 2–5; the grep's own filter excluded them, caught by a must-hit control) · a claimed duplication of `writing-style.md` (already pointed at it, rows distinct — one genuinely duplicated `Capture filter` row, deleted) · a claimed empty `## Key Rules` heading (the grep could not see fenced code blocks).
