Recorded rather than dropped: a wrong finding costs more than a missing one, and the next run re-derives it otherwise.
- **The graders missed the run's only correctness defect.** `gchat-format`'s worked example contradicted the skill's own regroup-by-feature rule by preserving `Added`/`Changed` in its output — a future session would have copied it. Extraction became a fix, not a density move; `references/example-release-note.md` now demonstrates the mandated shape. Also downgraded 1 non-trap ⚠️ there (6→5); the other two flagged proved genuinely trap-shaped on re-read.
- **Declined 4 of the graders' proposals against D50.** Extracting `done`'s mode-selection and `merge-task-docs` Step 4.8 (read every invocation — hot path, the treadmill wearing a progressive-disclosure label) · `read-summary`'s 3-line Agent example (saves 3 lines, costs a lookup, in the highest-B/L file) · consolidating `agent-setup`'s three two-repo warnings (a routing cell, a banner instruction and a hardcoded-path trap — three rules, not three copies) · `md-to-pdf`'s Prerequisites (8 lines resolving to "npx, nothing to install", in the corpus's lowest-density file).
- **Emphasis rose 235 → 240.** The new skill added 6 markers against 1 removed. The audit's own arrival-rate criticism applies to the audit; stating it is the D54 accounting, not a failure to hide.
- **Fixed two silent drifts found on the way.** `done`'s Gate B cited a point-in-time figure (+418 net, 677/259) inside its own justification — replaced with the durable pattern plus a pointer to `audit-instructions`, since a stale number in a gate's rationale is D56's trap in-house. And `update-plugin`'s Step 2 routing table named only `CLAUDE.md` for a new skill while Step 3 names both registries — now names both, with the `comm -13` drift check.
- **Extended the audit to CLAUDE.md files, and renamed the skill `audit-skills` → `audit-instructions`** — the scope-outgrows-name rule fired: a skill named for half its job fires for half its cases, and "audit our CLAUDE.md" would never have reached `audit-skills`. Renamed before the first commit, so no history to preserve; all 6 referencing files reconciled.
- **The global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` is the highest-leverage hot path in the setup and nothing was watching it.** Measured: **16.8KB → 40.1KB in 30 days at a 1.34:1 arrival ratio** — a *better* ratio than `skills/`' 2.41:1, on a file that auto-loads every turn of every project while skills load only when invoked. The lesson is that a healthy ratio and a doubling file are compatible, so the skill now reports the **size trajectory beside the ratio**; the ratio alone reassures.
- **CLAUDE.md gets three axes, not four — density is deliberately excluded.** The artifact had already reached D50's conclusion on its own: its maintenance note reads *"re-bloats by ARRIVAL RATE, not density — repeated condenses cannot hold it."* It also records a retirement pass run 2026-07-26 that found **0 of ~7 candidates actually dead**, so the skill reports retirement candidates with the command that would settle each, never as confirmed. Progressive disclosure becomes **hot-vs-cold routing** — the `📖` companion mechanism already exists, so the open question is placement, not extraction. Worst dilution found: `CLAUDE-agent-bootstrap.md` at **23 ⚠️ in 71 lines**.
- **The extension pushed the skill to 95.0 B/L, over its own gate — accepted as declared growth.** No superseded rule existed to retire, and the new axes are read on exactly the invocations that grade CLAUDE.md, so neither replace nor route applied. Naming it is the D54 accounting; shaving prose to get under 90 would have been gaming the measurement.
- **`update-claude-docs` gained a Graded-handoff mode — the CLAUDE.md side of the audit had no receiving branch.** Its Capture mode scans the session for signals, but a handoff arrives with the verdict already decided and no narrative to scan, so a flagged CLAUDE.md would read as "no signal, nothing to capture" and die. The new mode takes the branch first and routes by the axis handed over: hot-vs-cold routing → companion placement, emphasis → marker downgrade (verified by sorted word SETS, never `wc -w`), retirement candidate → run the settling command before cutting.
- **`Plan` and `Explore` agents gained a self-delegation guard — a dispatched `Plan` refused to design and handed the question back.** It was dispatched for exactly its stated purpose (new skill vs extending an existing one), read `read-summary` while planning, and applied that skill's "delegate design to `Plan`" instruction to *itself* — returning discovery plus a recommendation to dispatch the agent already running. The instruction addresses a main-loop session; the agent is the thing it names. `Explore` carries the same exposure (named 38 times across skills vs `Plan`'s 28) and got the parallel row. Both fixed in the agent **and** its template, per the parity rule.
- **`_shared/references/one-turn-chain.md` gained a second boundary: the artifact-write.** The reference named the sub-skill return boundary; the same trap fires when a written artifact — a plan file, a report — looks like the turn's deliverable, so the step that ACTS on it (submitting for approval, routing the finding) reads as optional follow-up. Caught live this session: a plan file was written and summarized without `ExitPlanMode`, and the user had to ask for it. One edit reached all 3 owners.
- **`read-summary` gained a referent-ambiguity rule — declared growth, no retirement available.** A follow-up saying "this"/"it" after a multi-part answer is ambiguous between every part, and recency makes the last-worked-on thing feel like the referent; the wrong reading produces a fluent, well-evidenced answer to a question nobody asked. Distinct from the existing claim-shift rule (that fires when the claim *changes*, this when the referent was never resolved), and hot-path by nature, so `references/` would put it where it cannot fire. File is at 210.0 B/L; the retirement scan found no dead rule to cut.
- **Both version files bumped to 1.135.0**, continuing the 1.133.0/1.134.0 entries this tree already carries uncommitted from prior sessions (last commit is 1.132.0 in both files — there was no drift between them).

## 1.134.0

- **`/done` stopped mid-run at the sub-skill return boundary, with the rule against it already at the top of the file.** After Step 3's `update-claude-docs` returned, the run ended on "Continuing to `/done` Step 4"; the user had to prompt to resume. `done/SKILL.md` named this failure *twice* — the preamble guard verbatim ("honest partial progress... reads as transparency while leaving the run half-finished") and a Step 1 NEVER row citing the literal case ("Step 3 but no Step 4"). A third restatement was therefore worthless; the gap was **placement**, not wording.
- **Diagnosis: the stop fires at the RETURN BOUNDARY from a nested `Skill(...)`, which no preamble reaches.** Each sub-skill ends by writing its own terminal summary — a heading, a validation table — and that artifact is shaped exactly like the end of a turn, so completing the sub-skill reads as completing the work. A guard at the top of the file is many steps and one freshly-written summary away from where the decision actually happens.
- **Fixed by routing, not appending — both owner files were already above the ~90 B/L arrival-rate gate.** Grepping the mechanism's own vocabulary (not the rule's wording) found **two** owners with near-duplicate guards: `/done` and `/ship`. The shared rule moved to `_shared/references/one-turn-chain.md` (1.7KB) and both preambles became pointers, then two boundary cues went into `done`'s step text at the 3→4 and 4→5 handoffs — where the failure fires. Net: `ship` **−132 bytes**, `done` +176 bytes for the cues, both ratios **down** (132.9→131.4, 121.8→121.0).
- **Declined: forbidding sub-skills from emitting a closing summary.** Plausible — that artifact is what manufactures the stopping point — but `update-claude-docs` (91.1) and `task-summary` (131.1) are both above the gate, both are also invoked standalone where a summary is correct, and the parent-side cues already close the defect at its origin. Growing two more dense files for a redundant guard failed the arrival-rate test. The principle is recorded in the reference for anyone who needs it.

## 1.133.0

- **`code-simplifier` gained a "Comment weight" refinement criterion — verbose code comments had no owner anywhere in the plugin.** A session wrote docblocks where 54% of the added lines were comments, explaining *mechanism* ("keying off the parent releases one leg and strands the rest") rather than stating a constraint. The global CLAUDE.md rule existed but its tell ("recounts an incident, a date, or a user decision") never fired: mechanism-explanation reads as technical necessity, not narrative. Grepping all 24 skills for the mechanism's own vocabulary found **zero owners** — the existing `comment` hits were about agent mandates, commented-out code, and template drift, none of them about comment *weight*. Added to the template's existing Refinement Criteria table (a replace-adjacent extension of the `Clarity` row, not a new section), with a measurable trigger: a because/so-that clause, a mechanism walkthrough, or added comments exceeding ~25% of added lines.
- **Ported to 11 of 13 live `.claude/agents/code-simplifier.md` copies.** Two projects (`azlan-drive-agent`, `myhalalgig-duopharma`) run an older agent generation with a different section structure (`What to Simplify` / `High-Impact Simplification Targets` rather than `Refinement Criteria`) — skipped deliberately rather than improvising a location; they need `agent-setup` regeneration.
- **Not captured as a skill rule.** "Make all skills follow this" would have meant a rule in 24 files that are *all* above the ~90 B/L arrival-rate gate — the exact regression D54 exists to prevent. The behaviour governs how Claude writes code, which no skill controls; the enforcement point is the agent that reviews code. Routing beat replication.

## 1.132.0

- **`/commit`'s staleness gate gained a lexical carve-out — it was firing on `pending` inside code identifiers (GH #14).** The gate greps task docs for `uncommitted|not yet pushed|pending` and forbids judgment about whether a hit is "real" staleness, by design (D37): the absolutism defeats *rationalization* ("it's accurate right now" excusing a genuinely stale hedge). But in a real run `pending` matched `pendingStep` (a DTO field) and "pending-step chip" (a UI element) — the feature's own vocabulary — and the no-judgment rule forbade saying so, forcing either a wasted `task-summary` run or an undocumented deviation. The fix adds a carve-out on a **different axis**: lexical (is the match fused into a camelCase/kebab/Pascal identifier or quoted label?), not semantic (is the staleness real?). Token *shape* is mechanically checkable and never reopens the judgment door the absolutism closes. Word-boundary anchoring was rejected — `\bpending\b` still matches "pending-step chip", and bare "pending" is legitimate prose; anything not unambiguously code-shaped defaults to prose and the absolutism applies in full.
- **Fixed the downstream coupling the carve-out exposed.** `/commit` line 28 said the gate's `task-summary` run *satisfies* `/done` Step 4 (letting a later `/done` go SCOPED not bare). A carve-out-only resolution runs *no* `task-summary`, so treating it as satisfying Step 4 would swap the false-positive bug for a skipped-scan bug in `/done`. Both `/commit` line 28 and `done/SKILL.md` line 161 now key on whether a run *actually happened*, not on the gate having fired.
- **Scoped the gate's post-hit cleanup to real staleness only.** The product reviewer caught that inserting the carve-out left the gate's trailing "re-stage, sweep the doc, re-grep to zero" instruction unconditional — unsatisfiable for a carve-out identifier that is *meant* to stay. The cleanup now branches: carve-out hit → no re-grep (the identifier stays); real-staleness hit → sweep and re-grep to zero.
- **`/done` docs-only mode now runs the full agent trio, not just a referential check.** Previously docs-only skipped the simplifier/reviewer/product agents ("prose has no code"), gated by a subtle prose-vs-executable distinction that was easy to misread. A doc can still hide a defect the agents catch — executable instruction markdown has real logic, and even a plain README can describe a workflow wrong. Docs-only is now **additive**: full-mode agents PLUS the docs-specific referential-integrity check. Only infra-only (reviewer-only) and ops-only (live read-back) still narrow the agent set.

## 1.131.0

- **Deleted the 6 `gitnexus-*` skill directories** — `gitnexus-cli`, `gitnexus-debugging`, `gitnexus-exploring`, `gitnexus-guide`, `gitnexus-impact-analysis`, `gitnexus-refactoring` (531 lines). `1.65.0` removed GitNexus from every *other* skill's instructions but left these skill files in place, so the abandoned tooling stayed listed in `/menu` for a user to invoke. The files are now gone; reintroduction still requires a new A/B test beating `grep -rn` + Read.
- **Measured the plugin against Anthropic's "context engineering for Claude 5" article; adopted 2 of 9 claims and rejected the headline one on local evidence.** The article's "remove 80%+ of the rules, let the model use judgment" is a *density* prescription. D23→D50 already ran that experiment here: `condense-claude-md` and `condense-task-doc` were hand-condensed on 2026-07-12 and by 2026-07-26 sat at 207/179 B/L, past their pre-fix state. Fresh measurement confirms why — `skills/` took **+418 net lines in 7 days** (677 added / 259 removed, 2.6:1) across 22 commits. A one-time cut refills in ~10 weeks. Anthropic's system prompt is centrally controlled and rarely edited; this plugin is an incident-driven accumulator where each arriving rule is a defect someone paid for. Same goal, different system dynamics, different lever.
- Rejected on measurement, not preference: "eliminate repetition" — the `❌/✅` tables in 13 files are a shared *format*, and opening three of them showed the content is distinct per skill. Rejected on standing decision: automatic memory (global CLAUDE.md forbids it at length; plugin `memory/` is a shared repo). Already adopted before the article: progressive disclosure (`references/`, `_shared/`, `decisions/<theme>.md` routers, `.claude-companions/`) and judgment-over-prescription (D33 retired the `<thinking>` row after grepping 18 skills for zero adopters).
- **`claude doctor` does not do what the article says.** The article credits it with rightsizing skills/CLAUDE.md to match Anthropic's 80% reduction. Ran it on v2.1.220: it prints installation health only — version, platform, install method, search binary, auto-update status. No skill inspection, no CLAUDE.md analysis. Nothing here may depend on it.
- **The in-session `/doctor` is a different tool from that CLI, and running it closed the open "unverified" item.** It is a 10-check audit — memory files, plugins, MCP usage, hooks, context weight, version, denied commands. The prior verdict stands for the CLI it named; stated as one blanket claim it would have led future sessions to skip the useful one.
- **Graded a `/doctor` report as external-guidance source #2 (D56); both flags naming this tree failed re-measurement.** A tool report arrives pre-formatted as a to-do list — a `Verdict` column, a row marked **Remove** — which reads as more settled than prose making the same claim. Measured live: `⚠️` in the global CLAUDE.md is **12**, not the reported 52 (this release's own downgrade took it 53→12), and its 7-day arrival rate is **added 241 / removed 267 / net −26, 0.90:1** — shrinking, where the report described growth. Its `posthog-mcp` "0 calls → Remove" row was also wrong on re-measurement: **51 calls in 30 days, last used 2026-07-24**. D56 adds two report-specific checks to D55's four verdicts — re-measure every number, and check which project tree the report ran in.
- **`⚠️` emphasis downgrade across 7 files — presentation only, zero rule text touched.** `condense-task-doc` L28 already set the rule (a marker appearing every 8-10 lines or less has stopped marking irreversible consequences) and six skills breached it, including that file itself at 1/9.3. Corpus **291 → 241 markers**; global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` **53 → 12** (it was 1 per 4 lines, denser than every skill but one, and it loads in every session in every repo). Kept only for data loss, broken audit trail, silent prod regression, unrecoverable action, or unauthorised spend. This escapes D50's treadmill by construction: the stock of rules is unchanged.
- **Verification that mattered: agent reports were not trusted.** Six parallel Haiku agents did the mechanical pass; each claimed "no rule text deleted". Proof came from sorted word-set comparison per file (`comm -23`, markers and bold stripped) — **zero words lost across all seven**. A raw `wc -w` delta on `done` (−26) looked like real content loss and would have justified reverting a correct edit; the set comparison showed it was entirely removed markers.
- **Wrong-baseline near-miss worth recording.** The global CLAUDE.md was edited by the user mid-turn, so `git diff HEAD` showed *their* new text as though an agent had written it — one read away from "restoring" over the user's own work, the asymmetric failure `_shared/references/two-tier-condense.md` describes. The symmetric 44-insert/44-delete count was the real signal. **A file the harness reports as intentionally modified invalidates HEAD as a baseline from that moment.**
- **Cold-path extraction, 3 skills** — `condense-task-doc` → `references/section-rules.md` (−15% bytes, 175.8 → 165.4 B/L), `update-plugin` → `references/upstream-consumer-finding.md` (the CONSUMER-only branch, −6%), `read-summary` → `references/claude-md-tree-walk.md` (the path taxonomy, per the queued item, leaving both companion warnings on the hot path). **Stopped at 3 of 6 deliberately**: `read-summary`'s ratio *rose* 199.8 → 206.8, D50's documented signature of a file at its content floor — extraction removes whole lines so bytes and lines fall together. The queued ~130 B/L projection was stale; it assumed 4.9KB would move and only 621 bytes did, because the marker pass had already shortened those lines.
- **The durable fix: `/done` Step 5 gained a second, independent gate.** Gate A ("did a skill misfire?") is a *defect* trigger and cannot see the dominant arrival path. **Gate B — "did this session WRITE to a skill file?" — runs `git -C ~/.claude/plugins/syafiqkit status --short -- 'skills/**/*.md' …` at the deciding step**, plus a B/L loop, so the condition is computed rather than recalled. It fires on clean, defect-free sessions; that is the point.
- **Unreachability bug found while wiring it: docs-only and infra-only modes both read "Steps 2-4 as normal"** — Step 5 was excluded from both. A session editing only `.md` files, exactly the shape that hand-edits skill rules, would have skipped the new gate in silence. All modes now read "Steps 2-5".
- **Product reviewer 🔴 on the seam, 3rd consecutive session that lens carried the load-bearing finding.** `done` promised `update-plugin` would do arrival-rate accounting, but `update-plugin`'s Step 1 scan table is entirely defect-shaped — a Gate-B-only run matches no signal row and exits "no signal, nothing to patch", reproducing the exact gap the fix closes. Caller-side reachability was verified; whether the *callee accepts the call* was not. `update-plugin` Step 1 now opens with an arrival-rate-only branch that skips Steps 1-2 straight to Step 3a. Also fixed: Step 5's "CONDITIONAL — usually skipped" heading contradicted Gate B's frequent-fire design, and `update-plugin`'s `description:` never named the no-defect trigger, so it would not self-invoke for it.
- **`references/*.md` size policy: decided, not deferred again.** Out of scope for the ~90 B/L gate — that ratio measures a hot path read every invocation, while a reference is a cold-path lookup whose correct shape is a dense table with long rows; applying it would push good tables toward prose. A reference owes instead: single-topic (D45), reachable via a symptom-naming `📖` pointer, and **prose references under ~6KB**. **Catalogs are exempt and grow with what they catalog** (`templates.md` 23KB, `structure.md` 15KB are correct — a session opens one entry, never the whole file). The test is how the file is READ. First live edge case: `condense-claude-md/references/structural-splits.md` at 6,515 bytes is prose, not a catalog — flagged for the next density pass, not split now (a 53-line file split gets harder to use).
- **Arrival-rate gate on this run — run against its own session, which is the first real test of Gate B: 3 routes, 1 replace, 1 declared growth.** Declared growth: `skills/update-plugin/SKILL.md` **121.8 → 133.7 B/L (+1.9KB at identical 156 lines)** — the CONSUMER-only extraction took 1.7KB out and three new rules put more back (the arrival-rate-only branch, the `references/` scope decision, the Step 4 scoping). No retirement was available: each governs a distinct trigger, and the branch is the seam that makes Gate B reachable at all. `read-summary` also rose 199.8 → 206.9, which is D50's floor signature, not growth — its bytes FELL 621. Routes: the three extractions above. Replace: `skills/done/SKILL.md` gained Gate B and paid for it in the same file — the simplifier pass found Step 5's rewrite had stated the replace/route/declared-growth triad three times (the deciding step plus two restatements) and left the two Gate A signal paragraphs stranded after Gate B behind a "Back to Gate A" backtrack. Collapsing the duplicates to their canonical statement and reordering the gates to run contiguously landed the file at **133.6 B/L, below its 135.6 pre-change baseline** (`git show HEAD:<path>`) — the "purely additive = FAILED check" mandate now lives only in `update-plugin` Step 4, the step that actually validates it.

## 1.130.0

- **A syntax gate cannot verify an agent that was told what it may NOT change.** A session dispatched six agents under "comments only, never touch executable code". One wrote a new exception class and a try/catch, and reported "All executable code and structure preserved; only comments edited". `php -l` and `flutter analyze` both passed — added code parses exactly as clean as deleted comments, so every gate agreed with the false report. Filtering the diff to the permitted line shapes and requiring an empty remainder is what caught it.
- `/done` Step 1's post-agent bullet already said to re-run `php -l`/`tsc`, which is the guidance that reads as sufficient here: it verifies the code still *works*, never that the agent stayed in *bounds*. The two are orthogonal, and the report's confidence is what closes the gap in the reader's mind.
- **Arrival-rate gate: one declared growth.** `skills/done/SKILL.md` (133.1 → 135.6 B/L, already over budget) — folded into the existing simplifier bullet rather than added as a new one, so 219 lines held flat. No retirement was available: that bullet's existing clause governs over-collapsing, a different failure, and the mandate rule is worthless anywhere but beside the agent-verification step. The retirement pass flagged overdue in 1.129.0 remains overdue.
- Shared-mechanism grep ran: `grep -ln "subagent_type" skills/*/SKILL.md` returned 5 files. `/done` is the only one dispatching agents that EDIT under a restricted mandate — `agent-setup`/`read-summary` dispatch read-only or generator roles, and `ship`'s "docs only" match is a push-scope rule with no agent behind it.
- Not captured: a user correction rejecting a defensive `git stash create` snapshot before a bulk edit ("no need to stash, just do on top what's already built"). That is a working-style preference with no skill-trigger implication — routed to `update-claude-docs`, per this skill's own scope test.
- **`task-summary` mandated overwriting the exact sections its own ownership guard forbids touching** (issue #13). §4 marks a full `## Quick Start` rewrite and a `## Last Session` overwrite MANDATORY on every update; §1 says additive-only once another writer owns the doc. Both fire together on a contested `current.md`, leaving only bad moves — obey the mandate and delete a peer's bullets, or skip it silently. The reporter improvised a third path the skill never sanctions, which is the real cost: an unwritten rule gets re-invented differently every session. `## Last Session` was not actually silent — it already carried "Parallel sessions: overwrite only your own content" — but the clause trails the mandate mid-row, so a reader executing the instruction has already acted before reaching it. A qualifier behind its mandate reads as a footnote; it has to interrupt. Quick Start was the true gap, with nothing at either of its two mandate sites.
- **The first fix was gated on a check that the reporter's own invocation never runs** — caught by the product reviewer, not the code reviewer, and worth recording as a review-lens result. All three new branches keyed off "§1's ownership guard fired", but §1 opens *"Before **scanning**: do you own these docs?"* and its only remedy was "do NOT run the multi-domain scan". The Input table's `Full path` row says merely "Use as-is" — so a session invoked with an explicit path (the issue's own repro, and what `write-summary`/`update-summary` always pass) never evaluates the condition. The branches were dead code on the most common entry point. §1 is now run-wide ("Before writing — scan or explicit path alike") and names the contested state the §4 branches refer to. **A conditional whose condition no step computes reads as enforced and resolves to its permissive default** — the same shape as 1.127.0's unmeasured floor gate, 3rd recurrence.
- A file-scoped code review cannot catch this: the three changed lines are internally consistent, and the reviewer correctly confirmed so. The defect lived in the reachability path *between* §1 and §4 — exactly the ordering-between-steps class `/done`'s prose-vs-executable gate was widened for in 1.127.0.
- **Extracted to `skills/_shared/references/contested-doc-sections.md` after the sibling sweep found the same contradiction in two more skills** — `condense-task-doc` L66/L70 and `merge-task-docs` L84/L143 carry identical unguarded Quick Start / Last Session overwrite mandates. Three owners crosses CLAUDE.md's "3+ skills duplicate a rule verbatim → extract" threshold, reversing this run's earlier single-owner call (the initial reasoning — that `diff-ownership.md` answers *which files are mine* while this answers *which sections I may overwrite* — still holds, which is why this is a new file rather than an addition to that one). Detection stays in `diff-ownership.md`; the new file owns only what to do once a doc comes back contested.
- **Arrival-rate gate: net −1 rule across three files, one new reference.** All three skills held their line counts exactly (`task-summary` 218, `condense-task-doc` 112, `merge-task-docs` 147). Bytes vs HEAD: 27991 → 28588 (128.4 → 131.1 B/L), 19575 → 19753, 14642 → 14924; new shared file 19 lines / 2229 bytes. Extraction is what kept the growth to pointers — each skill states its branch in one clause and routes the reasoning out. ⚠️ Earlier bullets in this entry quoted a 28151 baseline for `task-summary`; that figure already included a prior session's uncommitted +160 and was wrong. `git show HEAD:<path>` is the baseline, per the file's own Size budget rule.
- **`update-plugin`'s shared-mechanism sweep existed and still didn't fire — its trigger was scoped to propagating a fix, not to placing a new rule.** L66 read "a **fix** to one skill's handling of a shared mechanism is a fix to all of them", so a session choosing *where a new rule lives* never matched it; the 3-owner situation behind this release's extraction surfaced only because a product reviewer volunteered it. Widened to cover placement, with the grep-pattern trap named: grep the MECHANISM's vocabulary (the words other skills already use), never the rule's own wording, which by construction exists only in the file being edited and returns a confident 1-owner count. **Arrival-rate gate: REPLACE** — 156 lines held, 18812 → 19004 bytes (120.6 → 121.8 B/L), old text out entirely rather than a second bullet beside it.
- Shared-mechanism grep ran on that patch: `grep -ln "_shared/references/<topic>\|canonical home\|extract to .*_shared" skills/*/SKILL.md` returned `update-plugin` and `notes-summary`; the latter is a false positive (its "canonical home" is a notes file's storage path, not rule placement). Sole owner, no sibling patch owed.
- `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` was found three versions behind `plugin.json` (1.127.0 vs 1.130.0) — the silent mismatch CLAUDE.md's Version Bumping section warns about, present at HEAD, unrelated to issue #13. Number corrected; no automated guard added (deliberate scope call, not an oversight).

## 1.129.0

- **Writing a CORRECTION into a doc had no verification step, and it is the costliest kind of entry to get wrong.** `update-claude-docs` treats every signal as something to capture; nothing distinguished "here is a new fact" from "the existing fact is wrong". This session two build agents reported a 48-test gate; the lead had been running four of the five suites, concluded both had miscounted, and wrote that into the **global** `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`, a project agent file, and a task doc. One command proved the agents right — the fifth suite runs offline — and three edits had to be reverted.
- The asymmetry is what hides it: writing "that figure is stale" feels like diligence, costs nothing visible, and produces a confident-looking rule that later sessions read as settled. A wrong correction also discredits a source that was right, so the next reader trusts the accurate doc less.
- `task-summary` shares the mechanism at its stale-gotcha sweep, which instructs flipping any claim asserting a live bug to "fixed" — same unverified flip, opposite direction. Both now require running the deciding command first, and `task-summary` additionally notes the flip goes both ways.
- **Arrival-rate gate: one REPLACE, one declared growth.** `task-summary` (128.3 B/L, over budget) took a 1-line-for-1-line fold into the existing sweep bullet — 218 lines held, no rule added. `update-claude-docs` (88.7 → 91.1 B/L) gained one Entry-style row; no retirement was available in that table, all four existing rows govern live behaviour, and the row belongs beside the capture filter it qualifies rather than in `references/`.
- Shared-mechanism grep ran: `grep -ln "stale\|contradicts\|drift" skills/*/SKILL.md` returned 14 files, too broad — narrowed to skills that *write* a correction rather than merely mention staleness. `task-summary` was the only sibling; `condense-*` and `merge-task-docs` delete or relocate content without asserting a prior claim was wrong.
- Not patched: `/done`'s Step 1, despite the signal surfacing there. Its "a finding is a claim, not a verdict" rule already covers reviewer findings, the file sits at 133 B/L with a retirement pass overdue since 1.127.0, and the defect belongs to the skill that owns doc writes.

## 1.128.0

- **`/done` Step 1 manufactures stale review findings by design, and the skill never said so.** The simplifier and the reviewer are spawned in the same batch over the same files — one edits while the other reads. This session the simplifier extracted a predicate into a model method and repointed its caller mid-flight; the reviewer, reading concurrently, filed the pre-extraction duplication as its top finding *and* called the fresh method "dead code". Both reports read as authoritative, and following the reviewer verbatim would have reverted a correct fix.
- Worse, the two disagreed on the **remedy**: the reviewer wanted the predicate derived from the state machine's transitions table; the simplifier had actually run it and found that table also admits states the predicate must reject. The rule now added — **the agent that executed code outranks the one that reasoned** — is the tiebreaker that was missing.
- Distinct from the global companion's "parallel reader/writer agents on shared state": that warns against *choosing* such a pairing, whereas `/done` prescribes it every run, so the lead needs the re-read instruction inline where findings are triaged.
- **Arrival-rate gate: REPLACE.** Folded into the existing "confirm the diagnosis" bullet rather than added beside it — the new rule is a workflow-generated instance of that same rule, and a second bullet would have competed with it. 219 lines held; bytes 28728 → 29160 (131.1 → 133.1 B/L). ⚠️ **Second consecutive byte-growth on `done` with no retirement found** — the retirement pass this file has been flagged for since 1.127.0 is now overdue, not merely available.
- Shared-mechanism grep ran: `grep -l "code-simplifier" skills/*/SKILL.md` → `done`, `agent-setup`, `update-claude-docs`. The latter two only *register* the agents (roster/colour table) or route a finding into the simplifier's preserve table; neither spawns the pair concurrently. `done` is the sole prescriber of the race — no sibling patch applies.

## 1.127.0

- **`/done`'s docs-only mode would have skipped the review that caught a 🔴 in a rule shipped minutes earlier.** The mode triggered on "`*.md` only, zero application code" and instructed "running them against markdown is theater" — correct for a README, wrong for this repo, where a `SKILL.md` is the executable artifact. The 1.126.0 diff (3 skill files + 2 manifests) was textbook docs-only; the user overrode it explicitly and all three agents returned confirmed findings, including a floor gate whose two inputs no step computed before the deciding step read them. The gate is now **prose-vs-executable, not `.md`-vs-code**: `skills/*/SKILL.md`, `commands/*.md`, `.claude/agents/*.md` and their `references/` take full mode, and the product reviewer especially — both file-scoped lenses read the same 3 files and missed a defect that lived in the ordering *between* steps, not in any line.
- The pre-existing timing exception ("did THIS SESSION produce code") was what suppressed this: it reads as satisfied whenever no application code exists at all, which is exactly when the new gate decides. Both now say to check the other — the same overlapping-wording failure D44 recorded.
- **Arrival-rate gate: REPLACE, with declared growth.** All three edits rewrote existing sentences (219 lines held), but bytes went 27999 → 28728 (127.8 → 131.1 B/L). No retirement was available: the one candidate examined, the `run_in_background: false` row, is a live guardrail — all four of this session's agents returned as notifications despite the flag. `done` remains the densest non-condense skill and the standing retirement-pass candidate.
- Shared-mechanism grep ran: `grep -rln "docs-only\|entirely documentation\|no code\b" skills/*/SKILL.md` → `done`, `task-summary`. `task-summary`'s hit is its file-discovery blind spot (`git diff --name-only` hiding staged files), a different mechanism that decides nothing about reviewability. `done` is the sole owner of this gate; no sibling patch applies.
- **Simplifier pass on 1.126.0's own new text**, 4 line-neutral cuts (−338 bytes): authoring rationale removed from `declared-budget.md` (it restated the table row's conditions), a re-enumeration of the reference's Detect table dropped from `update-claude-docs` Step 4's pointer, and both of `read-summary`'s new #11 cross-references tightened — one had stacked a second `⚠️` inside a rule that already opened with one.
- Also recorded: **D51** (the pruner-spawn floor for undersized files, issue #10's fix and D44's successor) and **D52** (an agent pass over the session's *own* fresh fixes inverts D47's findings-vs-clean reliability prior, which stays scoped to definition-handed audits).

## 1.126.0

- **`/done` Step 1 required confirming a reviewer's DIAGNOSIS but never verifying its REMEDY.** The bullet said "confirm each against the actual code before fixing" — so the finding got checked, the suggested one-liner got applied, and the run moved on without re-running the thing that was failing. A real run applied `TestWidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized()` to fix a genuine "Binding has not yet been initialized" crash; the diagnosis was correct and the fix did cure that crash, but the binding also makes Flutter stub out networking and return 400 for every HTTP request, so a live integration suite went from 2 failures to 3 — and the new failure looked like a server bug, not like the fix. It was caught only because the suite happened to be re-run out of habit. The bullet now states the full loop (confirm → fix → **re-run → count**) and that a correct diagnosis licenses nothing about the remedy.
- **Arrival-rate gate: REPLACE.** `skills/done/SKILL.md` was already dense (126.1 B/L) and the fix extends the existing reviewer-findings bullet rather than adding a rule — line count held at 219, ratio 126.1 → 127.8. No retirement was needed because nothing was added.
- Shared-mechanism grep ran: `grep -l "reviewer found\|finding is a claim\|code-reviewer" skills/*/SKILL.md` → `done`, `agent-setup`, `update-claude-docs`. Only `done` acts on reviewer findings; the other two hits route false positives into agent files, a separate concern. No sibling patch applies.
- **`update-claude-docs` Step 4 mandated a pruner spawn with no size floor** ([#10](https://github.com/syafiq555/syafiqkit/issues/10)). D44 taught the step to skip when the owner had *decided* against pruning, but a file with no decision can still be trivially small: a 26-line project CLAUDE.md — 7% of the 350 default — met every condition for a spawn that could only return a no-op, and the reporter declined it. `_shared/references/declared-budget.md` gained an Act row for the undersized case (under ~half the applicable budget **and** ≤5 net lines added this session); Step 4's spawn row is now gated on it. Expressed as a **ratio of whatever budget applies**, so it introduces no new absolute number — `CLAUDE.md` L152 reserves line counts to the two condense skills — and one rule covers a declared 460 and the 350 default alike. Both figures are ones the caller already computes (`wc -l`, `git diff --stat`). Per D44's rejected-options log the rule lives in the shared reference only; verified one copy (`grep -rln "half its applicable budget" skills/` → 1 file), with all 6 citing sites inheriting it.
- **`read-summary` L40 and L77 gave opposing instructions for the same moment, 37 lines apart, with no cross-reference** ([#11](https://github.com/syafiq555/syafiqkit/issues/11)). L40 says route doc drift in the same turn; L77 says gate a mid-investigation finding behind `AskUserQuestion`. Doc drift found during an investigation satisfies both, and the reported run closed with a prose offer — the one response *both* rules independently forbid. It went unanswered and survived only because `/done` happened to reach the same doc later. Reconciled by finding KIND: a doc contradicting the code is **maintenance** and routes now; a defect in the **code** is unscoped work and goes through `AskUserQuestion`. Both sites gained a one-line pointer to the other's case.
- **Arrival-rate gate: REPLACE, net negative.** `read-summary/SKILL.md` was 2.2× over the ~90 B/L gate and already extracted once (D50), so both cross-refs were paid for by cutting real duplication — the `Explore` example's inline `rg`/return-raw warning (already in `explore-delegation.md:10-11`, which L17 routes to) and a claim-shift sentence restated more fully at L29-32. Net **−46 bytes**, 88 lines held. ⚠️ Deliberately *not* paid for by cutting L40's "an offer parked on the user's reply is not routing… the finding dies" clause: `doc-authority.md` is a declared cold path, so moving the rule's motivation there is how the reported failure recurs. `declared-budget.md` 79.0 → 87.5 B/L (a first pass landed at 94.0 and was re-cut — its prose restated the table row's own conditions); `update-claude-docs/SKILL.md` +17 bytes, no new lines.

## 1.125.0

- **`/ship`'s completeness check could only validate the note against the CHANGELOG — never the CHANGELOG against what shipped.** Step 5.4 prescribed counting the entry's items against the output bullets; a real ship counted 11 vs 11 and passed while three user-facing changes (a mail Reply-To, per-status tooltips on a locked control, a status relabel on a second surface) had shipped uncredited, because the changelog itself was short. The user caught it by asking "is this what we deployed?" — every self-check inside the skill said green. Step 5.4 now requires reconciling the entry against the deployed diff before formatting, and names two cheap cross-checks: a commit subject claiming a count against the entry's actual count, and commit bodies naming changes the entry never mentions. **Arrival-rate gate: REPLACE** — the "count against whatever structure" tail was redundant with the sentence preceding it and was compressed to make room.
- **Neither `/ship` nor `gchat-format` said how a release note should be SHAPED, only what to cut.** `/ship` 5.3 covered tone (lead with the win, don't bury it under caveats) and `gchat-format` covered trimming, so a note could satisfy both and still be 14 peer bullets ordered `Added` then `Fixed` — which splits one feature across two headings and gives the headline feature the same weight as a tooltip fix. A technical reader could not tell what the release was, and said so. Both skills now require regrouping by feature: a plain-language lead naming the before-state (which the changelog rarely carries — it lives in the task doc), the main feature's items together regardless of source heading, unrelated work demoted to a catch-all, and feature-repairing bugs folded under the feature rather than billed beside it.
- **Recovered bytes in the same edit**: `/ship` Step 5's scope callout carried a quoted user anecdote (flagged by Step 3a's own checklist as belonging in the CHANGELOG, not the skill body) and was tightened. It now also states that the last-*deployed* version is not always the previous commit — this run's prior deploy had FAILED, so the naive baseline would have mis-scoped the note.
- **Arrival-rate gate, declared honestly**: `skills/ship/SKILL.md` was above budget before this run (114.8 B/L) and grew to 122.3 despite both changes being replacements and one retirement. No further retirement was available in Step 5 — the remaining rules each guard a distinct failure with no live overlap. Flagging rather than burying: `ship` is now the densest non-condense skill and is the next retirement-pass candidate. `gchat-format` (45.2 → 49.5 B/L) is well under budget; its addition needed no offset.
- **Version fields were already out of sync before this run** — `plugin.json` at 1.124.2, `marketplace.json` still at 1.124.1. Both bumped to 1.125.0; the secondary-field drift is exactly what `/ship`'s version-bump gate exists to catch.
- Shared-mechanism grep ran: `grep -l "release note" skills/*/SKILL.md` → `ship` + `gchat-format`, both patched. `grep -l CHANGELOG skills/*/SKILL.md` → also `commit` and `done`, neither of which generates or validates a release note, so no sibling patch applies.

## 1.124.2

- **`agent-setup`'s model-override rule covered the file and not the caller.** The template-drift row already governed an in-file `model:` on a generated agent, but nothing covered the mirror at dispatch: passing `model:` alongside a registered `subagent_type` silently overrides the tier that agent's frontmatter sets, with no error and nothing for a drift check to find. Triggered by a session asking to re-run `browser-verifier` "with opus" — the agent pins no `model:`, so it already inherited the session's Opus and the override would have changed nothing, which is the case most likely to be mistaken for a real A/B. **Arrival-rate gate: this was a REPLACE** — the existing drift row was extended in place, no new row added to a file at 117 B/L.
- Shared-mechanism grep ran across all 5 skills naming `subagent_type`; none prescribes a `model:` alongside a registered type, so `agent-setup` remains the single home. No sibling patch needed.
- **Signal examined and deliberately NOT patched**: `/done` Step 1's one-message batch rule was violated this session (three agents emitted across three messages, reading each result before spawning the next). The rule is already stated three times in `done/SKILL.md` — the NEVER row, Step 1's opening, and the emission block, which names this exact "sequential-by-drift" failure and prescribes the recovery that was used. At 126 B/L a fourth restatement would violate the arrival-rate gate and, per the skill's own escalation guidance, make the rule less likely followed rather than more. Recorded as a model-side lapse, not a skill defect.

## 1.124.1

- **1.124.0's arrival-rate gate shipped unenforced — a product review caught it reproducing the exact weakness it was built to fix.** Step 3a stated the replace-or-route rule, but Step 4 (Validate) had no row checking it ran, making the gate prose a session is trusted to remember. That is structurally identical to how D23's capture regressed: D23 also "captured the pattern into `update-plugin`" and the files still regrew. Step 4 now requires, for any file touched that was already above ~90 B/L, naming which of the three the change was — a replace, a `references/` route, or a declared growth with the reason no retirement applied — and treats a purely additive CHANGELOG entry against a dense file as a **failed** check, not a pass. Replaces a self-diagnostic `Tell:` buried in the rule's own prose with a checklist item that fails.
- **Step 3a stated the ~90 bytes/line threshold with no way to compute it.** `wc -lc` returns raw bytes and lines, not a ratio, so applying the gate meant deriving the arithmetic — against this repo's own documented rule that an eyeballed ratio inverts the diagnosis. Added the one-liner (`for f in skills/*/SKILL.md; do ... bc; done | sort -rn`) beside the threshold.
- Density pass on `update-plugin` itself: stripped the worked-incident B/L figures from the arrival-rate callout (the 1.124.0 CHANGELOG entry already carries them verbatim) — 118.5 → 117.6 B/L, applying the skill's own "anecdote belongs in the CHANGELOG, not the skill body" checklist row to the rule that introduced it.
- Two findings recorded as open rather than fixed: the gate is reachable only from `update-plugin` while rules most often arrive as direct hand-edits mid-session (and `/done` Step 5 is conditional-by-default), and the new `references/*.md` files inherit no size policy of their own — `declared-budget.md` is CLAUDE.md-scoped. Both in `doc-condensation/current.md` Next Steps as the open half of D50.

## 1.124.0

- **Density sweep across all 24 skills found three files 2.3× over the ~80-90 bytes/line threshold — and it was D23 recurring on the same files.** `condense-claude-md` (207 B/L), `read-summary` (202) and `condense-task-doc` (179) topped the ranking; two of them were the exact files D23 hand-condensed in v1.62/1.63 at 147/140 B/L, meaning the in-place pass didn't hold and they regrew past their pre-fix state. None of the three had a `references/` dir, while every acceptable-density large skill (`task-summary`, `done`, `update-claude-docs`) does — so this pass extracted cold paths structurally instead of re-wording. `condense-claude-md`'s Restructuring #6/#7 split mechanics → `references/structural-splits.md` (22,349 → 14,094 bytes, −37%); `read-summary`'s staleness-handoff + authoritative-for blocks → `references/doc-authority.md` (22,418 → 17,659, −21%). Both SKILL.md keep the highest-cost fact inline above the pointer, per `condense-claude-md`'s own #6 rule.
- **Live bug: four skill files still prescribed `rg`, which the global CLAUDE.md declared abandoned on 2026-07-13** (its `-r` is `--replace`, not recursive — it silently substitutes the match and exits 0). `read-summary` L82 told the model to run `rg --files` for CLAUDE.md discovery while L20's own Explore prompt, 62 lines above, warned against `rg` — the skill contradicted itself. Also fixed in `condense-claude-md`'s seam-test, `update-claude-docs` L79 and its `references/structure.md` §1. All four now use `grep -rl`. Found by the shared-mechanism grep, not by the density scan.
- **The write-mode rule had three homes and the verify-baseline rule two.** `condense-claude-md` stated write-mode at Process #3, again in Hard rules, and again in `_shared/references/two-tier-condense.md`; `condense-task-doc` did the same at step 7 and Hard rules. Collapsed to the single canonical statement in the shared reference with the Process steps pointing at it — three homes is how one silently goes stale. New Step 3a checklist row names the pattern.
- **New arrival-rate gate in `update-plugin` Step 3a — the durable fix for what condensing alone can't hold.** Bytes/line measures how tightly rules are written and says nothing about how many arrive; a skill gaining ~1 rule per session and retiring none regrows regardless of wording quality (`done` was deliberately shrunk in 1.123.23 and is back to 126 B/L three versions later). A skill above ~90 B/L now gains a rule only by replacing one, routing it to `references/`, or stating in the report that it grew and why no retirement was available. Paired with an explicit retirement rule citing D33's precedent that absence of uptake is admissible evidence — with the caveat that a rule must be verified dead before cutting, since a tool that is still installed is a live guardrail whatever its own "abandoned" note says.
- Fixed a stale internal cross-reference in `condense-claude-md` Hard rules (`see Process #6` → `#5`; there is no #6).
- `read-summary`'s bytes/line barely moved (202 → 201) despite the −21% byte cut — which is the atomic-file gate's own predicted result and the evidence for the arrival-rate diagnosis: extraction removed whole lines, so bytes and lines fell together, and what remains is irreducibly one distinct rule per line. Further compression there would erode content, not bloat.

## 1.123.28

- **`condense-claude-md` offered only RELOCATION levers for "split", so an under-budget file would get a new file it didn't need.** The skill declares itself (L10) the single source of truth for "every split decision", but all six of its split mentions assume the destination is another file — subdir `CLAUDE.md` (#6), task doc, companion (#7) — and neither #6 nor #7 carries a threshold at its point of use. The in-file `### ` subsection split (group one oversized table into anchored subsections, every row staying inline) appeared nowhere. The rule did exist, but only in `update-claude-docs/references/structure.md` §6 — the wrong skill, since a session reading the declared source-of-truth for sizing gets the relocation levers and no in-file option. A user asking to split a 129-line file's gotchas table had to say "within the file" to steer away from the default. Added a budget gate above #6/#7 naming the in-place split as what an under-budget file gets, plus the axis to group on (where the reader is when the symptom hits, not which subsystem authored the row), and closed the second site at step 5's density callout, which routed a "still reads as a wall" verdict to seam-test/companion only.
- Shared-mechanism check ran across the three skills that own split decisions: **`condense-task-doc` does not have the defect** — every one of its split triggers is explicitly gated on `>300 lines`, so it never splits an under-budget doc. The bias was specific to the CLAUDE.md skill. Numbering of #6/#7 was deliberately left intact (the gate is unnumbered, above the list) rather than renumbered — eight internal cross-references across two files point at those numbers.

## 1.123.27

- **`done` had no mode for a session that changed a live system but not the repo.** A session provisioned a demo account on production entirely through `tinker` — both repos clean, no session commit, the only artifacts throwaway scripts in a scratchpad dir. The mode table offers docs-only / infra-only / full, all of which presuppose a repo diff, and docs-only's own exception only covers *committed* code. Step 1 was skipped by improvisation. New **ops-only mode**: skip the three code agents (an empty partition returns a meaningless green report), verify by reading the live system back instead, and treat Step 4 as the point of the run since a live-system change leaves no trace in `git log`. Added a companion rule that an empty `git status --short` does not by itself select a no-code mode — committed-this-session, another writer's tree, and genuinely-no-repo-work look identical and need opposite handling.
- **Fleet audit for the same empty-input shape found it in two more skills** (the audit row D44 left open; `done`/`ship` were already fixed). `sweep-doc-overlaps` Step 4 terminated on zero merge candidates with no control — and because Step 2 delegates every verdict to batch agents, any batch that mis-scoped its `ls` or returned an empty table contributes zeroes that compile into `"N pairs checked, correctly separated"`, a line the user reads as a verified all-clear. Now requires three checks before accepting zero: N reconciles with the Step 1 inventory, at least one batch returned a reasoned `keep separate`, and one pair is spot-checked inline (the zero-candidate path is otherwise the only exit where nobody opens a doc). Its sibling `merge-task-docs` has carried this control since 1.64.5; the discovery front-end deciding whether the merge runs at all never inherited it. `md-to-pdf` Step 1 branched on a bare `grep -c '```mermaid'` returning 0, which is identical for "no diagrams", an unreadable path (error goes to discarded stderr), and a file ugrep skipped as binary on one NUL byte — live for docs generated or concatenated by other tooling. Added a `grep -c ''` control line and the `grep -a` re-run; an unproven 0 ships a PDF with every diagram as a raw code fence and reports success.
- **`read-summary`'s investigation exit said "then ask" without saying HOW, and the default is prose.** Its Investigation-intent rule correctly stops an investigation from escalating into an unrequested plan ("report the finding and its blast radius, then ask whether they want a fix designed") but never named `AskUserQuestion` — so the run ended on "want me to patch these?", collapsing three separate forks (which findings to act on, fix-now vs. defer, where the record lands) into one yes/no. The user corrected it twice in the same session, the second time immediately after the first was applied. The exit now names the tool and its `Tell` covers a single prose offer, not just a numbered list of asks.
- **Delegating an AUDIT (not a gather) to `Explore` needed clauses the shared reference didn't carry.** `explore-delegation.md` was written for the gather case — "return raw, don't rank" — which says nothing about an agent handed a *defect definition*, where absence of matches reads as its own failure: 3 of 4 audit agents filed findings that failed re-verification, one quoting the very line that disproved its claim. Two clauses added to the shared file (so all four referencing skills inherit them): every finding must quote its line verbatim AND name the definition clause it satisfies; an unqualified "clean" is rejected in favour of enumerated scope. The reliability inverts from the intuitive expectation — enumerated clean verdicts held, findings did not.
- Audited the remaining 19 skills for both shapes and closed the second audit row too: **no skill outside `done` carries D24's self-caught-deviation blind spot.** It needs a *reporting* gate (a step deciding what to tell the user about problems); `update-plugin` is the only near-equivalent and already names deviation in its Step 1 signal table. Constraint tables (`brainstorming`'s HARD-GATE, `gchat-format`'s completeness check) are not the shape — they decide what to do next, not what to report. `condense-task-doc` was checked and is the best-guarded file in the set: step 6 already carries `"zero deletions means this step wasn't done"` verbatim.
- **`ship` Step 5's new tracker item was numbered `6b.`, which silently broke the list into three.** `6b.` is not a valid GFM ordered-list marker, so the line renders as a paragraph and *terminates* the list — items 7 and 8 restart at 1 and 2, and the sequence reads `1…6, para, 1, 2` instead of a continuous run. Renumbered to 7/8/9 (the only `6b.`-style marker in the plugin; both surviving `item 3` cross-references sit below the insertion point and stay valid).

## 1.123.26

- **`ship` Step 4 assumed one push → one CI run, so a second run's genuine failure went unnoticed and the user caught it from an email.** A Dourr ship pushed `master` and forward-merged `production` in quick succession, starting two GitHub Actions runs 6 seconds apart that contended for the same droplet's SSH. The production run was polled by id and succeeded; the staging run failed all 3 SSH retries and stayed failed, leaving staging a commit behind master while the ship reported green. Step 4's language was singular throughout and its snippet was literally `gh run list --limit 1`. Now `--limit 5` with `workflowName,attempt`, plus a rule to re-list at the end and assert zero unresolved failures across all runs, and a note that a retried run reuses its id — so a stale failure notification and a currently-green run can describe the same id, and neither the email nor the id settles it (`attempt` + `conclusion` do).
- **`ship` had no rule against hand-fixing a tracked file on the deploy target.** Mid-ship, a prod `docker-compose.prod.yml` was edited server-side to unblock a worker; that repo's deploy runs `git reset --hard`, so the edit was destined to be discarded silently on the next deploy. The user caught it ("why not update locally too? that one is also in the repo"). Step 4 now requires reading how the deploy updates its checkout before editing anything there, and routes tracked-file fixes through git — only gitignored env files legitimately live server-side.
- **`ship` never told anyone about the ship.** It generates a release note and stops; the user had to ask for the tracker update afterwards. No skill in the plugin mentioned issue trackers at all (verified by grep). Step 5 gains 6b: if the project documents a tracker, post there in the same run, searching for an existing entry before creating one, with one entry per repo on a multi-repo ship.

## 1.123.25

- **`update-claude-docs`'s third structural lever (manual companion file) had no size gate — reachable from a bare "split by category" request even when the file was nowhere near budget.** Asked to split a 216-line `backend/CLAUDE.md`'s 79-row gotchas table "by category," the skill jumped straight to extracting 4 new `.claude-companions/shared/*.md` files, when the file was well under the 200/350-line budget and every row was uniformly high-frequency Laravel content — no oversized-file problem existed to justify the lever. The user corrected: "no need to put in a companion file for the ones that's always needed... just in the same file but differentiated by category." `references/structure.md` §6 now gates the three structural levers behind an explicit budget check — under budget gets in-place `### ` subsection headers with every row staying inline, no new file; the companion lever is reserved for a block that's both over budget AND genuinely low-frequency.

## 1.123.24

- **`condense-claude-md`'s companion split (#7) assumed one grab-bag file — a heterogeneous gotchas table split this way just recreates the dense wall one file over.** Asked to shrink a 285-line `backend/CLAUDE.md` whose ~155-row table spanned Tenancy, OAuth/MyDigital ID, SMS/OTP, Trust/Bricks, PDF-stamping, and blog-sync bugs, the first pass trimmed row prose (56KB → 47KB) and, on request for more, dumped everything into a single `CLAUDE-backend-gotchas.md` — same problem, new address. The user caught it twice: a minimap screenshot showing the file still read as one unbroken block despite the byte win, then "not all need to be in a single file." Restructuring #7 now says explicitly: cluster moved rows by topic FIRST, write one narrow companion per cluster (or route to an existing domain subdir CLAUDE.md when one already owns that topic), and a byte/line drop is not the completion signal — the pointer index has to let a reader pick the ONE file that answers their symptom without opening a file still bundling five topics.
- Same gap in Process #5's exit criteria: it checked bytes and cell length, never whether a still-single oversized table just got tighter versions of the same rows. New line: byte reduction from row-trimming alone doesn't prove the file stopped reading as one dense block — re-run the atomic-file gate's multi-topic question before declaring a large table's condense done.
- Final split (for reference, not itself a rule): 64 of 141 rows moved to 4 new topic companions (`CLAUDE-oauth-gotchas.md`, `CLAUDE-sms-otp-gotchas.md`, `CLAUDE-blog-guide-sync-gotchas.md`, `CLAUDE-backend-misc-gotchas.md`) + 2 clusters routed to existing domain files (`Tenancy/CLAUDE.md`, `Trust/CLAUDE.md`) instead of a companion, since those already auto-load in scope. `backend/CLAUDE.md` landed at 32,799 bytes (−42% from 56,322), each companion independently under 6KB.
- Checked `read-summary` and `update-claude-docs`, the two siblings that also reference companion files (shared-mechanism grep) — both already describe *consuming* a pointer generically ("a" companion file, not "the" one file) and needed no change.

## 1.123.23

- **`/done`'s parallel-agent rule was violated by a session that had just read all three of its warnings — so the fix is a message SHAPE, not a fourth warning.** The run globbed 3 project agents, correctly computed "1 per role, 3 total", stated it, then emitted one `Agent` call and ended the message; the remaining two roles went out serially in later messages. Step 1's own text predicted this verbatim ("Stating the count correctly and then emitting one call anyway passes no gate and raises no error"), which is the point: the diagnosis was already perfect and prose still didn't enforce it. Step 1's callout now prescribes the mechanism — **the spawning message opens with the first `Agent` call and carries no prose before the calls**, since narration is what ends a message after call #1 — plus the recovery (next message is calls-only for the missing roles; a serialised batch still completes, an abandoned one doesn't). The two upstream mentions were rewritten as pointers to it rather than restatements.
- Same mechanism added to `_shared/references/explore-delegation.md`, the shared home for agent-batching rules — the shared-mechanism grep found both carriers, so both were patched rather than only the skill in use.
- Net effect on density: `done/SKILL.md` **shrank** 25,816 → 25,787 bytes (126 → 122 bytes/line) while gaining the mechanism, by replacing three overlapping prose blocks instead of appending — the escalate-by-sharpness-not-length rule applied to itself. Partly addresses the "not condensed" note left in 1.123.22.
- Skipped as non-signals: two behavioural misses this session (closing with an inline question instead of `AskUserQuestion`; offering a design choice derivable from the system's own data) — both working-style, routed to `update-claude-docs` per this skill's own boundary. `browser-verifier`'s opt-in rule held again: proposed, waited, never auto-spawned.

## 1.123.22

- **A file partition is also a BLIND SPOT, and `/done` never said so.** Its partition guidance covered ownership (don't touch another writer's files) and review coverage (disjoint slices), but nothing warned that no agent can see a producer/consumer seam crossing its boundary. This run proved the cost: a backend agent added a `warning` key to an HTTP response, the typed frontend client didn't declare it, so the key was silently dropped and the "failure renders as success" bug the key existed to fix reappeared one hop later — `tsc` green, two clean agent reports. New rule at the top of `/done` Step 1's After-all-complete list: trace every value introduced to a *consuming* site, not just its emitter.
- **"Mirror `<file>`" instructs an agent to copy that file's DEFECTS with equal fidelity.** Told to mirror a sibling hook for a convention, a build agent reproduced its broken `localStorage.getItem('AGENCY_ID')` (the real key is kebab-case) — a faithful copy that reads as correct work and passes every gate. Worse, the project's own CLAUDE.md already named that file as the offender not to copy. `task-builder.template.md` + both generated copies now require resolving a named file's convention against its documented source before copying, and saying so when the two disagree.
- **Closed the two pointers 1.123.21 left owed.** `_shared/references/diff-ownership.md` existed but nothing pointed at it — both `done/SKILL.md` (Ownership guard) and `task-summary/SKILL.md` (§1) were contested that run, so the pointers were deferred to a report and never applied. Both files were free this run; both now carry the classify-by-diff-content rule inline.
- Skipped as non-signals: the react-hook-form `onInvalid` positional-arg trap (project-specific — routed to the project's `CLAUDE-gotchas.md` and `code-simplifier.md`), and `browser-verifier`'s opt-in rule (already stated correctly at `done/SKILL.md:74`, and followed — proposed and waited).
- Not condensed: `done/SKILL.md` sits at 124 bytes/line, above the ~90 flag, but its lines are distinct load-bearing traps rather than the Step 3a bloat patterns — a real condense pass there is its own change, not a mid-`/done` aside.

## 1.123.21

- **The ownership guard in `done`/`task-summary` tells you to establish which files are yours but names no METHOD — and the obvious one is wrong under this harness.** Edits are auto-staged, so your own writes land `M ` (staged) identically to another writer's pre-existing staged work; classifying by status plane marked 14 of this session's own files as "not mine" and would have scoped every downstream step to a single file. Every step still reports success — agents review an empty slice, doc skills skip the docs you changed. New `_shared/references/diff-ownership.md`: classify by diff CONTENT (`git diff --cached -- <f> | grep <a marker only this session wrote>`), plus the both-planes-true case (contested *and* yours) and the ban on repo-wide `stash`/`checkout -- .`/`reset`/`clean`/`restore`.
- **Shared-mechanism grep returned 3 skills; reading them cut it to 2.** `read-summary`'s "ownership" hit means *which doc owns a topic*, not which writer owns a file — the same token doing two unrelated jobs, and a keyword match on it is not a match on the concept. The two genuine carriers (`done`, `task-summary`) are both contested by another writer's uncommitted work this run, so the rule went to `_shared/` (its canonical home regardless) and the two pointers are **owed**: `done/SKILL.md:40` (Ownership guard) and `task-summary/SKILL.md:60` (§1 "do you own these docs?").
- **`update-plugin` had zero guidance for patching a CONTESTED target** — grep for contested/another-writer/ownership in its SKILL.md returned nothing, so the only paths were clobber it or drop the finding. Step 2 now routes: shared rule → `_shared/` + pointers from free callers; single-file rule → name the exact file + anchor line in the report. A finding parked in conversation is a finding lost.
- Skipped as non-signals: `/done` needing no change to route the pruner (it already delegates via `update-claude-docs` Step 4 — correct as written), and this run's Sonnet-over-Haiku tier choice for dense-prose architecture research (a judgment call the user didn't correct; global CLAUDE.md already covers Haiku tiers).
- Flagged, not fixed: `update-plugin/SKILL.md` is at 111 bytes/line, above the ~80-90 density threshold — pre-existing, and a real condense pass is its own change rather than a mid-`/done` aside.

## 1.123.20

- **`claude-md-pruner` now prunes task docs as well as CLAUDE.md, branching on artifact type** (ADR **D43**, `tasks/plugin-maintenance/agent-architecture/decisions/injection-and-delegation.md`). Task docs had no pruner at all — `condense-task-doc` does row-pruning inline during a condense pass, but nothing carried `memory: project` or background-spawn capability for task-doc *staleness*, and neither condense skill does liveness verification (both assume content is live and only judge shape). New Step 0.5 detects the artifact; Bootstrap, the classify table (§2a/§2b), step 3's grep targets, the NEVER-remove list and the sizing delegate target (`condense-claude-md` vs `condense-task-doc`) each fork from there.
- **This extends D29, it does not reverse it.** D29 rejected *dissolving* the agent into `condense-claude-md` because its grep-verify-against-the-codebase checks have no equivalent there — reasoning that is untouched and is exactly why the agent survives to gain a second artifact. Research confirmed no consolidation was available: only one pruner agent ever existed, so this is one agent widening, not two merging.
- **The name stays `claude-md-pruner`, deliberately.** A rename breaks two things silently: `update-claude-docs:152` Globs the literal filename and `:157` spawns by literal `subagent_type`, and a Glob miss falls through to "skip pruning" with **no error**; and `agent-setup`'s missing-agent check runs templates→agents only (no `comm -13` orphan direction), so every existing project would hold both files, one permanently dead. The `description:` carries the real trigger surface and names both artifacts. Recorded as a knowing exception in `CLAUDE.md` § Conventions so a future reader doesn't "fix" it — the `comm -13` orphan check remains a real gap for any future rename, deferred only because this change avoids one.
- **Task-doc cut/keep rules are pointed at, never copied.** `condense-task-doc` stays their single owner per `CLAUDE.md` § Conventions; the agent's §2b table routes to it rather than restating it, and that skill now declares its ownership explicitly (mirroring the sentence `condense-claude-md` gained in 1.123.19). Verified by grepping three distinctive phrases from `condense-task-doc` against both pruner copies — 0 hits each.
- **The highest-risk difference is structural, not stylistic**, and is called out as such in both copies: the CLAUDE.md branch deletes sections freely, while task docs require `Task Status`/`Bugs Fixed`/`Critical Gotchas`/`Next Steps` headings to survive emptying and MADR `Rejected` fields to be untouched. Every check in either skill detects *excess*, so a wrongly-deleted heading is invisible afterward — on a split doc it silently stops the index showing open work. The pre-existing `:61` TODO-routing row (which routes content *into* a task doc) is scoped to the CLAUDE.md branch, being a no-op on the other.
- **`agent-setup` carve-out removed, not just annotated**: `claude-md-pruner` was explicitly excepted from the "every task-doc-consuming agent carries `Skill` + names `/read-summary`" rule at two sites (L161 rule, L179 checklist). It consumes task docs now, so it belongs inside that rule — its Bootstrap's task-doc row invokes `Skill(read-summary)` rather than hand-rolling a Glob sweep. Three registry surfaces and the "why 8 agents" prose updated alongside.
- **Second on-sight migration item** added to `agent-setup` Step 5 for projects carrying a CLAUDE.md-only pruner, flagged as template drift rather than a project-specific fill (the same trap the 1.123.19 item names). Detector `grep -c "0.5 Detect the artifact"` verified in **both** directions — 0 on the pre-change fixture, 1 on both migrated files. A detector that fires on correctly-migrated files churns every project it touches; the checklist item now says so.

## 1.123.19

- **`update-claude-docs` Step 4 spawned `claude-md-pruner` unconditionally, re-opening a pruning question the target project had explicitly closed** ([#9](https://github.com/syafiq555/syafiqkit/issues/9), reported by @munajaf against an installed 1.123.1 copy). The file in question records the owner's decision in its own header — line budget ~460 not the global 350, 13 consecutive passes confirming every gotcha row is load-bearing, per-stack splitting evaluated and declined, "don't re-open either question" — and Step 4's table had exactly two rows: agent found → launch, not found → skip. The reporter deviated from the step twice in one session to behave correctly, which is the signal that a step is missing a case rather than that the operator was lazy. Added the third row: agent found **but the file records pruning/splitting as decided** → skip the spawn, report size against the file's own budget.
- **The near-miss is why this survived.** Step 4 already carried ⚠️ "only background-prune files that are SETTLED", which scopes to *timing within a session* (don't prune a file you're still editing) — so it reads as satisfied while the permanently-closed case goes unhandled. A warning whose wording overlaps an uncovered case actively suppresses the fix. Sharpened its opening to name the axis it does NOT cover.
- **One root cause, four sites — the issue named two.** The defect is an authority inversion: these skills assert a global default as if they own a file's size policy. Fixing only the caller leaves a *directly-invoked* pruner or condenser still re-litigating, so the rule went to `_shared/references/declared-budget.md` (detection signals, act table, fallbacks) with one-line pointers from `update-claude-docs` Steps 4+5, `claude-md-pruner` (agent **and** template, parity rule), `condense-claude-md` step 4, and `structure.md` §6. Four independent copies of "350 is the ceiling" is why the bug existed in four places; four independent copies of the fix would rebuild the same drift surface.
- **`condense-claude-md` needed more than a threshold swap** — the reporter's file declined the *split*, which is that skill's step-4 offer, not just its number. A declared decision suppresses the offer, not only the count.
- **Guarded the mirror failure**: a declared budget suppresses the DECISION, not the MEASUREMENT. `wc -lc` still runs, the number is still reported, and an overage *against the declared figure* is still flagged — a file 200 lines past its own stated 460 is over budget and must be told so. Also noted a budget declared *below* the default is equally authoritative; the signal is "the owner decided", not "the owner wants more room".
- Resynced the two version files, which had drifted apart on disk (`plugin.json` 1.123.18 vs `marketplace.json` 1.123.17) — the exact silent mismatch `CLAUDE.md` § Version Bumping warns about.

### Follow-on: one owner for size policy

- **`claude-md-pruner` no longer carries a size policy at all.** #9's fix taught the agent to defer to a declared budget, which was correct but still left a *second implementation* of the thresholds. Collapsed step 0 to `### 0. Size policy is NOT this agent's — delegate it`: `condense-claude-md` owns every number, budget, and split decision; the agent keeps only staleness/duplication verified against the live repo, which the skill genuinely does not do. Its `~200`/`350` figures are gone entirely (`grep "200 lines\|350"` on both pruner files returns nothing).
- **The agent survives because a skill cannot carry `memory: project` or be spawned in the background** — that, not a threshold, is what earns it a file. Rewrote the Philosophy paragraph's "distinct jobs / distinct lanes" framing, which had been the licence for the duplication.
- **The task-doc side already worked this way** — `task-summary` L20/L52 both say "delegate to `condense-task-doc` rather than hand-rolling." CLAUDE.md was the lone outlier, so this is bringing one file into an existing pattern rather than a new architecture. Recorded the pattern as a `CLAUDE.md` § Conventions row so it stops eroding: shrinking a CLAUDE.md → `condense-claude-md`, a task doc → `condense-task-doc`, callers may name a number only as a trigger to delegate. `merge-task-docs` still enforces its own 300-line budget — deliberately left, since it judges a hypothetical merged doc that doesn't exist on disk yet.
- **Migration is on-sight via `agent-setup` Step 5**, since most projects already carry the old agent. The note flags it as **template drift, not a project-specific fill** — the hardcoded number looks locally tuned, so L194's "project-specific fills are NOT drift" carve-out would otherwise read as covering it and leave the file alone. Detector: `grep -n "Target length\|outer ceiling"`, verified to hit an unmigrated fixture (2) and miss both migrated files (0/0) — a detector that fires on correctly-migrated files would churn every project it touched.
- Fixed a stale pointer the detector caught: both Philosophy paragraphs still read "Target length: Process step 0" after step 0 stopped setting one. `/done` needed no change — it invokes `update-claude-docs`, and Step 4 there is what routes to the pruner.

## 1.123.18

- **`task-summary` documented one repo of a two-repo feature and reported done — a SIBLING checkout's `tasks/` tree is unreachable from every scan the skill prescribes.** A cross-repo batch shipped code to both repos in one session; the doc write touched five files in the originating repo and zero in the sibling, leaving the sibling's `current.md` four days stale with a Quick Start claiming work was awaiting a push that had since been superseded. Nothing failed: the docs that WERE written were complete and correct, which is what makes this silent. Root cause is a topology gap, not carelessness — §1's `git status --short` carried the parenthetical "(every repo in a multi-repo project)", but every sibling skill (`commit`, `ship`, `done`) defines multi-repo as NESTED sub-repos (`git -C <subdir>`, "scan immediate subdirectories"), so the phrase reads as "check your subdirs" and two independent sibling checkouts match none of it. Added an explicit sibling-repo rule to the Multi-Domain Scan naming the topology, the silence (`the doc you did write is complete`), and the tell (files committed in two repos, task doc opened in one).
- **The read side already solved this; only the write side was missing it.** `read-summary` has a dedicated SIBLING REPO step (Read Order 6) plus a `Related:` trigger for "a second repo enters", and even names the mechanism — "the harness only walks the tree from the working dir". So the plugin reliably READ both repos and reliably WROTE one. New rule points at that step rather than restating its reasoning.
- **Shared-mechanism sweep**: `grep -rln "multi-repo\|cross-repo\|sub-repo\|sibling repo" skills/*/SKILL.md` returned 9 skills. `commit`'s cross-doc status mirror sweep had the identical single-repo scope — it greps the feature keyword across `tasks/**/*.md` from one root, which is exactly the gate that should have caught the stale sibling item and structurally could not. Patched with the same scope fix. `done` delegates its doc step to `task-summary` and inherits the fix; `ship` delegates to `commit`; the rest describe the nested topology correctly and were left alone.
- Also scoped §6's back-reference grep to every repo the feature spans (a cross-repo feature's stalest mirror routinely lives in the *other* tree — here, an item asserting a UI affordance was missing that the same batch had just added), and made Validate item 6 a countable check: repos touched vs. repos whose docs you opened.
