- **Density pass**: 128 B/line on `task-summary` — trimmed a restating clause from the new rule; no stacked ⚠️ re-justification introduced (the four repo mentions each do distinct work: pointer, rule, validation, grep scope).

## 1.123.17

- **`## Next Steps` by-kind grouping promoted from permitted-when-long to REQUIRED on sight, and moved into the template where docs are actually copied from.** 1.123.16 reframed the anti-grouping ban onto the temporal axis but left the by-kind axis conditional ("once a list is long enough that its top-priority item no longer reads first") and stated only as prose in `task-summary`'s update table — meanwhile both `templates.md` skeletons still showed a bare `- [ ] [Pending work item]`, so every doc created from the template started flat by construction. Added the canonical group vocabulary (blocking / broken-recovery / missing-signal / blocked-on-external / discoverability / polish / deferred) plus a copyable skeleton to `templates.md`, and wired it into both template bodies. Headings are renameable to fit the domain; the axis is not.
- **Enforcement needed a gate, not just a rule.** A stated-once rule is skippable — the same failure §5.8 already documents for the deploy-word greps. Added `## Next Steps` grouping as its own step 3 in the update path's MANDATORY gap-check (step 2 compares table columns and is structurally blind to a flat checklist) and as a Validate item, both worded to fire at any length.
- **Shared-mechanism sweep**: `grep -rln "Next Steps" skills/` found two other writers. `merge-task-docs` concatenates two docs' lists into one longer flat list — patched with the specific trap that grouping the result by SOURCE DOC is the temporal axis wearing a different hat. `condense-task-doc` gained a regroup-on-sight note on its `## Next Steps` row, a condense pass being exactly when a list has outgrown readability. All three callers point at `templates.md` rather than restating the vocabulary — verified one home, zero duplication.

## 1.123.16

- **`git show HEAD:<path> | wc -c` is prescribed as the size baseline in two skills, and it is wrong on an already-dirty doc — the canonical fix existed but neither caller cited it.** A `task-summary` rewrite measured 40,090→25,283 bytes and would have reported −37%; the working tree already held a 25,257-byte version condensed by a prior uncommitted session, so the real delta was byte-flat and the "reduction" was another writer's work. `_shared/references/two-tier-condense.md` L25 already documents this exact trap ("a dirty file makes HEAD the wrong baseline") — but `task-summary` Size budget and `condense-task-doc` step 2 both hand you the trap-laden command with no caveat and no pointer. Patched both callers to gate the command on a clean start, name `wc -c`-before-first-write / `git show :<path>` as the alternatives, and cite the reference rather than restate it. Caught here only because the pre-edit number happened to be on hand; nothing in the workflow required measuring first.
- **`## Next Steps`' anti-grouping rule banned an axis but read as banning all structure.** The rule forbids sub-headings ("**this session's findings:**", "**earlier:**") because "grouping drifts it into a changelog", while the same cell permits "order by priority if needed" — leaving a 44-item list with its 🔴 at position 18 and no stated way to fix that. Reframed the ban onto the temporal axis explicitly: grouping by KIND of work (blocking / missing-signal / blocked-on-external / polish / deferred) is correct once the top-priority item no longer reads first, with the durability test — would this heading still be true in three sessions — as the discriminator.

## 1.123.15

- **The growth-rate diagnosis fixed in 1.123.14 for `condense-claude-md` was never ported to `condense-task-doc`, and recurred verbatim.** A 44KB task-doc index condensed to 39KB and reported done; the user replied "39kb, 1 doc update would pass the limit already" — the same sentence shape as last release's "37kb would fill up in 1/2 update". Per-section measurement showed ~29KB of 39KB sat in four append-only blocks (Bugs Fixed, Next Steps, Cross-Cutting Notes, LLM-CONTEXT teasers) that gain a row per session and lose none, while every ADR was already correctly split into `decisions/*.md`. Added a step-2 branch: when a doc is ALREADY split and the index is still oversized, measure per section, then route each append-only ROW to the theme file owning its mechanism (the row's own "see ADR-N" column usually names it) rather than compressing — with an explicit ban on generic `bugs.md`/`misc.md` catch-alls, which are second indexes waiting to bloat.
- **Three files encoded "doc-wide operational tables stay in the index" with no size ceiling.** `templates.md` L136 is canonical, restated in `condense-task-doc` and `merge-task-docs`. The rule is right (dropping those tables silently stops the index showing open work) but unbounded, which is what let them reach 24KB. Patched all three with the ceiling; `merge-task-docs` gets the sharper framing since merging two mature docs doubles those tables at a stroke.
- **`condense-task-doc` was the only skill in its family with no `AskUserQuestion` rule** (7 siblings have one), so a preserve-vs-delete fork and a target-structure fork were both asked as inline prose; the user replied "use ask question tool". Added a Hard rule matching `merge-task-docs` Step 2's fork convention, plus the note that an answer naming a constraint on HOW ("not a generic name") governs the whole run, not just the option it rode in on.
- **Write-vs-`Edit` mode was an absolute rule that inverted on dense files.** "Never use `Edit` for a full condensation" assumes the doc is mostly cruft; on a doc set that survives ~96% it means re-emitting a full file to change a tenth of it. Made the mode conditional in `condense-task-doc`, `condense-claude-md` and `_shared/references/two-tier-condense.md` (which deferred to the rule being replaced), with the self-check that a `Write` rewrite landing under ~15% byte delta was an `Edit` job.
- **Density pass**: abstracted a `## Files` rule in `condense-task-doc` that was over-fitted to one project (named its ADR numbers, bug numbers and repo layer labels) — the exact anti-pattern Step 3's "write the principle, not the incident" rule warns about. Ratio 173→172 B/line while adding two rules.

## 1.123.14

- **`condense-claude-md` measured file STATE but never file TRAJECTORY, so a repeat condense reported a passing number as done.** Running it on the global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (43KB) produced 43→37KB and a "done" report; the user replied "37kb would fill up in 1/2 update". `git log` showed five condenses in six weeks with the post-condense floor rising 16.8→30.4KB, and `⚠️`-rules growing 8→48 in a month against reference tables that grew only 42% — a growth-rate problem no ceiling check can see. Added a Process #5 rule to check commit history before drafting, compare a stable content class against a growing one, and report **headroom in days at the observed rate** rather than bytes-vs-ceiling, plus the honest verdict that compression is a treadmill when growth is the diagnosis. Its trigger also names the "quite dense already" phrasing that opened this session, which was the hint the light pass would cap out.
- **An `AskUserQuestion` option was priced on an unverified fact, and the user chose based on it.** The recommended "retirement pass" option previewed `rg` and GitNexus as two dead rules worth ~850B; `which rg` returned `/opt/homebrew/bin/rg` — still installed, trap still live, rule not removable. Only GitNexus was genuinely gone, so the chosen option returned ~450B and the user's next message was "seems like dont change much". Restructuring #6 already banned outsourcing the seam-test to a menu, but nothing covered the options you legitimately DO ask being wrong. Added a rule to verify every factual claim in a label/description/preview before presenting (`which`/`ls`/`grep -c` per named item), noting that a rule whose tool is still installed is a live guardrail regardless of its own "abandoned/removed" note.
- **A fleet-wide rewrite was projected from the three longest examples and overshot ~40%.** Capping all 50 warning-rules to imperative+`Tell:` was estimated at 16.8→8KB from the previewed extremes (mean 335B, previewed rows ~670B); it landed at 13.4KB. Added a rule to project from the MEDIAN, quote the basis, and distinguish narrative rules (compress hard) from procedure rules (every clause load-bearing, barely move).
- **Companion splits left orphaned duplicate rows the byte count paid for twice.** Three rows sat in both `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` and `CLAUDE-platform-gotchas.md`, surviving two days past a commit whose own message was "prune rules already covered by companion files" — invisible afterward, since pointer and companion both look correct. Added a Hard rule to `grep -c` each moved row against both files after any split, sweeping pre-existing companions too. No sibling skill needed the same patch: `grep -ln` across `skills/*/SKILL.md` found five content-relocating skills, none implementing the companion split themselves (`update-claude-docs` Condense mode invokes this skill).
- **Density pass**: collapsed four ⚠️ callouts that re-justified one rule ("a capped light pass isn't the answer, escalate to the structural lever") into the atomic-file gate, and tightened the ceiling-vs-context-efficiency callout now partly covered by the new growth-rate rule. Net +1.7KB for four new rules (186 bytes/line, from 176).
- **Version files were out of sync** — `plugin.json` at 1.123.13, `marketplace.json` still at 1.123.12. Both set to 1.123.14.

## 1.123.13

- **`done` and `ship` had no guard against an honest mid-run hand-back, the one pause mode their existing rules structurally miss.** Running `/done` on a Dourr server-ops session, the run completed Step 3 (`update-claude-docs`), reported accurate partial progress — Step 4 explicitly labelled outstanding — and ended the turn offering to continue; the user replied "u shouldnt stop until done finish". The header already said "Execute all steps in sequence without pausing for confirmation" and a NEVER row already forbade reporting a step done when only part ran, but neither binds here: nothing was asked, and nothing false was claimed. The gap is that both skills invoke sub-skills via `Skill()`, and each returning sub-skill is a natural-feeling turn boundary where transparency reads as good practice while the run is half-finished. Added a ⚠️ callout to both headers naming the mode ("ending the turn with steps outstanding is a pause, even when you announce it and ask nothing") with the tell — your reply names a remaining step instead of performing it. `ship` had **zero** continuation guard despite delegating to `/commit` mid-chain and still owing push, CI verification, and the release note.

## 1.123.12

- **Two open Next Steps items from `doc-condensation/current.md`, both patched.** (1) `update-plugin` Step 5's consumer report was copy-pasteable but unfenced, with the issues-URL pointer written immediately after it — the same boundary class already fixed in `ship` 5.8 and `gchat-format` (fenced content is invisible-boundary-prone once its own last line could read as more report). Fixed by fencing the report and moving the URL pointer above the fence, matching the established pattern. (2) `condense-task-doc`'s split rule (`task-summary/references/templates.md`) only covered a single-domain split — index + its own `decisions/<theme>.md` — where `current.md` survives as router. This session's own `plugin-maintenance/current.md` → `{agent-architecture,doc-condensation,madr-structure}` split was the uncovered case: a multi-domain fan-out with no surviving parent index, where doc-wide-but-not-thematic content (a skills registry table, a cross-cutting decisions index) has no sibling folder to land in and silently drops unless caught by `/done`'s referential-integrity pass after the fact. Added a "Multi-domain fan-out" subsection to `templates.md` naming the fold-into-most-relevant-sibling-or-flag-to-user rule, and pointed `condense-task-doc` Step 2 at it.

## 1.123.11

- **`done`'s Step 1 post-agent handling triaged product-reviewer findings by SOURCE, not by KIND, so a real correctness bug surfaced through the product reviewer would have been parked as a scope question instead of fixed.** Reported via [GitHub issue #8](https://github.com/syafiq555/syafiqkit/issues/8) from a consumer install running `/done` on a WhatsApp CRM feature: the product reviewer flagged `WhatsAppOfficialService::sendTemplate()` missing the `$fromPhoneNumberId` parameter its sibling `sendMessage()` had — template replies would silently send from the wrong number. That's a diff-blind correctness bug (a code reviewer scoped to the diff structurally can't see an untouched sibling call site; a product reviewer catches it because it reads intent, not the diff), but the wording only had one branch: "product recommendations, not auto-fixes ... ask which to implement now." Following it literally would defer a shipping-blocker as a scope decision. Fixed by splitting Step 1's post-agent bullet into two branches — a confirmed correctness/security defect gets fixed immediately like any code-reviewer finding; a genuine missing-journey/UX/business gap still goes to the user as a scope decision.

## 1.123.10

- **`agent-setup`'s template-drift check said "diff each file against its template" but a single agent summarizing all 8 diffs in prose dropped field-level drift entirely.** Running the checklist on Autorentic's `.claude/agents/*.md`, a delegated diff agent correctly caught a whole-block issue (`code-reviewer.md` carrying a stray `Write`/`Edit` grant) but reported "no drift" on 5 files that, on direct grep, had visibly thinner `description:` frontmatter than their templates — only surfaced because the user separately asked "check the description for all agents" as a follow-up. A second follow-up ("check the front matter") then found a third defect the same diff agent missed entirely: 6 of 8 files carried an `Agent`-tool inline comment copy-pasted from `Explore`'s template line, naming the wrong agent ("lets this Explore spawn..." on `Plan`, `code-simplifier`, `product-reviewer`, `browser-verifier`, `claude-md-pruner`). Both are real per-line drift a whole-section diff summary calls "no drift" because nothing whole is missing — only a single field inside an otherwise-present block. Fixed by adding an explicit sub-item after the Template-drift checklist entry: run `grep "^description:"` and `grep -n "  - Agent\|# lets this"` directly on both copies for every file, not via a delegated prose summary.

## 1.123.8

- **`done/SKILL.md`'s own Output table had a warning callout inserted between two rows, splitting one GFM table into two broken halves.** Caught during a docs-only `/done` run's referential-integrity check (the check that stands in for code review when a diff is pure Markdown) — the `Task docs` and `Plugin` rows of the Step-4 exit-gate table had a `⚠️ Verify those two rows...` paragraph wedged between them, closing the first table early; the `Plugin` row would render as an orphaned single-row table missing its header separator. Fixed by moving the callout to the table boundary (after the last row) instead of mid-table. Added a general gotcha to root `CLAUDE.md`'s skill-review checklist: a blank line + prose between `|`-rows always splits a Markdown table, so any inserted callout must land on a table boundary, never between two existing rows.
- **Tightened the Ownership-guard wording in `done`/`task-summary`/`condense-claude-md`** — incremental hardening of the three-tell phrasing shipped in 1.123.5 (rival writer / parallel session / prior-session uncommitted work) and the `condense-claude-md` Restructuring #6→#7 fallback text from 1.123.2, no new decision.

## 1.123.7

- **`read-summary`'s trigger read as session-start-only, so it never re-fired when the work moved into a new domain/repo mid-conversation.** A session that loaded one domain's task doc at the start then drifted into a SECOND repo (Autorentic) for the bulk of the work only checked that repo's docs because the user pointed at them ("the read summary is not only at the start... everytime we get into another domain... even end also need to check in case there's task docs related"). The `description:` frontmatter said "Use at session start" and body line 29 buried the scope-change rule in prose framed around one ship's multiple commits. Fixed by rewriting the frontmatter to name three re-trigger points (mid-conversation domain shift, a second repo entering, near-end related-doc check) and promoting the buried body rule into an explicit ⚠️ 3-point list with a Tell ("the user points you at a doc/repo you should have found yourself"). Also fixed a pre-existing version drift: `marketplace.json` sat at 1.123.2 while `plugin.json` was 1.123.6 — the silent mismatch the convention warns about — both now 1.123.7.
- **`read-summary` had no rule against the inverse of its live-state warning: re-deriving a decision the doc ALREADY made and handing it back to the user as a question.** The doc recorded a resolved tradeoff (a resource constraint → a concurrency/scheduling choice); after reading it, an `AskUserQuestion` re-presented that same fork, drawing "everything is in the task docs." The "authoritative FOR" section only warned about trusting a stale snapshot for live-state, never about wasting a settled decision. Added the mirror rule: use the decision the doc made, ask the user only what THEIR context decides, measure what's live. `tackle`'s shared-mechanism grep confirmed it already carries the equivalent ("don't just ask which items they want") in its own description — no divergence to fix.

## 1.123.6

- **The command 1.123.5 prescribed to prove a write landed has a SECOND silent blind spot: a `git diff` pathspec resolves against CWD, not the repo root.** Running the exit gate's own `git diff HEAD --stat -- tasks/marketing/blog-automation/current.md` from inside that directory returned empty with exit 0 — identical to "the write never happened" — for a file that showed 231 changed lines once the path was given relative to cwd. Hit while verifying a task-doc write this session; the doc had been rewritten seconds earlier. Worse than the staged-file case it was meant to fix, because 1.123.5's fix reads as the trap-proof form, so an empty result now looks authoritative. Fixed by anchoring the gate command to `git -C "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"` and stating that **an empty diff is inconclusive, never proof of a missing write** — fall through to the grep tie-breaker the gate already recommends. Step 2's shared-mechanism grep found the same `git diff HEAD -- <file>` baseline prescribed in `_shared/references/two-tier-condense.md` (which `condense-claude-md` and `condense-task-doc` both point at); patched there once rather than copied into each, since that file is already the canonical home for the dirty-baseline rule.

## 1.123.5

- **`/done`'s concurrency guard only fired on a RIVAL writer, so uncommitted work left by a PRIOR session slipped through as ownable diff.** Invoked after a one-file UI change, `git status --short` returned 18 files in the primary repo and 7 in the sibling — all of it a previous session's undeployed feature work, with no agent running and no parallel session. Step 1 says to partition that list across agents, which would have handed reviewers and a simplifier 25 files this session never authored. The guard's two tells ("a background `Agent` you spawned is still running" / "files you never touched — a parallel Claude session") both presuppose someone racing you, so neither trips on a tree that is simply dirty from yesterday, even though the destructive outcome is identical. Worked around by hand-typing scope boundaries and a banned-command list into all four agent prompts — a prompt-level fix that dies with the session. Fixed by renaming the section to **Ownership guard**, adding the third tell (uncommitted work predating this session, no other writer) and folding the repo-wide-command ban into the Step-1 row: a file partition does not scope `git stash`/`checkout -- .`/`reset`/`clean`/`restore`, and a review agent with edit tools runs one as readily as a build agent. Step 2's shared-mechanism grep across `skills/*/SKILL.md` found `task-summary` carrying the same two-tell wording on its own pre-scan guard; patched identically so the two cannot diverge.
- **`/done`'s exit gate demands proof that a doc/CLAUDE.md write landed, but never says how — and the obvious command lies.** The Knowledge and Task-docs rows require "confirmed the target changed," and a bare `git diff -- <path>` returns empty for any file staged earlier in the session, reading as a write that never happened. Hit on three of six targets this run; resolved with `git diff HEAD --stat`. This is the same staged-file blind spot Step 1 already documents for `git status --short`, absent at the opposite end of the workflow. Fixed by naming `git diff HEAD --stat` on the gate rows, with a grep for the written text as the tie-breaker.

## 1.123.4

- **`read-summary`'s Plan Mode branch ended with an escape hatch ("only when the doc read leaves a real gap") that fires wrongest when the task doc is best-written.** Invoked in plan mode on a workshop brand/model transcript, the skill's Read Order ran correctly — the doc had a dedicated gotcha table naming the exact tables, the config-vs-DB disconnect, and the missing CRUD. That accuracy made the gap-check return "no gap," so no `Explore` agent was spawned; four rounds of inline grep/Read followed, each one confirming the doc, and the turn went straight to `AskUserQuestion` with a design fork. The user interrupted the question: *"deploy explore agents to research user journey and code before u propose anything."* The delegated pass then surfaced three design-changing facts the doc never mentioned because nobody had hit them yet — a third free-text entry point (`walkin/create.blade.php`, plain `<input>`, no dropdown at all), the wizard's model cascade being DB-backed while the walk-in modal's is config-backed (same brand, different model lists), and existing `Appointment` mutators already uppercasing brand at the write layer. Line 96 already mandated `Explore` for downstream searching, but reads as a *routing* rule (which agent type) rather than a *trigger* rule (whether to delegate at all), so it didn't catch this. Fixed by inverting the default — delegate unless the doc answers the question outright — and replacing the subjective gap-check with an objective tell: you have formed a recommendation from the doc plus your own greps and are about to put it to the user. Step 2's shared-mechanism grep run across `skills/*/SKILL.md` for `Plan Mode`: only `agent-setup` (unrelated context) and `read-summary` match; `tackle` routes through `read-summary` rather than restating the rule, so the fix is correctly single-file.

## 1.123.3

- **`task-summary`'s trigger description was too thin to fire on a re-entry mid-session.** Asked to "rewrite these docs with proper template with /task-summary," the skill was invoked once early, then a later "continue" in the same session was answered by hand-editing docs against the already-recalled template shape instead of calling `Skill(...)` again — producing docs missing the mandatory Quick Start section. The description named only "create or update," with no phrase covering "rewrite/conform to template" or a session that already knows the shape. Fixed by widening the description to name rewrite/conformance phrasing explicitly and stating that the skill should be invoked fresh even when its rules are already known from a prior read — mirroring how `condense-task-doc`'s description already names its trigger phrases and edge cases.

## 1.123.2

- **`condense-claude-md` — a failed subdirectory seam-test (Restructuring #6) was read as "no split possible" instead of "fall through to the companion split (#7)."** On `mrcount/resources/CLAUDE.md`, the file's two big gotcha tables correctly matched the "already all-atomic-facts, skip compression" signal (Process #6, line 81) — but that branch stopped after grepping candidate subdirectories and finding none owned the "Critical Anti-Patterns" table, and reported "leave as-is, already dense." The user pushed back ("seems still densed") before a second pass applied #7 and moved ~97 of 127 rows to a `.claude-companions/` file, cutting the project file from 359 to 217 lines. This is the mirror image of 1.122.3 (which fixed jumping to companion *without* running the subdir seam-test first) — together they cover both wrong endpoints of the same decision chain. Fixed by folding an explicit "#6 failing routes you to #7, it isn't a dead end" clause into line 81's existing sentence, rather than adding a new standalone callout (line 77 already makes an adjacent point about not stopping at a light-pass result, so this was tightened to avoid restating it).

## 1.123.1

- **`update-claude-docs` — Step 1's grep table classified a 0-hit search as "New — add entry", which is wrong for any CLAUDE.md that delegates detail to companion files, and would have appended two duplicate rules this run.** The global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` on disk is a 196-line index whose rules live in `~/.claude-companions/shared/*.md`, reached only by `> 📖` pointers; the version injected into context is the expanded one. So a rule quotable verbatim from context returned zero hits from `grep -n` AND `grep -an` on disk. `grep -a` is no help — the text is in a different FILE, not an unreadable one, which is exactly why this misreads as the known NUL-byte trap and gets waved through. Caught only by a control (`grep -c ""` → 196 lines, `grep -ac` on a common term → 14 hits) proving grep could read the file at all, which localized the rules to `CLAUDE-verification-rules.md` rows 47 and 50 — row 50 was then refined instead of duplicated. Fixed with a fourth outcome on the table: clear the companion check before classifying as New, using the quotable-from-context-but-absent-on-disk gap as the discriminator, and noting a new rule may itself belong in a companion rather than the index. Cross-checked the shared mechanism per Step 2: `condense-claude-md` (which creates companions) and `read-summary` (which reads them) already handle this; `update-claude-docs` sat between them and was the only blind spot.

## 1.123.0

- **`md-to-pdf` — the skill described only Mermaid *diagram* rendering, so a request for a chart got answered "Mermaid can't do quantitative charts with axes" and steered to flowcharts + a pie; the user had to push back before real bar charts were rendered.** The capability was present but unreachable: `xychart-beta` appeared once, as a *troubleshooting* row about its pale default palette — a footnote you only reach after already choosing it. The overview line ("Convert Markdown files to clean PDFs with rendered Mermaid diagrams") and the `description:` frontmatter both framed the skill as diagrams-only, so nothing in the read path ever surfaced charts as an option. Fixed by naming charts in the frontmatter (adds "chart"/"graph" trigger words) and the overview, plus a chart-type routing table mapping intent → construct. Two adjacent gotchas added from the same run: a `pie` whose top slice exceeds ~85% collides its remaining labels into an unreadable smear (keep the table, drop the chart), and `mmdc` exits 0 on charts that render but don't communicate — so every PNG needs a visual read before embedding, not just an existence check.

## 1.122.5

- **`task-summary` — `## Bugs Fixed`/`## Critical Gotchas` rows were written as full investigation narratives (5-10 sentences, timestamps, assertion counts), not the ≤2-sentence rows Layer 2 already specifies — so a doc could pass its own density rules at write time yet still bloat one row per bug-fix session, needing a full `condense-task-doc` pass every few commits.** Traced against `tasks/marketing/blog-automation/current.md`'s git history: a prior condense (`fbd1825b`) was undone by exactly this pattern across 9 subsequent `/done`-driven commits, each appending one verbose row. Root cause: the write-time instruction for these two sections said only "Append new bugs" / "Append new rows" with no compose-time density reminder — the ≤2-sentence cap existed elsewhere in the doc (Layer 2) but wasn't cross-referenced at the point of writing. Fixed by instructing both rows to be composed already condensed (paraphrase root cause + fix directly, never transcribe the session narrative and trim after) rather than adding a separate post-write check — a check that fires after the bloated row is already written doesn't prevent it, it just adds a pass.

## 1.122.4

- **`update-plugin` — Step 2's cross-skill "shared mechanism" grep has no check in Step 4 that it was actually run.** In the same Dourr session as 1.122.3, the first `update-plugin` pass patched only `condense-claude-md` and reported done without checking whether other skills shared the seam-test/companion-split mechanism — the user had to ask "u didnt check related skills? only the skill i used?" before the grep ran (it turned out clean, but only by luck of asking). The rule existed in Step 2 but nothing in Step 4's validation checklist tests that it fired — a fix framed as "this skill's bug" reads as self-contained and the cross-skill check silently gets skipped. Added a Step 4 gate: before reporting done, name the grep you ran across `skills/*/SKILL.md`, or state explicitly that no shared mechanism applies.

## 1.122.3

- **`condense-claude-md` — offered a subdir-vs-companion split as a menu choice instead of running the seam-test first.** In a Dourr session (`blog-runner/CLAUDE.md`), the skill jumped straight to proposing a `.claude-companions/` companion file without first grepping candidate subdirectories, even though Restructuring #6/#7 already prescribes running the seam-test before falling back to a companion split. The user caught it ("why u straight go to companion file instead of checking whether split claude sub directory is better"). The rule existed but wasn't followed in practice — strengthened it into an explicit "run the grep yourself, don't hand it to the user as an `AskUserQuestion`" instruction, and consolidated two other restatements of the same "don't wait for pushback" point into one. Also added: a file can pass the line/byte ceiling in Process #4 and still be a legitimate split candidate when the user's actual goal is context-window efficiency rather than a size violation — the ceiling check and the "does this need to auto-load" question are distinct triggers.

## 1.122.2

- **`update-plugin` — the "write a general rule, not a retelling of the incident" instruction only lived in the Step 3a density table, out of the rule-writing path.** In a Dourr session this skill patched `task-summary` with a rule over-fitted to the triggering incident (naming the specific UI element, marker, and exact words); the user had to say "make it generalize." Step 3's "Adding a workflow rule" guidance specified only the rule's *format* (`❌ X | ✅ Y`), never that its *content* must abstract past the session — and the one line that says so (line 95, "strip to the bare rule") sits inside the density-*shrink* checklist, which a fresh one-row edit doesn't trigger. Added a generalization instruction directly to Step 3's rule-writing block, with a tell (a proper noun / literal UI string / a count from this run in your rule = over-fit).
- **`task-summary` — a user's mid-session scope decision (defer/decline/deprioritize) wasn't captured, and a deferred item kept its act-now marker.** A `/done` run surfaced findings into Next Steps; the user deferred one ("not now, needed later"). The deferral was acknowledged in chat but never written to the doc — the item still read as act-now and kept its urgency marker, and the user had to prompt repeatedly to get it recorded and made consistent. The `## Next Steps` update row said nothing about capturing an explicit scope decision or about keeping markers consistent with status. Added a generalized rule covering both.

## 1.122.1

- **`condense-task-doc` — Step 9's line-budget check had no path back to Step 2's split trigger.** A Dourr session condensed a whole-doc MADR (`blog-automation/current.md`, 351→344 lines) via real sentence-level bloat that Step 2 correctly detected — but 9 accumulated ADR blocks kept it at 344 lines, still over the 300-line target, with nothing left to compress (each block already near its ~20-line floor). Step 9 as written only says "run a second, more aggressive pass," which erodes real content for no line-count gain when the excess is MADR blocks, not prose. The user caught it live ("seems still quite big, will fill up after 1-2 iterations"); the fix that actually worked was `templates.md`'s no-ask split (Key Technical Decisions → `decisions/pipeline-history.md`, 344→177 lines), which the skill's own Step 2 already describes for the pre-condense case but never re-offers post-condense. Step 9 now names this explicitly and points back to Step 2's split branch instead of prescribing more compression. Also fixed a version-file drift: `plugin.json` (1.122.0) and `marketplace.json` (1.121.1) had silently diverged — exactly the failure mode the Version Bumping rule warns about.

## 1.122.0

- **`merge-task-docs` + `condense-task-doc` — the index→`decisions/` split silently dropped every operational section, and no gate could see it.** Both skills' split branches described the resulting index as "Quick Start + a routing table", but `templates.md` L136 specifies three things: "Quick Start, **doc-wide operational tables**, and a routing table". The paraphrase lost the middle item, so `Task Status`/`Bugs Fixed`/`Critical Gotchas`/`Next Steps` went down into `decisions/*.md` and never came back up. Confirmed on Autorentic `dd63b1fa`: three template-compliant source docs (all with Critical Gotchas + Next Steps, two with Bugs Fixed + Task Status) merged into an index holding only Quick Start + Files + Last Session — the only one of 10 split docs in that repo missing them. It survived three months and a `/done` run because **every gate in both skills is subtractive** ("no rows deleted", "no duplication", prune-the-excess); none detects an absent section, so a merge that drops whole section types is indistinguishable from a clean one. Cost: an open 🔴 regression was invisible to anyone reading `current.md`, the exact failure the index exists to prevent. Fixed both split branches to cite the three-part rule, added an **absence gate** to `merge-task-docs` Step 7 (`grep '^## '` the index, diff section types against the sources, sanity-check against a sibling split doc), and a `condense-task-doc` rule that a required section may lose every row but never its heading — leave a pointer row instead.

## 1.121.1

- **`condense-claude-md` — the companion-file split (Restructuring #7) could ship machine-specific paths into a tracked file.** An Autorentic session split a `CLAUDE.md` section into `.claude-companions/shared/CLAUDE-integration-and-reset.md`, carrying over hardcoded `~/herd/dourr/...` paths verbatim from the source section — fine when that content lived partly in a per-user `CLAUDE.local.md`, but now shipped to every teammate's machine with a different checkout path. Added a rule: reword to a portable reference (name the sibling repo/file, not its absolute path) before writing any `shared/` companion file, and grep the moved block for `~/`/`/Users/` first.

## 1.121.0

- **`read-summary` — the doc-staleness handoff could be satisfied in letter while dropping the finding.** Its step 2 said to "tell the user to run `task-summary`" and "confirm scope before writing", which together made the correction contingent on a user reply. An Autorentic session caught a task doc claiming prod held one external invoice when it held two, reported it, offered to fix it — and the user (reasonably) acted on the answer instead of the offer. The correction only landed because `/done` happened to reopen the same doc later. Step 3 now states that an offer parked on a reply is not routing: hand off in the same turn, or carry the correction as an owed action into the next doc-writing skill, never as a question that expires. Also added step 1 (**sweep, don't spot-report**) — the same doc's `Quick Start`/`Immediate next actions` still said "deploy the B8 fix" for a fix already live on prod, higher-impact staleness than the line originally flagged, because those fields are written once and revisited least.

## 1.120.0

- **New skill `sweep-doc-overlaps`** — a real Autorentic session asked to find "similar things across domains" in `tasks/`, and `merge-task-docs` had no entry point for that: its own Step 1 assumes a domain or keyword is already known. Fanned out 5 parallel `Explore` agents across all ~25 domains (batched by directory, not by hypothesis) and surfaced two real cross-domain candidates — `payment/{gateway,stuck-payment,consolidated-payment}` (same 2C2P callback pipeline, mutual `Related:` refs) and `statement/agency-leaderboard` → `report/pm-reports` (explicitly reuses the same aggregation service + UI pattern) — against ~20 keyword-adjacent pairs that were correctly kept separate. The new skill packages that pattern: discovery only, batched by domain for write-safety even though this is read-only, points agents at `merge-task-docs`'s own merge-test section instead of restating it, and hands confirmed groups to `merge-task-docs` for actual execution rather than duplicating its write/delete/reconcile logic.

## 1.119.3

- **`condense-claude-md/SKILL.md` + `update-claude-docs/references/structure.md` — the companion-file location rule ("nearest git-repo root") left the global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` case ambiguous, since `~/.claude` isn't itself a git repo.** Both the global `~/.claude-companions/` and a project's `.claude/companions/` had drifted to sit nested inside `.claude/` instead of as its sibling, only caught when the user questioned the location directly. Both files now state explicitly: for the global case, use `~/.claude-companions/`, a sibling of `~/.claude/`, never nested inside it.

## 1.119.2

- **`read-summary/SKILL.md` — Read Order step 6 (SIBLING REPO) sent you to the sibling's `CLAUDE.md`/`CLAUDE.local.md` but never said to follow their `> 📖` companion pointers, which is where the facts it names actually live.** A two-repo session needed the sibling's staging DB/container layout; step 6 lists exactly that topic ("containers, credentials, deploy mechanics, or server state"), the main file delegated the detail to a companion, and stopping at the main file read as complete. Result: queried the PROD database believing it was staging and got a plausible wrong answer with no error — a real row from the wrong environment. Step 5 has had a Companion bullet for the current repo all along; step 6 is its sibling-repo twin and lacked it, so the rule existed nowhere a two-repo session reads.
- **Same step — added the discovery gotcha that turned one miss into two.** `grep -rl` reaches neither `.claude/` nor gitignored files, so an existing companion returns 0 hits for even a common word (it greps fine when NAMED directly; `--no-ignore` doesn't help). The companion was declared broken and the fact nearly re-documented elsewhere; the `claude-md-pruner` agent caught it. Rule: settle a companion's existence with `ls <path>`, and note that a control which merely hits (`README.md`) proves recursion works, not that the target is in scope.
- Checked the shared-mechanism sweep: `commit`/`ship`/`condense-task-doc`'s `grep -rl` uses are content sweeps over tracked `tasks/**/*.md`, not existence checks of `.claude/`-or-gitignored files — not affected, deliberately left alone.
- Density pass per Step 3a: first draft ran the ratio 185.1→191.6 b/line; collapsed the restated control explanation to land at 188.6 with both rules intact.

## 1.119.1

- **`agent-setup/templates/code-simplifier.template.md` — the Component-vs-Utility extraction table missed the case that matters most for a fat component: STATEFUL logic.** A session shrank a 799-line React component and the first simplifier pass extracted only presentational JSX/constants (799→640, modest); the user had to manually invoke a second run, which extracted the stateful clusters into hooks and took it to 329. The template framed extraction as a two-way choice (rendering→Component, transform→Utility), which structurally can't point at "extract effects/refs/event-wiring into a hook/composable." Added a framework-neutral callout: a fat component is shrunk by extracting LOGIC into hooks, not markup — splitting JSX is the low-value pass. The AR-specific example row lives in the project's `code-simplifier.md` High-Impact table, not the template.

## 1.119.0

- **`condense-task-doc/SKILL.md` + `task-summary/SKILL.md` — the unit of condensation is the doc SET, not the file named in the args.** A `/update-summary <path> condense this` run condensed only `current.md` and reported a byte delta, while the `decisions/*.md` siblings holding 2× the index's bytes went untouched — the correction was "when i say condense meaning u hv to look at all inside too not only the current.md". The skill's splitting rule creates those siblings but nothing said a later condense must cover them. Process step 1 now scopes to the set (`ls` the directory), step 8 reports per-file + set-total deltas, and `task-summary`'s Size budget measures `cat current.md decisions/*.md | wc -c`. Description updated so the trigger itself carries the set scope.
- **`condense-task-doc/SKILL.md` — duplication scanning was per-file, which is blind to the cross-file case by construction.** The same run reported "no duplication" off per-file greps; a set-wide grep then found four Bugs Fixed rows explained in full in the index AND in full in their owning ADR. A fact surviving in two files passes every single-file check, and step 7's diff-verify only proves nothing was *lost*. Step 5 now greps the whole set at once (naming the index-vs-ADR pattern, and requiring a grep of the pointer's TARGET before trusting it), and a new step 10 re-runs that grep after all writes land — section-by-section editing is how duplication gets introduced *during* a pass.
- Density pass on both touched files per Step 3a: cut a worked incident ("1 of 6 fabrications") from step 7, a duplicated `two-tier-condense.md` citation, and step 2's restated split-vs-condense mechanics; collapsed the cross-file bullet in "What to cut" to a pointer at Process step 5 rather than leaving the rule stated twice.
- Fixed a live version mismatch — `marketplace.json` was stranded at 1.117.3 while `plugin.json` read 1.118.0, the exact silent drift the Version Bumping convention warns about.

## 1.118.0

- **`read-summary/SKILL.md` — investigation exit gate now covers scope escalation, not just wrong-question substitution.** An investigation that follows real evidence into a wider defect ("this affects X too" → "the whole flow needs checking") kept converting each finding into more planning, ending at an `ExitPlanMode` for work the user never scoped — they rejected it and deferred the session. The existing exit gate catches answering the *wrong* question; nothing caught answering the *right* one and escalating past the deliverable. New rule: report the finding + blast radius, then ask before designing a fix. Tell named — the investigation is about to emit a plan, migration list, or work partition.
- **`task-summary/SKILL.md` + `condense-task-doc/SKILL.md` — carved out MADR `Status: committed` from the git-state deletion rule.** The two skills instructed "ANY hit of `committed` is a delete" while the plugin's own `references/templates.md` prescribes `Status: committed | planned | debating` as the MADR decision-lifecycle field — following one rule broke the other, and a real `/write-summary` run hit the contradiction on `e-signing.md`'s seven decision blocks. The rule's own rationale (`git log` answers it) doesn't hold: no git command reports whether a decision was accepted. Carve-out added at both statements of the rule, per the shared-mechanism rule.

## 1.117.3

- **`update-plugin/SKILL.md` — density pass on its own Step 1 scan-scope callout.** Ironic case: the skill's own density checklist flags "a skill that preaches conciseness while itself running long, self-justifying paragraphs" as highest-priority — its two stacked ⚠️ paragraphs about whole-transcript scanning did exactly that, one restating the rule and one defending it with specific incident numbers ("1 of 3", "2 of 3 signals"). Collapsed to one paragraph, kept the mandatory numbered-message-list mechanism, cut the decorative incident numbers (verified via diff — no rule dropped). 95.9→92.1 bytes/line.

## 1.117.2

- **`done/SKILL.md` — density pass, Step 1's agent-count enforcement section.** 5 stacked `⚠️` paragraphs (110.8 bytes/line) restated overlapping angles of one rule — per-role multiplication, one-block emission, no "spawn the rest next," anchor-to-Glob, post-send counter-check — collapsed to one paragraph with no rule dropped (verified via diff). `-1,039 bytes` (-4.5%). `task-summary/references/templates.md` checked in the same pass (55.2 bytes/line) — already clean, no changes.

## 1.117.1

- **`task-summary/references/templates.md` — named one exception to the whole-doc-MADR split's "no ask required" default.** A `condense-task-doc` session found two files over the split threshold and, instead of splitting silently per the documented default, asked for confirmation because the doc's own Next Steps had already deferred the split twice as "disproportionate for this session's scope" — a self-caught deviation from the skill's explicit instruction. The deferral history is a real scope signal the default didn't account for (it assumes the routine single-file case): the rule now names it as the one case worth a confirmation, since it usually means multiple files split at once. `condense-task-doc` and `merge-task-docs` both inherit the fix via the shared reference, no duplication.

## 1.117.0

- **`/done` — raised Step 1's changed-file agent-count tiers and removed light mode entirely.** User asked to raise the agent-count threshold (hitting the 41+/7-agent tier too easily); clarified via follow-up questions into raising the three file-count buckets ≤15/16–40/41+ → ≤30/31–80/81+ (agent counts per tier unchanged: 3/5/7 total) and dropping the `<5`-file light-mode carve-out rather than rescaling it. Light mode was threaded through four separate decision points — the mode-selection section, the agent-count table's asymmetric-count sentence, `task-summary`'s invocation scope in Step 4, and the Output table's Product row — all four edited; verified zero `light` string hits remain in the skill file. Every diff now routes through docs-only/infra-only/full mode only.
- **plugin-maintenance (task docs, no skill behavior change)**: split `decisions/agent-architecture.md` (332 lines/34.6KB) and `decisions/doc-condensation.md` (310 lines/34.2KB) into thin routers + 3 theme sub-files each, both over the 300-line split threshold with nothing left to condense. Fixed a genuine pre-existing D32 ID collision found along the way (two unrelated decisions both numbered D32 across the two files) — renamed the leak-guard one to D40.

## 1.116.7

- **`two-tier-condense.md` (shared by `condense-claude-md`, `condense-task-doc`, `update-plugin`'s Step 3a) — dropped the spawned background Haiku agent; the Draft step now always rewrites inline in the current turn.** A `condense-claude-md` run spawned the background Haiku drafter exactly as the shared reference prescribed, and the user had it killed mid-run with "i dont want to use agent for that." The Draft/Verify split itself stays (write the full rewrite first, then diff-verify as a separate pass), but neither step spawns an `Agent` anymore — removed the Haiku-specific prompting instructions (checklist handoff, number-verbatim constraint framed as agent instructions) and reworded Verify's rationale to apply to a self-authored draft instead of a delegated one. Fixed once in the shared file plus its two direct callers (`condense-claude-md` step 3, `condense-task-doc` step 7) that had restated "spawn a background Haiku agent" themselves — `update-plugin`'s own reference was already agent-agnostic.

## 1.116.6

- **`read-summary` — added a post-read delegation reminder: open-ended codebase search still routes through `Explore`/`Plan`, never a bare `general-purpose` agent.** A session ran the skill correctly (loaded task context via its own Explore-delegated doc discovery), then afterward needed a "where is the email-verification column" search and dispatched `general-purpose` with a manual `model:` override instead — a project CLAUDE.md rule (#critical, agent-dispatch policy) that this skill's Read Order/Intent Detection never restated, since its own Explore usage is scoped narrowly to doc discovery. Added one line after the "Task description" intent (Normal mode) pointing at the existing CLAUDE.md rule rather than duplicating it — the Plan Mode section already had equivalent coverage (line 97) but Normal-mode task/investigation intents had none.

## 1.116.5

- **`condense-claude-md` — corrected the root-file byte ceiling from ~15KB to 40KB.** The skill's Process step 5 (and its own "verified: 48KB → 44KB" worked example) drove split/companion-file decisions off a target that was roughly 2.5x too tight — a real condense session hit this directly: a 42KB file's light pass reclaimed only ~1.4% (42,265 → 41,667 bytes) and the skill's own guidance then correctly triggered a companion-file split recommendation, but the split was being chased against a threshold well below where it actually needs to kick in. Fixed both mentions in-file (target line + the worked-example conclusion, which no longer holds unchanged at the new ceiling — 44KB now reads as under the 40KB bar rather than "3x over") and added the fresh 42KB→41.7KB data point alongside the existing 48KB→44KB one so the "1-10%" light-pass ceiling reflects both. Checked for the same constant across `task-summary`, `agent-setup` (SKILL.md + `code-reviewer.template.md`), `commit-invoice-generator`, and `update-claude-docs` — all five hits were unrelated (rule counts, minutes, file counts), only `condense-claude-md` encoded the byte target.

## 1.116.4

- **`agent-setup` — fixed two update-run gaps reported via GitHub issue #7.** (1) Step 1's "Agents exist" row and Step 5's template-drift check only diffed *existing* agents against their templates, never enumerating `templates/*.template.md` against `.claude/agents/*.md` to catch a template with no generated agent at all — added a distinct **Missing-agent check** (`comm -23` on sorted basenames) to both steps. (2) Step 5's model-override exemption ("model overrides already justified in-file are NOT drift") read as "leave overrides alone," letting an unjustified deviation (`opus` instead of the template's `sonnet`, no comment) survive an update-run — sharpened so an in-file justification comment is the only exemption; an unjustified override is now explicitly drift. Verified both fixes against this repo's own live state: `task-builder`/`browser-verifier` templates currently have no generated agent here, reproducing Finding 1 exactly.

## 1.116.3

- **`task-summary` + `condense-task-doc` — added two rules a real doc set hit: cross-file gotcha duplication and `⚠️` overuse.** In a session working an index + `decisions/<theme>.md` doc set, a single OPEN item (a signature-placement silent-skip) ended up restated with full mechanism across 10 locations spanning both files, and `⚠️` appeared roughly every 9-11 lines — dense enough that the marker stopped signaling danger. Layer 1's existing "one fact, one home" rule only covered duplication *within* one doc; it didn't name the cross-file case explicitly, and nothing anywhere addressed `⚠️` density. `task-summary`'s Layer 1 table gained two rows (OPEN-item canonical-home-across-files; `⚠️` reserved for irreversible/destructive only), and `condense-task-doc`'s duplicated-facts bullet gained matching detection so a condense pass catches both. Caught mid-session when the user rejected writing the fix as a `## Doc Conventions` block INSIDE the task doc itself — the right home is the tool that writes these docs, not new content shipped in every project's doc.

## 1.116.2

- **`task-summary` + `condense-task-doc` — split "commit/deploy status" into two different facts, only one of which belongs in the doc.** 1.116.1 (same session) made the doc-status grep mandatory but still bundled "committed/uncommitted" with "deployed to staging/prod" as one thing to dedupe-and-keep — wrong fix. The user's actual objection (raised immediately after condensing a real doc under the 1.116.1 rule): `committed`/`uncommitted`/`pushed` isn't useful even said once, even when accurate — `git log`/`git status` already answers it and a doc copy is wrong the instant someone commits. Deploy/environment status ("deployed to staging", "prod gate un-approved") is NOT git-tracked and stays genuinely load-bearing — that's the one that collapses to Quick Start's state line, not the git-state one. Both skills now delete `committed`/`uncommitted`/`pushed` on sight rather than deduping it, and keep the collapse-to-Quick-Start treatment for deploy/environment words only.

## 1.116.1

- **`task-summary` — named commit/deploy status as a mandatory grep target, not left to judgment.** Layer 1 already had a rule ("Commit/deploy status is one fact too — goes ONLY in Quick Start's state line"), but the litmus test and Validate step only said "grep the doc's 2-3 most critical phrases," leaving it to the agent to decide each run whether the status word counted. A real doc still had "UNCOMMITTED" in 5 places (LLM-CONTEXT, Quick Start, a Task Status row, Last Session, and a sibling `decisions/*.md` Status line) — a full sweep only happened because a downstream `/commit` run's staleness gate caught it, not because `task-summary` itself would have. Litmus test + Validate §8 now name the status word explicitly (`uncommitted`/`committed`/`deployed`/`shipped`/`staging`/`prod`) and require a doc-wide case-insensitive grep, re-run to zero, whenever the write changes a commit/deploy state.

## 1.116.0

- **Removed the transcript-scan `Explore` agent from `/done`** (user request: "no use"). It was added over 1.114.0–1.115.0 to defeat recency bias in the doc-update steps, but in the session that removed it the scan returned a full Mode B record AND the doc-update still failed — because that failure was a false "done" report, not a recency miss, so the scan protected against nothing while costing an agent slot + ~47k tokens. Stripped all 7 references from `done/SKILL.md` (Step 1 spawn, Steps 3/4 reconciliation hints, Step 5 handoff, both Output-table rows) and deleted the now-orphaned `_shared/references/transcript-scan.md`.
- **`/done` exit gate: Task-docs + Knowledge rows now require confirming the artifact CHANGED, not just that the skill was invoked.** `task-summary` runs as its own process; invoking it and then not verifying let a session report the task doc "updated" when it was untouched (the exact "manufacture evidence of completion" the gate warns about, one level deeper). Both rows now demand a re-read confirming `Last updated` + content actually changed.

## 1.115.0

- **`product-reviewer` charter → whole-system product vision + `update-plugin` template-parity guard.** The product-reviewer only audited the session's diff, so it missed a project-level product gap the user caught by eye (a PM confirming a legal agreement they can't view). Rewrote its charter (template + plugin's own agent + the originating project copy — all three) to step back to the PROJECT level and name net-new build-or-skip opportunities, not just diff-scoped dead-ends. Root-caused a second miss: the fix was first applied only to the generated `.claude/agents/*.md`, not its `agent-setup` template (would vanish on regeneration). `update-plugin` Step 2 now routes any agent behavioral fix to the TEMPLATE first + every generated copy, with a tell for catching it.
- **`transcript-scan.md` — new Mode B (all-parties full record) + two fixes from a real session where the scan under-performed.** The scan returned a lossy list: it omitted 3 mid-turn user messages (folded into `<system-reminder>` blocks) and paraphrased "deploy to STAGING" as "...to production", and the consuming `/done` doc-steps treated it as optional enrichment so nobody caught the loss. Fixes: (1) **Mode B** — an all-parties grounded timeline (user verbatim + assistant decisions + subagent findings, each cited to a transcript line), now the default for doc-update consumption, alongside the existing user-only Mode A anchor. (2) The mid-turn-`<system-reminder>` extraction was one buried DROP-table row — promoted to a prominent MUST-EXTRACT warning (it's the highest-frequency miss and the sharpest corrections). (3) `/done` Steps 3/4 now treat the record as a **mandatory reconciliation target, not optional enrichment** — walk it and confirm every correction/decision/finding is captured before finishing. Requested after the user found both product-reviewer and transcript-scan output unsatisfactory.

## 1.114.1

- **`_shared/references/transcript-scan.md` (3 fixes from a real session)**: (1) the Pass-2 contaminant table dropped a genuine human message that the harness folded into a `<system-reminder>` on another turn (a mid-turn "run /done" the user typed while a turn was running) — added a KEEP row for the inverse leak (a real message silently dropped, not a contaminant kept). (2) The raw numbered list is a snapshot frozen when the agent ran, but nothing said so — a user reading it as a complete session record asked "that's all?" only to discover later messages were excluded; the Output contract now requires "captured through message N" when the list is shown to the user. (3) Added a "When the user wants a session SUMMARY" section: summarizing the transcript is a distinct deliverable from the raw anchor, and a summary agent confabulates ~⅓ of specifics (wrong filenames, invented review findings, non-existent enum values) — the prompt must forbid inventing and the parent must fact-check the summary against real artifacts before relaying. All three surfaced when a user pushed back across six turns on the scan's output.

## 1.114.0

- **agent-setup (browser-verifier template)**: the agent kept trying to spawn *another* `browser-verifier` inside itself — the `Agent` tool was granted (per D31, for reaching `Explore`) but the grant lived only in a `tools:`-line code comment, invisible to the agent at runtime, so nothing told it *which* type it may spawn. Added a runtime-visible `## Constraints` rule — sub-spawns may ONLY be `Explore`, never another `browser-verifier` and never an editing agent — and tightened the `tools:` comment to match. Template only; this repo generates no `browser-verifier.md`, so consumers pick it up on their next `/agent-setup`.
- **`_shared/references/transcript-scan.md` (new) + done Step 1 wiring**: doc-update skills reconstruct "what happened this session" from the calling loop's own context, which is recency-biased and drops early corrections (`update-plugin`'s Step 1 admits scanning from memory has missed signals twice in one session). New shared reference: the caller resolves the session `.jsonl` path by globbing the unique `$CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` UUID (sidesteps the lossy cwd encoding of anthropics/claude-code#7009/#21085), passes it to an `Explore` agent that runs a two-pass filter (jq drops tool_result bulk; contaminant-strip drops harness-injected `type=="user"` turns — skill-body injections, `<task-notification>`, `<local-command-caveat>`, `[Request interrupted by user]`, bare command scaffolding), and returns a RAW numbered list of the human's genuine messages; judgment stays on the caller. `done` Step 1 now spawns this agent in its batch (skipped in light/docs/infra minimal runs) and feeds the list to Steps 3/5. Verified end-to-end: a ~560KB transcript distilled to the session's 3 genuine messages, including a mid-session interruption a memory scan would drop. `update-plugin`/`update-claude-docs` standalone wiring deferred. See D34.

## 1.113.2

- **update-plugin**: its own Step 1 scan warning ("re-read from the first user message, not the most recent tool call backward") is exactly the failure it produced in the session that wrote 1.113.1 — first pass found 1 of 3 real signals (the invoice fix only), a second pass after "you missed one" found 2 of 3 (added the ship fixes but still missed itself), and only a third explicit call-out ("fail to scan the entire conversation") forced a full re-read. Stating the rule wasn't enough; a mental re-scan kept defaulting to whatever was freshest in context. Added a concrete enforcement step: before writing anything, enumerate every distinct user message as a numbered list (not a summary) BEFORE judging any of them for signals — a mental scan is not the same artifact as a walked list, and this skill exists to catch exactly that gap in other skills' rules.

## 1.113.1

- **commit-invoice-generator**: had no rule distinguishing "update the previous invoice" from "create a new invoice", AND the workflow's step order made this worse — "check append vs new" would have landed after "output invoice table" even once added, so the doc-state check always happened too late to change the output. A session asked to update the invoice doc "use previous invoice" and the skill defaulted to creating a fresh `## INV-YYYY-NNNN` block, prompting the correction "previous invoice i meant the previous invoice, ure creating new invoice." Added a table for append-vs-new resolution AND moved it to step 1 — read the existing doc and resolve append-vs-new BEFORE extracting commits or estimating hours, not after.
- **ship**: two related gaps surfaced in one shipping session. (1) Step 3's push had no warning that `git push` carries the WHOLE local branch, not just the commit `/commit` just made — a push that also carried several days of prior-session backlog commits surprised the user twice ("is this what we deployed?", "but just now we have unpushed one?"). Added a check: if `origin/main..HEAD` is more than 1 commit before pushing, say so in the Ship Summary's operator commentary up front. (2) Step 5's release note was scoped to only the just-shipped commit's CHANGELOG entry, not everything the push actually deployed — the user caught it directly ("give me full release note not the one u committed only, but all the things we pushed"). Release note now covers every CHANGELOG version between the last-known-deployed version and the current one whenever the push carried backlog.

## 1.113.0

- **task-summary**: a module reverse-mapped *because* it has no task doc is now an explicit CREATE signal, not just a note to file. A planning session studied a whole student-practice flow across four Explore agents precisely because no doc existed, then recorded "no task doc exists / worth its own doc later" inside a *related* doc's decision + Last Session and moved on — guaranteeing the next session repeats the full-module study. The user caught it ("did you not notice this has no task docs? you had to study the full module because of that"). Added a rule to the Multi-Domain Scan: writing "no task doc exists for X" or "worth its own doc later" anywhere in a session is the trigger to turn the mapping you already did into that feature's own `current.md` now, then cross-reference — not to defer it.

## 1.112.0

- **agent-setup (Explore template)**: the registered `Explore` agent shadows the harness's built-in `Explore`, which Plan Mode spawns natively with `Write`/`Edit` to draft its plan document — an inherited impulse this shadow's `disallowedTools` blocks but never explicitly tells the agent to expect. A real Plan Mode session showed the agent finish its read-only research, then say "let me create a plan document with all the precise findings" and hit "Error writing file", because nothing in the prompt told it to return findings as its final text response instead. Added an explicit Bootstrap-section rule to the template and its materialized copies (`skormy` and the plugin's own self-hosted instance) naming the Plan Mode lineage as the cause and requiring findings to always come back as text, never a written file.

## 1.111.0

- **ship, read-summary**: neither skill discovered task docs by *shipped commit/file* — `ship`'s CI/prod verification only checked deploy status, and a follow-up `read-summary` investigation answered from a doc already loaded earlier in the session for unrelated Vue work, instead of searching for the doc owning a separate Laravel commit in the same push. A real ship ("what's committed") pushed 3 commits across 2 domains; one of them (a new artisan purge command) had an approved-but-unexecuted prod follow-up step logged in a completely different domain's task doc, and it only surfaced when the user quoted the doc line directly. `ship` Step 4 now requires discovering task docs per shipped commit's changed files (`git show --name-only` + `grep -rl` across `tasks/**/current.md`) and surfacing open Next Steps tied to them; `read-summary`'s discovery section now flags that a doc already in context is not evidence it covers a newly-in-scope commit/file.

## 1.110.0

- **condense-claude-md, update-claude-docs, read-summary**: companion files now live in a dedicated `.claude-companions/` folder at the nearest git-repo root, split into `shared/` (tracked, for companions supporting a checked-in CLAUDE.md/task doc) and `local/` (gitignored, for companions supporting `CLAUDE.local.md` or other local-only files) — replacing the old same-directory `<dir>/CLAUDE-<topic>.md` convention. Also carries the same anchor-repoint fix from 1.109.0 into `update-claude-docs/references/structure.md`, which had the identical unconditional-repoint bug (shared mechanism, same defect, both write-time twins of the same procedure). Migrated the one existing tracked companion (`resources/js/CLAUDE-gotchas.md` → `.claude-companions/shared/CLAUDE-gotchas.md` in Autorentic) and its 3 cross-references (`resources/js/CLAUDE.md` pointer + 2 task-doc decision files).

## 1.109.0

- **condense-claude-md**: the companion-file split's anchor-repoint rule was unconditional ("repoint every cross-reference to the companion, or they silently break") — wrong whenever the parent's own redirect line already indexes the moved anchor by name, which the pointer-quality requirement one section above mandates. A real run split `CLAUDE.local.md`, then "fixed" a sibling agent file's `CLAUDE.local.md #{billing-strategy}` reference to point at the new companion — unnecessary churn, since the parent's redirect line already listed `#{billing-strategy}` and the reference resolved fine as-is (caught and reverted by the user). Rule now only requires repointing when the anchor ISN'T named in the redirect index (e.g. renamed/merged during the move).

## 1.108.0

- **agent-setup / done**: `browser-verifier` is now user-triggered by construction, not by convention. The template's `description:` opened with "Use after implementing a UI/UX feature" — a standing invitation sitting in the exact line a caller reads when choosing an agent, which is why `/done`'s correct opt-in rule never bound the main loop mid-build. Replaced with an explicit `⚠️ USER-TRIGGERED ONLY` clause (propose and wait; a UI diff is a reason to offer, never to spawn). The same inferable-condition hole was closed in `done/SKILL.md` and `done/references/browser-verification.md`, whose gates both allowed "the diff touches UI **and** the user wants runtime proof" — a user *state* Claude was free to infer, and did. `agent-setup`'s load-bearing-rule list and verification checklist now carry the clause as a third protected invariant (`grep -c 'USER-TRIGGERED'` ≥ 1), so a future template edit can't silently drop it.

## 1.107.0

- **tackle**: rewritten from a 155-line 5-step build orchestrator down to a 5-line pointer, based on real usage feedback that the automation was worse than driving `read-summary` → build → `done` manually. Dropped: forced `Explore`/`Plan` agent spawns, Step 1b greenfield branching, the classification table and worked example — all now implicit in the frontmatter `description` (vague multi-item doc continuation only; a specific ask defers to `read-summary` directly). `agent-setup/SKILL.md` + `templates/task-builder.template.md` had 2 stale `/tackle Step 4b` references cleaned up in the same change. Registries (`CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, `tasks/plugin-maintenance/current.md`) synced.
- Root `CLAUDE.md` gained a Working Style rule: when authoring/editing a skill's instructions for Claude, default to trusting model judgment over prescribing procedure — cutting scaffolding, not softening it, is what "lighten this" means.

## 1.104.0

- **commit**: converted from a command (`commands/commit.md`) to a skill (`skills/commit/SKILL.md`), applying the existing D10 pattern (a skill/command sharing a name needs no wrapper) to the last remaining case in this plugin. Command deleted outright, no redirect stub — `/commit` continues to work identically (same workflow: repo discovery, changelog gate, task-doc staleness gate, cross-doc mirror sweep, type/scope inference, optional push). All three skill registries (root `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, `tasks/plugin-maintenance/current.md`) synced, and a stale cross-reference in `skills/ship/SKILL.md` (pointed at the deleted `commands/commit.md` path) was caught and repointed in the same change.

## 1.103.0

- **update-claude-docs (Issue #6)**: narrowed the auto-memory rule instead of mandating a destructive revert. The old rule ("if a memory file was touched this session, revert it before writing the CLAUDE.md entry") deleted only the *current* session's memory file — a real consumer run had 5 pre-existing memory files from earlier sessions under the old convention, and reverting just the newest one left a strictly less consistent state (5 kept, 1 gone) than either "all memories" or "none." The rule now scopes to NEW knowledge only: don't write new captures to memory, but leave any memory file this skill finds already written alone — this skill doesn't own memory cleanup. Also names *why* CLAUDE.md wins for this skill's output (team + subagent visibility) instead of silently overriding the harness's native memory feature.
- **task-summary (Issue #5)**: the mandated Backend/Frontend Gotchas split doesn't fit infra/deploy docs (no back/frontend — hosting, CI pipeline, build config). Templates and the update gap-check now state the split axis is domain-appropriate (Backend/Frontend for application docs, e.g. Hosting/Build-Pipeline for deploy-infra docs) while the `| Issue | Rule |` columns stay fixed; the gap-check flags a missing split or wrong columns, not a differently-named one.
- **done, task-summary (Issue #4)**: `git status --short` is canonically empty when the session's work is already committed, not staged — the existing blind-spot warning covered only the staged case, so a `/done` run after committing partitioned zero files and every agent reported clean on an empty slice. Added the recovery path to both skills (same shared instruction, per the "fix one skill's handling of a shared mechanism = fix all" rule): `git diff --name-only <base>..HEAD` off the session's starting HEAD. Also added an after-the-fact counter-check to `done`'s roll-call rule — writing "TOTAL = N" correctly doesn't prove N `Agent` calls were actually sent; count the emitted calls against TOTAL once results start returning, not just before sending.
- **two-tier-condense (Issue #3)**: the verify pass couldn't catch a fact that survives condensing verbatim but is false. A real run merged a per-phase Files table row (frozen at an earlier phase date) into a living map, reasserting a security bug as current when a later section of the same doc recorded it as fixed — the `-`/`+` diff passed cleanly because nothing was dropped or changed. Added two verify checks: treat merged per-phase/dated table rows as claims to re-verify against current code (later-dated section wins on conflict), and a dedicated post-diff scan for two surviving statements in the finished file that contradict each other. Also: never trust the drafter's self-reported `wc -lc` deltas — re-measure independently.

## 1.102.0

- **done, explore-delegation, two-tier-condense, tackle, agent-architecture (D32)**: corrected a factually wrong rule the plugin asserted in two places and contradicted in a third. `done`'s rules table and `explore-delegation.md` claimed `run_in_background: false` makes an `Agent` call block; `two-tier-condense.md` said the opposite ("agents run in the background by default") — and *it* was right. [Official docs](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents): *"As of v2.1.198, subagents run in the background by default. Claude runs a subagent in the foreground when it needs the result before continuing."* [Issue #69691](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/69691) (OPEN) adds that `false` is honoured in child sessions and **ignored in top-level interactive ones**. The prior CHANGELOG entry (1.x, "pass it explicitly") had already escalated this in the wrong direction — the rule was wrong, not weakly worded, so escalating it compounded the error.
- **The rule that was actually being violated**: parallelism is **every `Agent` call in ONE assistant message**. No flag substitutes for it; one-per-message serialises regardless of `run_in_background`. Conflating the two meant a session could set the flag correctly, emit calls one at a time, and believe it had a parallel batch. `done` Step 1, `tackle` Step 3, and `two-tier-condense` now state the block *is* the parallelism.
- Skills must now **tolerate an async return** from any delegated `Agent` — a step that reads a result "on the next line" is unsafe by construction. Results arrive as `<task-notification>` and are never lost: never poll (`sleep`/`ScheduleWakeup`/`TaskOutput`), never Read an agent's `.output` file (full subagent JSONL — overflows context).
- Recorded, not adopted: frontmatter `background: true` is the only *documented* per-agent control, and it forces background **on** — there is no documented way to force foreground at any level.

## 1.101.0

- **done (Exit gate)**: added the missing question — *is the work done, or just this skill's steps?* The gate was a six-row audit of the skill auditing itself ("did I spawn the reviewer? invoke task-summary?") with no check that the approved plan was finished. A real run built Part 1 of a plan literally titled `X + Y`, saw its tests go green, and invoked `/done` with Y untouched; every gate row passed honestly. The asymmetry that makes this worse than an ordinary miss: `/done` **manufactures evidence of completion** — a ✅ summary table, a task doc reading "Phase 5 shipped", a Quick Start saying "commit the fix" — so an early invocation doesn't just misreport, it writes artifacts the session later reads back and believes. The gate now re-reads the plan/request before the self-audit rows, because a step-completion checklist structurally cannot detect work-incompleteness.
- **tackle (Step 2 — triage)**: two fixes to the classifier. (1) **Split before classifying** — the Env-blocked row listed "a load-test target" among its examples, so a doc line reading "load test" got one class and the *local* half (needing only a script, blocked by nothing) was binned along with the *staging* half. One doc line is routinely two items answering different questions; decompose, then classify each piece. (2) **The user's own words outrank the classification** — a "blocked" verdict on the exact thing the user just pointed at and asked how to do is a signal to re-read the request, not to proceed to the item below it.
- **tackle (Step 1 + trigger)**: a doc path can arrive with a *specific* extra clause ("...also research how we'd do X locally"), not just the vague continuation the trigger described ("let's go"). That clause is a work item; the skill had no notion of it, so it became *input to a plan* — researched, folded into a task doc, cited in a summary — and never done. Research reported nowhere the user reads is not an answer to their question. Step 1 now extracts each clause as its own triage item before reading anything.
- **tackle (Step 5 — wrap)**: the "report what you did NOT do" rule enumerated its categories ("the human-blocked and env-blocked items"), which turned a safety rule into an implicit whitelist — an unbuilt item that was *neither* got checked against the list, found absent, and vanished silently while the report still read as rigorous about omissions. Naming the blocked item is what provided cover for the unnoticed one. Trigger is now "in the triage and not built", whatever the reason. Step 5 also gates `done` on the actionable items actually being built.

## 1.100.0

- **agent-setup**: `code-simplifier`'s recommended model default changed from `opus` to `sonnet` (template frontmatter + SKILL.md registry table). User preference — not a defect in the opus recommendation, just a standing cost/tier call for this agent going forward. Existing already-generated `code-simplifier.md` files in other projects are untouched; only future `/agent-setup` runs pick up `sonnet`.

## 1.99.0

- **done (Step 1 — agent emission)**: replaced the roll-call self-audit, which failed on the exact behaviour it described. The rule already named the failure verbatim ("Saying '3 agents' then emitting 1 `Agent` call raises no error") and prescribed writing the literal list before sending; a real run wrote `TOTAL = 3`, emitted one call, was caught by the user, and then emitted a second single-call block immediately after. Re-stating a rule you just recited is not a lever — the defect is the check's SHAPE, not its wording: "write the list, verify its length" is an audit performed in prose, and prose can be written without being obeyed. It costs nothing, enforces nothing, fails no gate, and surfaces only when a human counts the tool calls. The fix anchors emission to an **observable artifact already in the step** — the `.claude/agents/*.md` Glob run moments earlier — so the instruction is "one `Agent` call per Glob hit you just read", not "count the number you wrote." Also names the tell (a block sent intending to spawn the rest next: there is no next — the turn ends, results return, and every downstream check reads the step as run). Net −5 lines: the sharper rule is shorter than the one it replaces, per the skill's own "one hard constraint beats five soft guidelines".

## 1.98.0

- **two-tier-condense, condense-task-doc, condense-claude-md**: fixed the draft-verify baseline. The shared reference carried the correct rule as a bare parenthetical ("or against the pre-draft snapshot if the file was already dirty") while both consuming skills stated `git diff HEAD -- <file>` as an unqualified ⚠️ imperative — so the louder, closer instruction won and the caveat never fired. On a doc carrying uncommitted edits, HEAD is the wrong baseline: the *other writer's* changes surface as `-` lines and read exactly like the drafter rewriting a protected block, which is the precise failure the verify step exists to catch.
