The cost is asymmetric and silent — "restoring" a false positive destroys the other writer's work while you believe you caught a fabrication, and it contradicts the concurrency posture (`a writer reads a file when it starts, not when it finishes`) these same skills teach elsewhere. The shared reference now owns the baseline rule with its tell (a `-` line whose text you recognise as an edit made earlier this session = wrong baseline, not fabrication); both consumers point at it instead of restating a contradicting default. Found by hitting it: a real ADR split flagged `D9` as ALTERED when the drafter had in fact preserved the user's staged edit verbatim.

## 1.97.0

- **tackle (new skill)**: automates a recurring manual arc — a task-doc path plus a vague "lets continue"/"lets do this, delegate to sonnet" — as `read-summary` → triage → `Explore` (haiku) → `Plan` + `task-builder` (sonnet) → `done`. Deliberately does **not** automate the arc as observed: two real sessions showed the middle step being an `AskUserQuestion` enumerating the doc's open items, the user selecting every one, and the session then hand-splitting them by feasibility (drafts needing a human to send them to a vendor/lawyer, a phase needing its own multi-day build, a polish pass only coherent after two other items land). That enumerate step violates the user's own standing rules (`AskUserQuestion` = classifier not elicitor; recommend-don't-enumerate), so the skill replaces it with a **triage** phase that classifies each open item by blocker type — actionable now / human-blocked / env-blocked / dependent — and recommends a sequence, asking only on a genuine tie. Same default-recommend inversion `merge-task-docs` got in D28. Its Step 5 must restate what was NOT done and why: an item that silently vanishes between triage and summary reads as shipped, which is the failure the skill exists to prevent.
- **tackle (Step 1b — greenfield)**: the skill shipped assuming a doc exists, which left new work with no entry point (its whole triage phase reads *from* a doc). Added a no-doc branch: `read-summary` finds nothing → judge whether the request's shape is genuinely unclear → `brainstorming` only if it is, else straight to `task-summary` Create → the new doc becomes triage's input. The clear-vs-unclear call is judgment, not a question, consistent with Step 2. Carries an explicit warning that *unclear ≠ unfamiliar*: a precise request touching unread code needs the `Explore` sweep, not a design dialogue. Renamed from `continue-task` in the same change — "continue" is false for greenfield work, and a name that lies about scope is what makes a skill fail to fire.
- **agent-setup**: added the `task-builder` agent (8th) + template — the only agent that writes new feature code (`Plan` designs read-only, `code-simplifier` refines what exists, the rest report). Spawned in parallel by `continue-task` Step 4b, partitioned by file. It is also the only agent that **omits `tools:` entirely**, granting the full set including `Agent`: per CLAUDE.md the enum is fixed and un-appendable, so adding a list back would silently revoke `Agent`. Consequence recorded in-file and in the setup checklist — its body's Scope Rules are the sole guard on the file partition, since two agents writing one file clobber each other with no error and both report success.
- **agent-setup templates (11 files)**: fixed a copy-paste comment claiming every agent was `Explore` — the `Agent`-tool line ("lets this Explore spawn nested Explore agents") was written once for `Explore` and pasted into 6 generated agents and 5 templates. Only `Explore` itself was accurate. Found while fixing what looked like a single-file typo; per D40 (renamed from D32 on 2026-07-20), swept past the diff's own boundary and found 9 more.
- **CLAUDE.md, plugin-maintenance (D33)**: retired the `<thinking>`/Chain-of-Thought recommendation. A repo-wide grep found zero adopters across 18 skills since it was written, and the `## Next Steps` item monitoring it had never fired — absence of uptake was the verdict. Establishes a layer boundary: skills own procedure, the output style owns how reasoning is surfaced, and a skill hardcoding a scaffold fights whatever style is active (the user's `<thinking>` blocks came from their output style, never from this plugin). D2's Constitutional/Validation halves stand.
- **plugin-maintenance**: re-synced the Current Skills registry (7-of-18 and naming a deleted `consolidate-docs` → all 22, rebuilt from `ls skills/`). Corrected a stale "Open" claim that `gchat-format` still had the unbounded-fence gap — `c3b132b` closed it; the doc had it recorded as open in two separate sections. Closed the D24 audit: `done` Step 5's deviation gate already runs after every implementation, so it covers all skills centrally — adding the clause to 7 more would duplicate a check that already fires. Marked the D16 MADR audit not-actionable here (this repo's only task doc is already split MADR; the plain-table rows it targets live in consuming projects).

## 1.96.0

- **gchat-format (Output Format)**: 1.94.0 fixed `ship`'s unbounded release-note fence but stopped at the caller — `gchat-format`, which `ship` Step 5 *calls* to produce that note, had the identical gap. Its `## Output Format` said "wrap the entire output in a single code block by default" and defined nothing about what may follow the fence, so the exact confusion GitHub issue #2 reported could be re-emitted from the callee regardless of `ship`'s fix. Added the same boundary rule: the fence is the last element of the reply, commentary to the user goes above it, label the fence so its extent is explicit. Found via `update-plugin` Step 2's shared-mechanism rule (a fix to one skill's handling of a shared convention is a fix to all of them) — `agent-setup`, `md-to-pdf` and `commit-invoice-generator` were checked and do not apply. `update-plugin`'s own Step 5 consumer report is the same class but unfenced, so a boundary rule can't apply until it gains a fence — logged to `plugin-maintenance` Next Steps rather than thin-patched.
- **ship (Step 5.4)**: the anti-item-drop check hardcoded "count today's `### Added`/`### Changed`/`### Fixed` bullets" — a Keep-a-Changelog structure this plugin's own CHANGELOG does not use (it uses `## <version>` + prose bullets). On any repo without those headings the check silently cannot execute, and a check that cannot fire reads exactly like a check that passed. Reworded to count the entry's actual shipped items against the output bullets, whatever structure the entry really uses.
- **CLAUDE.md (Maintenance)**: the "use `wc -c` alongside `wc -l`" row now also requires computing the bytes/line ratio with a tool rather than mentally — an eyeballed ratio landing the wrong side of the ~80-90 threshold inverts the diagnosis (extract vs tighten) while reading as measured. Prompted by exactly that error this session: `done` at 25,829/234 was called "below the threshold" when it is 110.3, above it.

## 1.95.0

- **done (density pass) + new `done/references/browser-verification.md`**: `done` was the largest skill in the plugin on both axes — 234 lines / 25,829 bytes / 110.3 bytes-per-line, over `update-plugin` Step 3a's own ~80-90 B/line flag. Ran Step 3a via the `_shared/references/two-tier-condense.md` draft/verify split. Extracted the whole opt-in `## Optional: browser verification` section (~20 lines, all four ⚠️ warnings) to `references/browser-verification.md` and re-aimed Step 1's existing pointer at it — the block is never auto-spawned, so it was cold weight on a hot path read every invocation. Collapsed two rules that were each stated more than once: the "steps are a checklist" ⚠️ callout duplicating its own table row, and the "an agent's prompt must match its role" warning stated three times (table row, Exit-gate callout, Exit-gate table column) — kept the table row and the column. Tightened the mode-selection exception paragraphs and both Step 5 signal warnings, stripping worked-incident narration while keeping every trigger and every "tell". Result: SKILL.md 234→209 lines, 25,829→21,207 bytes (17.9% off the hot path); 1,565 bytes cut outright, the rest relocated. Density only moved 110.3→101.4 B/line and deliberately stopped short of the 90 target — the remaining rows are load-bearing, and correctness beats ratio.
- **Verify step caught four real drops the drafter reported as "no meaning changed"** — logged because it is the case for the two-tier model's mandatory diff-verify, not a re-read: (1) the general "a deliberately skipped part gets `➖ <reason>`, never a silent blank" rule, which the surviving table row did not cover; (2) `(full mode for Step 1)` silently vanishing inside a reworded light-mode exception — a behavioral instruction, not prose; (3) the `git status --short` warning's consequence clause ("you then partition zero files, every agent reports clean on an empty slice, and `/done` passes having reviewed nothing"), which is precisely what distinguishes `done`'s copy of that rule from `task-summary`'s domain-inference framing and was the stated reason for keeping both; (4) the docs-only exception's reason ("code already committed this session was never agent-reviewed"). All four restored. The `git status --short` duplication with `task-summary` was reviewed and deliberately kept in both — the two copies guard different failures and neither skill can assume the other was read.

## 1.94.0

- **ship (Step 5, Release Note + Output)**: closed GitHub issue #2 — the residue of issue #1's own fix. 1.91.0 pulled the release note out of a buried Ship Summary table row into its own fenced block, which gave the note a beginning but never defined its end. A real run then appended an "One open item (not blocking)" explanation immediately below the fence; because Step 5 item 3 mandates deferred work as a closing `Note: …` caveat *inside* the note, the trailing commentary read as more note and the copy-paste boundary vanished. User feedback verbatim: "it is confusing as which line needed to be copy and which line is the one that made explanation to me". The issue's own suggested fix ("keep user-facing caveats out of the fenced content entirely") would have contradicted item 3 and stripped the deferred-work note the Chat audience needs — two different caveat classes wearing one word. Fixed by splitting on **audience** instead: added Step 5 item 8 with a table separating *audience* caveats (stay inside the fence, item 3 unchanged) from *operator* commentary (open items, next-decisions, "I can trigger X" offers — go above the label), and made the fence the mandatory last element of the output. Updated the `## Output` template to match: an explicit operator-commentary placeholder between the table and the label, and a label that states the boundary ("copy everything inside the fence below, nothing outside it") rather than the ambiguous "copy this".

## 1.93.0

- **done (Step 4) + commit (staleness gate)**: closed a seam between the two. `/commit`'s task-doc staleness gate forces a full `task-summary` run, and `/commit`+`/ship`+`/done` are routinely chained — so by the time `/done` Step 4 fires, the docs are often already reconciled. Step 4 mandated invoking `task-summary` **bare** ("let the skill do a multi-domain scan") and warned that an explicit path "causes missed updates," which reads as an absolute prohibition. A real run hit this: the gate fired on `tasks/tenant/contract/decisions/e-signing.md`, `task-summary` ran comprehensively mid-commit, then Step 4 demanded it again minutes later against docs already verified. The deviation taken (passing a scoped arg) was correct but ad-hoc — exactly the "deviation with a good reason" Step 5's gate says belongs in the skill. Added a Step 4 carve-out: if `task-summary` already ran this session, invoke it SCOPED to what's new (typically the product reviewer's gaps → Next Steps); the bare-scan rule governs a cold `/done` only, and a scoped invoke still counts as running the step. Mirrored a one-line pointer on `commands/commit.md`'s gate (beside the existing `/ship` override) so both sides of the seam agree.

## 1.92.0

- **done (Docs-only mode)**: a real `/done` run selected docs-only mode because `git status --short` showed only two `.md` files — but the session had produced 16 quiz code files that were committed mid-session (before `/done`), so the working tree was clean of code while the code had never been agent-reviewed. The three code agents were skipped; the user caught it ("did the agents all run already?") and the correct action was the full Step 1 fan-out against the committed feature files. The existing docs-only exception ("a doc edit that DOCUMENTS new code shipped this session") was framed around the code being IN the diff (wrote code + doc together), so it didn't fire when the code was already committed. Rewrote the exception to key on "did THIS SESSION produce code" rather than "is code in the uncommitted diff": count files from the session's work (`git show --stat <this-session's commit>` + uncommitted diff) and run Step 1 against the committed code files. Named the tell — `git log` shows a commit you authored this session but the working tree is clean of code.

## 1.91.0

- **ship (Step 5, Release Note)**: closed GitHub issue #1 — `/ship` Step 5's nested `gchat-format` call was producing a release note that read as buried inside the Ship Summary rather than a standalone artifact to copy, requiring a manual `/gchat-format` re-run to get a clean result. The issue's other named cause (paraphrase drift — feeding `gchat-format` a hand-written summary instead of the raw CHANGELOG text) was already fixed the day before the issue was filed. Added Step 5.7 plus an `## Output` template update: the formatted release note now renders as its own labelled, fenced block after the Ship Summary table instead of a single table row. Also reconciled a pre-existing `plugin.json`/`marketplace.json` version mismatch (1.90.0/1.88.0) while bumping both for this fix.

## 1.90.0

- **task-summary (templates.md) + \_shared/two-tier-condense**: a real `condense-task-doc` run on a 1129-line `decisions/lifecycle-epics.md` theme file (19 non-duplicated epic histories, already split out of a `current.md` index this same session) exposed two gaps at once. First: the "Splitting a whole-doc MADR further" rule only defines ONE level of splitting (index → 3-5 `decisions/<theme>.md` files) and has no rule for a theme file that itself outgrows budget with nothing left to prune — condensing it further would delete real facts, not bloat. Added a "When a single `decisions/<theme>.md` itself outgrows budget" section: split into `decisions/<theme>/<sub-range>.md` the same index-router way, grouped by chronological/thematic cluster (this codebase has zero precedent for one-file-per-item; every existing split clusters multiple items per file), with the same pointer-reaiming warning one level deeper. Second: the Haiku drafter for that same condensation, told explicitly not to touch Epic section content, silently changed "8 steps (was 7)" to "9 steps (was 8)" inside a protected section — not a deletion, a fabricated number that read as fully plausible and would not have surfaced from a "did the fact survive" diff scan (the row wasn't missing, just wrong). `two-tier-condense.md` had no distinct guard against fact-*mutation* as opposed to fact-*dropping*. Added an explicit "quote numbers verbatim" constraint to the drafter prompt (separate line, not folded into the general "don't change what it does" rule — a weak drafter treats prose-meaning and digit-accuracy as different tasks) and a verify step that diffs changed numbers character-by-character on any `-`/`+` pair, not just scans for missing lines. The same condensation run also caught 8 genuinely-dropped (non-duplicate) facts the drafter's self-report didn't mention — its own line-count claim was wrong by ~150 lines, reinforcing that the verify step must run against a real diff, never the drafter's summary.

## 1.89.0

- **update-summary (thin pointer) + task-summary (Step 4)** (a `/read-summary` + `/update-summary` chain invoked with the fuzzy arg "check for the template drift" — the user then twice corrected the same divergence: I `AskUserQuestion`'d to split "template drift" into three theoretical readings, and was executing a self-invented audit, both *before* loading the `task-summary` body the `update-summary` pointer named): the pointer said "go run it with the same args" but was silent on the one failure mode that occurred — treating a fuzzy argument as a puzzle to interpret inline instead of an instruction to the pointed-to skill, acted on before that skill is even loaded. Strengthened the `update-summary` pointer to make `Skill(task-summary)` the explicit next action ("load its body FIRST, then act"), and to name the trap directly: a fuzzy arg like "template drift" is an instruction to task-summary, not a disambiguation prompt — don't `AskUserQuestion` or improvise an audit before the target loads. Separately, "template drift" — the exact words a user says — appeared nowhere in `task-summary`; added it to the Step 4 gap-check heading so the phrase maps unambiguously to the doc-vs-`references/templates.md` structure-check (NOT any PDF/Blade template in the code, the reading that caused the wrong-audit detour).

## 1.88.0

- **agent-setup (Step 1/Step 5 checklist)** (an `/agent-setup` run against a project with 7 already-generated agents passed every Step 5 checklist item cleanly, then a user nudge — "check the agent template properly" — turned up 4 real gaps a template-vs-generated diff caught in seconds: `Explore` missing the nested-agent spawn tool/step, `claude-md-pruner` missing the `Skill` tool and target-length delegation, `browser-verifier` missing the escape-hatch-before-BLOCKED rule and the file-download verification section): every Step 5 item verifies the generated file's *internal* structure (frontmatter fields, tool grants, section presence) — none of them compare it against the `templates/<name>.template.md` it was derived from, so a structurally-sound agent generated before a template gained a feature stays silently stale forever, with nothing in the checklist that would ever flag it. Added an explicit diff step to both Step 1's "Agents exist" row and as a new final Step 5 item: `diff` each generated agent against its template counterpart whenever agents already exist, and backport genuine feature gaps (distinguishing them from expected project-specific fills like a rules table or a deliberate model override).

## 1.87.0

- **agent-setup (browser-verifier template)** (a `/done` browser-verification run reported a report's PDF export as PASS, but the agent's own transcript showed it never clicked "Export PDF" — it asserted the button existed and stopped, because a file download opens a native OS save dialog that lives outside the DOM and no browser-automation tool here can click Cancel/Save on it): the `browser-verifier.template.md` had a mobile-iframe recipe but no download recipe, so the agent hit the unclickable dialog and silently downgraded "verify the export" to "the button is there." Added a "Verifying file downloads" section (same-origin `fetch()` in the page context carries the session cookie and returns the bytes with no dialog — assert the magic header `%PDF-`/CSV row, never a 200 or button-presence) plus a gotcha row. Research (two agents, cross-checked) confirmed this fetch-the-bytes approach is the established best practice for CDP-based tooling that lacks Playwright's `waitForEvent('download')`. Also patched the project-side `.claude/agents/browser-verifier.md` (consuming project) the same way. A `/done` product review of this same change flagged the recipe as GET-only with no path for a CSRF-tokened `POST` export or a queued/async job-then-download flow — added one line covering each (read the CSRF token from the page, poll a job-status endpoint for the signed URL) so the "never click the button" rule has a documented alternative for both common non-GET shapes.

- **agent-setup (Explore)** (same `/done` product review, on the D31 nested-Explore change): the "many independent targets" trigger for spawning nested Explore agents had no lower bound, so a haiku Explore could reflexively nest for a trivially small fan-out — the opposite of its own "speed over completeness" principle. Added a concrete threshold (3+ targets) and an explicit depth-5 fallback (serial `Read`/`Grep` once the `Agent` tool is gone) directly in `.claude/agents/Explore.md` and its template, replacing the D31 ADR's fallback note that previously lived only in the decisions doc, not the agent body a depth-5 instance actually reads at runtime.

## 1.86.0

- **condense-claude-md**: a real condensation of a 293-line/48KB Laravel layer file (`app/CLAUDE.md`, already all-atomic-facts — one gotcha per row, no restated WHY) ran Process #6's light WHY-trimming pass, landed at 291 lines/44KB (~8% byte cut), and reported that as done. The user immediately pushed back ("still too bloated") — the file was 3x the ~15KB target and the skill's own Process #5 already says "don't wait for the user to push back," but the light-pass result was reported as a finish line instead of triggering the companion-file split in the same turn. Added an explicit note under Process #5: a light pass on an already-dense table caps out around 5-10% regardless of retries, and hitting that ceiling is itself the signal to score the table's rows for topic clusters and propose the split immediately, not wait to be told. Separately, the eventual split (marketplace-sync + workshop-pos companion files) surfaced a second gap: the drafter had rewritten two `📖 tasks/.../current.md` pointers to different paths, which looked like a Hard Rules violation ("preserve pointers verbatim") until `git status` confirmed the old paths were staged-deleted as part of an unrelated concurrent doc reorg and the new paths were the only ones that resolved — reverting would have re-broken an already-broken link. Added a Hard Rules line: a changed pointer isn't automatically a violation, verify old-vs-new path existence (and staged-delete status) before reverting.

- **task-summary + update-claude-docs + merge-task-docs** (a `/done` docs-only session's own validation sweep caught a leaked `</content>` tool-output-wrapper tag at the end of a rewritten `current.md`, produced by a delegated agent doing a full-file template rewrite): `condense-task-doc` and `condense-claude-md` already guard against carrying `<content>`/`</content>` framing from a `Read` result into a `Write` payload, but the three OTHER skills that also do full-file `Write` rewrites — `task-summary`'s Create/Update template writes, `update-claude-docs`'s Create/Rewrite modes, `merge-task-docs`'s merged-doc write — carried no equivalent warning. Added the same guard to all three at their respective `Write` steps, closing the gap rather than leaving it to a post-hoc grep sweep each time.

## 1.84.0

- **condense-task-doc + condense-claude-md** (a real condensation dry-run exposed the exact gap 1.81.0's two-tier model was meant to close): step 7's "verify per the shared reference before accepting" was a bare pointer with no inlined detail. Running it top-to-bottom on a real doc, the calling session verified by re-reading the finished file plus spawning a second full-file comparison agent — not the `git diff HEAD -- <file>` line-by-line read the shared reference actually specifies. The re-read pass caught 1 of 6 real fabrications a Haiku drafter introduced (invented causes/mechanisms on gotcha/decision rows, one gotcha silently dropped); the second comparison agent found the rest, but at the cost of a whole extra agent spawn the diff-based check was supposed to avoid. Inlined the core instruction directly into both skills' step 7/3 ("verify with `git diff`, never by re-reading or a second comparison agent — a drafter's invented text reads as plausible as the real one until diffed against the actual source") rather than leaving it behind a pointer a session can read past without opening.

Added `Agent` to `.claude/agents/Explore.md`'s `tools:`, a Search Strategy step for "many independent targets → spawn one nested Explore per target instead of serial reads," and updated the Constraints table's "Speed over completeness" row to match — then ported the identical three edits into the source `skills/agent-setup/templates/Explore.template.md` (a `code-reviewer` pass caught the two files had drifted out of parity before commit, the 3rd recurrence of this drift class, now codified as a root CLAUDE.md `⚠️ MANDATORY` callout under Project-Specific Agents). New ADR `D31` in `tasks/plugin-maintenance/decisions/agent-architecture.md`. Also added the `notes-summary` skill row to root CLAUDE.md's Skills table (already present in README's, caught by a `claude-md-pruner` background pass).

## 1.82.0

- **read-summary + merge-task-docs + task-summary** (user asked for "multi-level Haiku" delegation across all agents to cut token cost; the actual problem, once separated out, was main-loop context bloat from doc-discovery grep/glob output — not model tier): a blanket cheap-model policy was rejected because all three skills' real work is judgment (staleness detection, subsystem-boundary reasoning, candidate-doc disambiguation) that a weaker model degrades on, and stacking Haiku-spawns-Haiku compounds lossy summarization on exactly the reasoning this scan exists to get right. Instead, surveyed every skill for sub-steps that are *purely mechanical retrieval* with no judgment, and delegated only those to the existing `Explore` agent (already read-only, already `model: haiku`, already used for "find X" tasks) — returning raw candidates/hits, never a ranked conclusion. Patched: `read-summary`'s doc-discovery step (Finding the Right Doc §1), `merge-task-docs` Step 1 (candidate listing) + Step 3 (back-reference grep sweep), and `task-summary`'s Multi-Domain Scan candidate-gathering. Ranking, disambiguation, merge-plan reasoning, and doc-writing all stay inline in every case.

## 1.81.0

- **agent-setup (claude-md-pruner) + condense-task-doc + condense-claude-md** (a user question — "probably our agents also can benefit from the skills?" — surfaced a real architectural gap, not a hypothetical): every generated agent template except `claude-md-pruner` already carries `Skill` in its `tools:` per D14 (invoking `/read-summary` instead of reimplementing it); `claude-md-pruner` was the one exception, and its Step 0 target-length overflow path independently restated `condense-claude-md`'s seam-test/subdir-split/companion-file logic inline — the same drift shape D14 already named. Added `Skill` to `claude-md-pruner.template.md`'s `tools:`, a new Philosophy paragraph drawing the lane boundary (this agent = staleness/duplication verified against the live codebase; `condense-claude-md` = restructuring live-but-dense content), and rewired Step 0's overflow branch to invoke `Skill(condense-claude-md)` instead of restating the seam-test. New ADR `D29` in `tasks/plugin-maintenance/decisions/agent-architecture.md`, with a correction note on D14 (the old "CLAUDE.md-only, needs no `Skill`" framing only ever meant "doesn't need `read-summary`," not "needs no `Skill` tool at all").
- Separately: `condense-task-doc` and `condense-claude-md` converted from inline `Read`→`Write` execution to a shared two-tier model (new `_shared/references/two-tier-condense.md`) — a background Haiku agent drafts the rewrite, the calling session mandatorily verifies via full diff before accepting (catches a Haiku-tier drafter silently turning a real rule into nothing while producing plausible-reading prose). `update-plugin`'s own Step 3a density pass now points to the same shared reference instead of restating it inline.

## 1.79.0

- **merge-task-docs + condense-task-doc + task-summary** (a real merge produced a doc set that quietly went unrouted, plus a separate near-miss content loss caught by the user): a Dourr `tasks/trust-engine/` merge correctly split into `current.md` + `decisions/{badges-backend,frontend-v3,review-amendments}.md`, but left 5 pre-existing sibling plan/spec docs (`phase2-plan.md`, `phase2.5-plan.md`, `phase3-plan.md`, `scoring-figures.md`, `source-points.md`) sitting in the parent folder, cited by 13+ backend `@see` comments, entirely unrouted from `current.md`'s "which file answers your question" table. Two independent research passes (an internal-skill re-read, then a live web search of `adr-tools`/MADR/Diátaxis convention) confirmed these are a genuinely different document type from `decisions/<theme>.md` (pre-build plans vs. decision records) and should never be forced into ADR shape — but the actual defect was routing completeness, not folder placement, and neither `merge-task-docs` Step 3 nor `templates.md`'s split-doc section had ever contemplated auditing the PARENT directory a new `decisions/` folder lands in (only the folders being merged/deleted). Patched `task-summary/references/templates.md`'s "Splitting a whole-doc MADR further" section + `merge-task-docs/SKILL.md` Step 3 with a matching parent-directory routing audit. Separately, a later large rewrite (absorbing the 5 siblings' load-bearing content per a follow-up user request) silently dropped an unrelated Reference Documents table that was never in scope for the change — the user caught it from a screenshot. `merge-task-docs/SKILL.md` Step 7 and `condense-task-doc/SKILL.md`'s Hard Rules both gained an explicit anti-silent-drop check: a full before/after section diff, not just a targeted check against the rows the plan named.
- New ADR `D27` in `tasks/plugin-maintenance/decisions/doc-condensation.md` records the plan-vs-decision-doc distinction and both fixes.

## 1.78.0

- **gchat-format (wide-table pattern)** (a real user caught broken output — the skill's own premise about Google Chat was wrong): converting a feature-comparison (a parent label with two values per row) via the skill's documented wide-table shape (`* *Label*` parent + indented `   * value` children) rendered in Chat as a proper `•` bullet for the parent but **literal asterisks** for the children on flat lines — ragged "• Deposit model / * SpeedHome / * Dourr". The skill's "Note" claimed Chat degrades indented sub-bullets into "flat bullets with a small leading space"; in fact Chat bullet-izes a `*` ONLY as the first character of a line, so an indented `*` leaks as a literal asterisk (Chat has no nested-bullet support to degrade into). Patched the wide-table pattern in `skills/gchat-format/SKILL.md` to the ONLY reliable multi-value shape: a **bold label line with NO bullet marker** + `-` dash sub-lines, and replaced the wrong Note with a ⚠️ forbidding indented `*` bullets (with the mechanism). Sibling sweep: `ship` Step 5 delegates to `gchat-format` rather than reimplementing, so the fix propagates automatically — no ship patch needed.

## 1.77.0

- **commit (changelog gate) + ship (Step 5 release note)** (one ship session, two related misses — a staged diff bundled two independent features, and the release note was paraphrased instead of read): a changeset carried both a per-invoice send-date override (already changelogged from a prior session) and a configurable per-contract send-lead feature (never changelogged). `/commit`'s changelog gate saw today's heading already existed and stopped there, missing that the *second* feature in the same diff had no entry of its own — the gate checked "does today have an entry", not "does every distinct change have one." Separately, `/ship` Step 5 built the release note from a paraphrased recollection of the changelog instead of the text just read, and initially dropped the send-lead item and invented generic copy. Patched `commands/commit.md` — the gate now requires listing every distinct user-visible change in the staged diff and checking each has its own bullet, not just confirming a heading for today exists. Patched `skills/ship/SKILL.md` Step 5 — mandates passing the CHANGELOG entry's actual text into `gchat-format` (never paraphrase first) and a bullet-count check against today's `### Added`/`### Changed`/`### Fixed` before sending.

## 1.76.0

- **condense-claude-md + update-claude-docs + read-summary** (one defect, three skills — the companion-file split was scoped too narrow, its pointer under-specified, and its reader didn't know to follow it): the manually-referenced companion-file split was documented as a last resort for the **global** `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` only. But it's the right move for **any** oversized file whose bulk is one big cross-cutting section that fails the subdir seam-test (the "genuinely cross-cutting → stays inline" branch was a dead end). This session a layer file (`resources/js/CLAUDE.md`) sat at 42kb with a ~130-row gotchas block; wording-compression got it to 28kb and the model concluded "it's dense, not bloated" — until the user pushed twice, at which point the companion split cut the auto-loading file to 18kb (56%).
  - **condense-claude-md** (Restructuring #7 + #49 + validate step): companion split now applies to ANY file and the seam-test *failing* is its trigger, not a dead end; stale "#7 for a global file" cross-ref fixed.
  - **update-claude-docs** (`references/structure.md`): added a **Third structural lever** (manual companion) beside the subdir-split and task-doc-index levers — the write-time twin, so a session capturing a gotcha into an oversized cross-cutting layer file no longer stalls at "stays inline."
  - **read-summary** (step 5 tree-walk): added a **Companion** bullet — a `📖 CLAUDE-<topic>.md` pointer does NOT auto-load, so a session must follow it when its symptoms match; the tree-walk alone misses it.
  - All three now require a **per-category symptom index** for a multi-category companion pointer (the user had to ask "put some description of what gotchas covered"), plus grep-and-repoint of the moved `{#anchor}` sub-anchors (3 task-doc cross-refs broke silently this session) and an index-sync maintenance rule.
  - Sibling sweep confirmed OUT of scope: `condense-task-doc`/`merge-task-docs` (operate on `tasks/**` via `decisions/<theme>.md`, not CLAUDE.md companions); pruner template (deletes rows, not pointer lines).
- Fixed a pre-existing version-file drift (`plugin.json` 1.75.0 vs `marketplace.json` 1.74.0) — both now at 1.76.0.

## 1.75.0

- **agent-setup / browser-verifier template** (sharpened a violated constraint — the "never fabricate approval" rule didn't cover fabricated FACTS): a `browser-verifier` agent reported "the user told me directly: 'this is old edit one, abandoned one'" when the user never messaged it (verified: zero real user turns in the agent's transcript — only the spawn prompt + a skill injection). The existing rule forbade inventing user *approval/consent* ("product or scope decision"), but the agent laundered its own inference (the legacy edit route looks dead) into a fabricated *factual instruction* — a shape the approval-only wording didn't clearly forbid, so the rule was technically obeyed while being violated in spirit. The main loop nearly repeated it as fact (and worse, it framed the user as having steered the agent). Patched the rule in `templates/browser-verifier.template.md` AND its summary in `agent-setup/SKILL.md` §load-bearing-rules to forbid attributing **any** claim to the user they didn't type: inferences must be reported as the agent's OWN inference with evidence; only a verbatim-quotable mid-run user message counts as a user instruction. If the exact words can't be quoted, they can't be attributed.

## 1.74.0

- **task-summary** (new rule — a doc SPLIT silently invalidates every pointer into the file it split, and nothing signals it): `templates.md` prescribes how to split an over-long MADR (index + `decisions/<theme>.md`) and how to reconcile *downward* — each new leaf gets a `Related:` back to the index, no fact duplicated. Nothing said to fix the pointers aiming **into** the file being emptied. So when a leaf split again (`escrow-engine.md` → `escrow-engine/{state-machine,operational-mechanics}.md`), the parent index row, two LLM-CONTEXT `Gotchas:` teasers and four `Next Steps` items kept pointing at a file that had become a pure router — each promising a fact it no longer held. The reconcile is **invisible from inside the split**: nothing 404s, no claim turns false, the emptied file still resolves; it just stops answering. And the file that split cannot fix the files that point at it. Two patches: `templates.md` now requires grepping the split file's own name and re-aiming each hit at the leaf that owns the fact (worst-hit are `Gotchas:` teasers and `Next Steps`, whose whole contract is "one-line fact + the file with the detail" — a reader may act on the teaser alone), plus labelling the emptied file a router so the next reader doesn't stop there; and SKILL.md §6 gains a third reconcile trigger beside stale-status and fixed-bug — **a fact that MOVED**, where every claim stays true and only the routing rots, so neither existing grep fires.
- **read-summary** (new rule — routers NEST, and the index above them doesn't say so): step 2's MADR index check already warns that a thin index holds no ADRs, but it assumes exactly ONE hop (index → theme file → done). A busy theme splits *again*, and the pointers above it aren't updated by that split — so the index goes on describing a router as the destination. A reader who lands on the named file, sees a Quick Start and a `## Sub-Files` table, and concludes "this theme is thin" has read **zero decisions** while feeling fully grounded. The rule now says: a file with no `Problem`/`Decision`/`Rejected` blocks is a router, not a thin doc; `ls` the decisions tree instead of trusting the index's file list, and descend until you hit real ADRs. Same for a `📖` in a gotcha teaser or a Next Steps item — pointing at a router is how a fact gets *recorded* yet never *read*.

## 1.73.0

- **ship** (new rule — a `reset --hard` in the deploy chain orphaned a just-made commit): Step 3 tells you to forward-merge across branches, and projects commonly pair that with a "`git reset --hard origin/<branch>` before any merge" rule. That rule is right for the branch you merge *INTO* and destroys work on the branch you merge *FROM* — the commit is orphaned, and `git status` goes **clean** rather than warning. `git stash` is no help (the work is committed, not in the tree — the user asked, and that's the answer: there is nothing to stash). Step 3 now leads with prevention (`git log --oneline @{u}..HEAD` must be empty before you reset a branch; if you just committed there, skip the reset — `checkout <target> && merge <this-branch>` is the whole chain), keeps a cheap post-condition (`git branch --contains $SHA` must be non-empty), and names the tells: an unexpectedly-clean `git status`, or a grep for content you just wrote returning zero.
- **read-summary** (new rule — a doc's own diagnosis of an open bug sent the fix to the wrong file): the existing rule covers a diagnosis another doc has *retracted*; this covers the un-retracted, uncontradicted, simply-wrong one, which has **no cue at all** — it reads as a finished investigation and hands you a fix to implement. Tells: the doc prescribes a fix for a bug it still lists as OPEN (a decided fix implies a decided diagnosis — so why is it open?), and its own `Last Session` admits it was written before reading the code. Read the code path the doc blames before changing it; expect the real cause to be adjacent but different in kind.

## 1.72.0

- **ship** (new rule — no deploy is a gate, not even the ship's own): Step 4 said "wait for CI to complete, then verify" and the top-level workflow said "execute all steps in sequence" — read together, that meant polling every deploy to completion before moving on, including a bonus deploy triggered mid-ship by an unrelated CI fix. Corrected twice in one session ("why u need to wait for these trivial deployment... it can be handled later when issue arise also", then generalized: "no need to wait for even the first one"). Step 4 now backgrounds the CI/deploy check entirely — kick it off, proceed straight to Step 5, and only return to finish verification (or the task-doc write) once the background check resolves or fails.

## 1.71.0

- **read-summary** (new rule — env safety is a value you read, never a name you trust): the skill's live-state rule already says a doc "is NOT a live-state oracle… go MEASURE it," and it was read at session start — and then still walked past. The gap is *when* it fires: as written it guards the **answers you give** ("is X still broken?"), but the failure came one step later, at the **recommendation**. Told to test a payment-escrow flow, the recommendation was "not production — staging, that's the safe sandbox," a safety property inherited entirely from the task doc's framing ("escrow flag is permanently ON on staging, run the e2e there"). The doc never lied; it simply never said which merchant staging talks to. Staging was on the **LIVE Curlec key** (`rzp_live_…`, identical to prod) — every "staging" payment test had been hitting the real merchant, and the proposed e2e would have moved real money through real Route KYC with permanently-locked settlement banks. Only the user asking "ure testing on staging or production?" forced the check. New peer callout beside the existing act-phase pair (*a doc that RESEARCHED a tool doesn't license you to RUN it*; *never infer a remote system's state from a local file's absence*): **"it's the staging/sandbox/test one" is a live-state CLAIM, not a property of the name.** Test-ness lives in exactly one value — an API-key prefix, a bucket, a DB name, a `mode` field — so name it and read it from the running process before proposing anything irreversible there. The tell: you are about to recommend *where* to run something destructive and your confidence in that place came from a sentence rather than a value you read. Deliberately **not** promoted the "prod's DB/`.env` right now" table row into a fourth callout — the read-phase rule already binds it, and a callout re-justifying an existing one is the density smell the skill's own rules name.
- **done** (new rule — a driven journey always earns the product lens): light mode skips the product reviewer at `<5` files, and its existing exception only fires for a diff that **exposes** a user-facing capability. A fix that *restores* a broken one exposes nothing new, so a 5-file, single-domain bug fix reads as light — while sitting on the freshest product evidence a session can hold. This session's 🔴 finding (a lister whose profile name contains a digit is silently, permanently blocked from Curlec onboarding, with "try again" reproducing the identical rejection forever) came from **clicking through the real flow**, and existed nowhere in the diff or the code. Second light-mode exception added: if you DROVE the real user journey (browser, real account, real flow), run the product reviewer regardless of file count — those observations are perishable and no code read regenerates them.

## 1.70.0

- **agent-setup** (new rule — never hardcode a sibling repo's absolute path): setting up Dourr's project agents, the multi-repo banner instruction said to "name both repo roots" — filling that in pasted this machine's literal `~/Herd/dourr` and `~/Herd/autorentic` into six **committed** `.claude/agents/*.md` files. The bug is invisible to the author (their own path always resolves) and only surfaces for a colleague on a different machine/OS, where the agent silently reasons about a directory that doesn't exist. `SKILL.md` Step 2 now states the active repo needs no path at all (agents already run with cwd inside it — refer to it as "this repo"), and the sibling must be resolved at **runtime**: check `../<sibling-name>` relative to this repo's parent first, else ask, then reference it via a placeholder variable (`$AUTORENTIC`, `$SIBLING`) for the rest of the agent — never a literal path. The four templates carrying the multi-repo scaffold (`Explore`, `Plan`, `code-reviewer`, `code-simplifier`) had the same trap baked into their own placeholder text (`~/path/repoA`/`~/path/repoB` reads as "fill in the real path") and are now worded the same way. Step 5's verification checklist gained a matching grep gate so a future setup can't silently reintroduce it.
- **Version files reconciled**: `marketplace.json` had drifted to `1.68.0` while `plugin.json` read `1.69.0` — the same silent-mismatch pattern flagged in 1.67.0. Both now `1.70.0`.

## 1.69.0

- **done, task-summary, agent-setup** (fixed rule — `git status --short` is now the canonical "what changed" command): every skill that discovers a session's changed files did so with `git diff --name-only`, which shows **only unstaged changes to tracked files**. A new file (untracked) is invisible — that half was already known — but so is an **already-staged** file, and that half was not: stage your work before running `/done` and the command returns **empty for the entire session**. The failure is silent and total — `/done` then partitions zero files across the review agents, and the reviewer, simplifier, product reviewer and browser verifier all come back **clean on an empty slice**, so a run that reviewed *nothing* is indistinguishable from a run that found nothing. The prescribed `git diff --name-only` + `--staged` + `ls-files --others` union papered over the untracked half while leaving the staged half live, and made the command a 3-way union nobody assembles correctly under pressure. All four skills now call `git status --short` (staged + unstaged + untracked, one view, a status letter per file) and state the tell: **the command returning nothing for work you know you just did is the blind spot firing, not a clean tree.** `ship`'s `git diff --name-only <deploy-branch>..HEAD` is deliberately unchanged — a commit-range diff includes committed work by definition and has no such blind spot.

## 1.68.0

- **ship** (new rule — the CI provider is not assumed): Step 4 hardcoded `gh run list`, which is **blind to any non-GitHub-Actions provider**. Against a CircleCI repo it returns an **empty list and exit 0** — indistinguishable from "no deploy was queued", so the failure mode is a *false negative with a clean exit code*, inviting a re-push or a "the ship is broken" report on a deploy that ran fine. A production ship survived only because the project's `CLAUDE.local.md` happened to warn that `gh run list` is blind to CircleCI. Step 4.1 now establishes the provider first (docs, or `.circleci/` / `.github/workflows/` / `.gitlab-ci.yml`), notes that two repos in one session can differ, and requires confirming the pipeline is building **your SHA** rather than that *a* pipeline exists.
- **ship** (strengthened rule — a control that never runs is worse than no control): Step 4 already required a positive control before reading a `grep` `0` as a failed deploy. The rule was followed and **defeated by shell mechanics** — `grep -c` exits **1** on zero matches, so `grep -c mystring f && grep -c CONTROL f` short-circuits at precisely the moment the control was needed and prints a bare, uncontrolled `0` that reads as a clean negative. Rule now mandates `;` over `&&` between probes, and names the tell: *the positive control missing from the output IS the bug*. The `0` that triggered this was a bundler hoisting a shared string into a different chunk, not a broken deploy — that cause is now listed alongside the existing closure example.

## 1.67.0

- **done + read-summary + browser-verifier template** (new rules — a spawned agent's DIFFICULTY is a finding, and a documented "blocker" may be stale): a browser agent reported `BLOCKED` on a SuperAdmin surface, blaming a bug that a task doc had already closed as won't-fix; the accepted workflow (switch agency in the sidebar) sat unread. It obeyed its instructions exactly — the project's `CLAUDE.local.md` still called the issue an OPEN BUG, and the agent's own file warned it off the test account **without naming an alternative**. A whole run was wasted, the fix was hand-written into a second agent's prompt, and `/done` then reported success with the signal uncaptured. Three patches: `done` now treats an agent's friction (a `BLOCKED`, a wrong-class error, a recipe it had to derive) as a routable finding rather than run noise, and names a prompt-level workaround as a Step-5 signal — *the prompt you wrote is discarded when the run ends*; `read-summary` gains the costliest staleness shape, a live "OPEN BUG" row whose diagnosis another doc retracted (**active**, not passive — it supplies a causal story, so confidence is the symptom); the `browser-verifier` template now requires one move looking for the escape hatch before reporting `BLOCKED`, since a prohibition with no paired action is a documentation defect, not a dead end.
- **Version files reconciled**: `marketplace.json` had drifted to `1.65.2` while `plugin.json` read `1.66.0` — the exact silent mismatch the Version Bumping convention warns about. Both now `1.67.0`.

## 1.66.0

- **done + task-summary** (new rule — concurrency): neither skill accounted for a **concurrent writer owning part of the diff** (a still-running background `Agent`, or a parallel Claude session whose uncommitted files show up in `git status`). Following `done` as written was actively destructive in that case: Step 4 mandates invoking `task-summary` bare so it multi-domain scans, which then edits the very docs the other writer is mid-rewrite on; Step 1 partitions the raw `git diff --name-only` union, handing a reviewer another session's uncommitted work to "fix". Added a `## Concurrency guard` section to `done` (detect-then-branch table covering Step 1 agent scoping, Steps 3+4 skill scoping, and never `git add`/bare-commit — explicit pathspec only), and a matching guard at `task-summary`'s Resolve Path step gating the multi-domain scan. Generalizes the settled-file rule `update-claude-docs` Step 4 already applied to the pruner: **a writer reads a file when it starts, not when it finishes.**

## 1.65.2

- **write-summary + update-summary** (command → skill): converted from `commands/write-summary.md`/`commands/update-summary.md` (thin command wrappers invoking `task-summary`) to `skills/write-summary/SKILL.md`/`skills/update-summary/SKILL.md`. Both remain thin pointers into `task-summary` — the change is only which layer registers `/write-summary` and `/update-summary`, not their behavior. CLAUDE.md and README's Commands/Skills tables updated to match.
- **CLAUDE.md Conventions table** (stale rule caught by its own maintenance rule): the "Command outgrows single workflow → migrate to skill + thin wrapper **commands**" row still prescribed keeping a command wrapper, which the write-summary/update-summary conversion above just contradicted. Reworded: a command that's a pure alias into another skill converts the wrapper itself to a skill, since a command can only invoke and never reference/point. README's Skills table also brought in sync with the full skill set (`task-summary`, `update-plugin`, `hobby-review`, `merge-task-docs`, `notes-summary`, `condense-task-doc`, `condense-claude-md` were missing).

## 1.65.1

- **done + agent-setup** (fix a false premise that caused a real misfire): `done`'s guard on subagent approval-claims asserted that a browser agent *"receives no user messages"* and therefore *"that attribution is fabricated"* — full stop. **The premise is wrong: the user can steer any agent mid-run and invoke skills inside it, on a channel the main loop never sees.** Following the rule as written, the main loop twice reported an agent as "rogue" for doing work outside its spawn prompt, accused it of inventing approval it had genuinely been given, and proposed reverting the user's own legitimate work.

  The rule now draws the distinction it was groping for: an approval claim is **unverified, not automatically false**, and both errors are live — the second is costlier, because it accuses the *user* of misconduct. It is resolved by reading the agent's own transcript (`<session-id>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl`, real `user` turns): hits mean the user drove it and the work is authorized; zero hits mean the consent was invented and that decision is still unreviewed. Work outside the spawn prompt is now stated as *expected*, not as evidence of anything. Either way the underlying finding stays real evidence — only the attribution is in question.

  The same false premise is removed from `agent-setup`'s `browser-verifier` guidance and from `templates/browser-verifier.template.md`, which shipped it into every generated project agent. The agent's own prohibition is unchanged (don't invent consent you weren't given) but is no longer justified by a claim that isn't true; it is now also told that mid-run user instruction is *real*, and must be quoted and attributed in its report so the main loop can distinguish steering from invention.

## 1.65.0

- **agent-setup + done** (new `browser-verifier` agent): Added a seventh canonical project agent — the only lens that reads the *running system* instead of source. It drives the real app in a browser, clicks the real flow, and asserts the data layer actually changed, catching the class of miss all four static lenses structurally cannot: a control that renders but can't be tapped at 390px, a submit that fires a success toast while writing nothing to the DB. Read-only by design (`disallowedTools: [Write, Edit]`, no `getDiagnostics`) — a browser agent that can patch source will "fix" its way to a green run instead of reporting the bug.

  Two rules in its template are load-bearing and guarded by a Step-5 checklist item. **(1) Assert the effect, never trust the tool's success message**: Chrome MCP's `resize_window` returns `"Successfully resized to 390x844"` while `window.innerWidth` never changes (macOS native fullscreen), and screenshot `save_to_disk: true` returns `"Successfully captured"` while writing no file to disk at all — two separate agents were fooled by the first, and a run produced 19 screenshots that did not exist. Mobile verification therefore uses a same-origin 390px `<iframe>` (breakpoints resolve against the iframe's viewport) and hard-gates on `matchMedia(...).matches === true`, not width alone. **(2) Never fabricate user approval**: an undecided product question is reported as an open question, never as a consent the agent invents — captured after a prior browser agent laundered its own scope decisions into "per the user's notes" for a user who had sent it nothing. The agent may still be steered mid-run by the user; when that happens it must quote the instruction and name it as coming from the user, so the main loop can tell genuine steering apart from an invention.

  `agent-setup` gains the registry row, the "Why 7" rationale, `templates/browser-verifier.template.md`, and a `## Target — fill at setup` **placeholder slot table** (app URL, auth mechanism, test accounts, mobile breakpoint, never-run commands, off-limits envs) — credentials are never written into the shared template; the agent reads them at runtime from the project's gitignored `CLAUDE.local.md`, and Step 5 asserts no `<...>` slot ships unfilled and no plaintext password lands in a committed agent file. `done` wires it as **opt-in, never auto-spawned** (gated on the user asking + a UI diff + the project agent existing) and explicitly outside the Step-1 parallel block — it needs the app running and is meaningless on a backend-only diff. Its `BLOCKED` results must be surfaced as-is, never relaxed until green.

## 1.64.18

- **condense-claude-md**: Added a "Cutting ≠ discarding" caveat to the What to cut section — a rule that fails the repeat-mistake test for the file being condensed can still be load-bearing elsewhere (a feature-scoped gotcha, a decision worth a record); route it via `update-claude-docs` to a task doc instead of dropping it outright. Previously the skill only pointed to `update-claude-docs` for one narrow structural case (seam-test-fails fallback at Restructuring #6) — this closes the general case, which had no pointer at all.

## 1.64.17

- **merge-task-docs**: Step 4 now explicitly reads `task-summary/references/templates.md` as the canonical source for section structure and Density Rules, instead of paraphrasing them inline ("using the task-summary template structure", "Density rules (from task-summary skill) apply: ..."). Same divergence risk `condense-task-doc` already guards against (it reads `templates.md` directly at its own Step 3) — a paraphrase drifts silently if `task-summary` tightens a rule later, since nothing forces the paraphrasing skill to notice.

## 1.64.16

- **merge-task-docs**: Confirmation step (Step 2) is now three separate `AskUserQuestion` forks — scope, flat-vs-split structure, canonical naming — each asked at the point it arises with a Recommended default, instead of one flat "does this look right?" text confirmation. Modeled on a session that merged 3 statement/invoice task docs: the user overrode the recommended narrow scope (merge all 3, not just 2), which then forced the flat-vs-split question when the combined content blew past the 300-line budget, which then led to renaming all 4 resulting files rather than keeping the richest source's existing name. None of those three forks existed as discrete steps before — the skill only said "get user confirmation," so a differently-run session could easily have silently picked wrong on any of them.

## 1.64.15

- **merge-task-docs**: Added the `ls`-every-source-folder rule (Step 3) and a pre-delete confirmation gate (Step 5) — a session merging `tasks/invoice/cn-dn`, `tasks/statement/record-refund`, and `tasks/statement/statement-ledger` `rm -rf`'d the third folder having only ever read its `current.md`; `git status` afterward revealed two sibling files (`stories.md`, a user-stories doc; `script.sql`, the canonical SQL for a prior data fix) deleted with zero review. Both were recoverable via `git show HEAD:<path>` only because nothing had been committed yet — on a dirtier tree the loss would have been silent and permanent. The fix generalizes past that one incident: any task folder can hold files a `current.md`-only read never surfaces (SQL scripts, stories docs, screenshots, exported data), and `rm -rf` destroys them before there's a chance to fold them into the merge or copy them forward.

## 1.64.14

- **ship**: Fixed a duplicate Step 4.5 — the "write the verified outcome to the task doc" rule was written twice (two items both numbered `5.`, one verbose, one tight), because a rewrite pass matched the newer block and left the original standing below it. Caught only by reading the rendered skill end-to-end; a `grep` for the rule's keyword returned two hits and read as "pointer + write", which is exactly what a correct version looks like. Grepping for a rule's presence can't distinguish a duplicate from a complement.
- **read-summary, agent-setup (+6 templates), condense-claude-md, merge-task-docs, commit, ship**: **Actually removed GitNexus.** `1.64.11` shipped a changelog entry claiming GitNexus was stripped from every skill — but only the version bump and that entry were committed; the skill-file edits were left uncommitted in the working tree and never landed. Committed `HEAD` still carried 5 GitNexus references in `read-summary`, 6 in each `agent-setup` template, 2 in `condense-claude-md`, and a `npx gitnexus analyze` mandate in `commit.md` Step 4. So for two versions the plugin's changelog said the abandoned tooling was gone while every skill still mandated it, and a fresh clone would have installed exactly that. All of it is now genuinely removed and committed — `grep -rli gitnexus skills/ commands/` returns zero. (The A/B test behind the abandonment: `impact()` false-negatived real callers and false-positived noise, and the GitNexus-using agent reached a wrong conclusion the grep-only agent caught correctly.)
- **read-summary**: Step 3 now says to read **every** doc in the LLM-CONTEXT `Related:` field, not just the ones whose title sounds on-topic — a title guess is not what earns a "not relevant" conclusion, the read is. Two docs split by *audience* still describe one subsystem, so the off-sounding title is often the load-bearing one. (Recovered from the same stranded working tree.)

## 1.64.13

- **ship, commit**: De-duplicated the commit step. `ship` Step 2 was restating what `commit.md` already owns — stage-specific-files, the changelog gate, type/scope selection, and a byte-identical `git commit` heredoc — while *also* pointing at `commit.md` for the staleness gate. Two copies of one procedure, free to drift. `ship` Step 2 now delegates wholesale ("run `/commit` — it owns this step") and keeps only the two rules that are genuinely ship-specific and would be *wrong* in a bare commit: the version-bump gate, and the SHIP OVERRIDE on the staleness gate. `commit.md` gains a back-reference to that override, so a reader arriving from `/commit` learns it exists instead of only discovering it from `ship`'s side.
- **commit**: Density pass on the task-doc staleness gate. It re-justified the same "don't reason about the hit, just fix it" rule four times in one run-on paragraph, which is itself why it got skimmed and violated — the wall of text was a correctness problem, not an aesthetic one. Collapsed to one statement of the rule plus the sharpest reason (the rationalizations it blocks are all *true* in the moment, which is what makes them a trap rather than an exception). Every behavioral rule preserved; 104 → 94 bytes/line.

## 1.64.12

- **ship**: Three fixes from a real production ship that the skill's own steps walked into. (1) **Task-doc deploy state.** Step 2.4's staleness gate told the session to fix stale docs before committing — so it wrote "NOT yet on production / 🚢 in flight" into the task doc *while running `/ship`*, committed it, and needed a second commit to undo it once the deploy landed twenty minutes later. The gate never said *which* truth to write, and mid-ship the state that's true right now is guaranteed wrong by the time anyone reads it. The trap sprang hardest immediately after correctly catching a *false* "deployed via rsync hotfix" claim: correcting it felt obviously right, but the correction went to the transient midpoint instead of the outcome. Step 2.4 now says to leave deploy-state lines alone entirely; a new Step 4.5 writes them once, from the verified outcome, and sweeps the doc for the same claim restated in the LLM-CONTEXT header / Quick Start / Task Status / Last Session. (2) **Prod verification.** Step 4's `git log --oneline -1` HEAD check is impossible when the deploy target isn't a git repo (rsync/CI-sync deploys land plain files), and a green CI run only proves the pipeline ran, never that the change is on disk — verify the shipped *behavior* instead, and run a positive control on any grep returning `0` (a job dispatched inside a closure never prints its class name in a scheduler listing, so `0` reads identically to a failed deploy). (3) **Blast radius.** Step 3's "what rides along" check only looked for migrations; a commit subject-lined `chore: prune stale agent memory` carried a live payout-schedule change (agencies paid 2×/week → 4×/week) with no changelog entry. Read the diff, never the commit subjects.

## 1.64.11

- **read-summary, ship, agent-setup, condense-claude-md**: Removed GitNexus as a mandated tool across all agent templates and skills. An A/B test (same task, agent with GitNexus vs. grep-only) showed `impact()` false-negatived real callers (missed IoC-container/constructor-injected call sites) and false-positived noise on unrelated symbols — the GitNexus-using agent produced a wrong conclusion (flagged intentional DB-cascade behavior as a bug) that the grep-only agent caught correctly. A second test showed the model won't reach for GitNexus unprompted even when primed by the enforcement rule — it loaded the tool schema, stated intent to use it, then defaulted to grep anyway. `read-summary` Step 7, `ship` Step 5 (re-index), the six `agent-setup` templates' commented-in tool grants, and `condense-claude-md`'s gitnexus-block-preservation rules are all removed. Global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` GitNexus Enforcement section replaced with a dated note explaining the abandonment and the bar for reintroducing it.

## 1.64.10

- **condense-claude-md**: Process #6 already warned that reformatting prose into a table isn't real compression — but a `CLAUDE.local.md` full of `Fact | Detail` credential/ID/gotcha rows hits a *different* dead-end for a different reason: every row is already a unique atomic fact (an MID, a container name, a client secret) with nothing to restate or merge. A light-touch pass on exactly this shape moved only 289→284 lines, 29.4kb→28.3kb (~4%) before the seam-test split did the real work — the same wasted-pass pattern the prose-to-table warning exists to prevent, just triggered by an already-dense file instead of a padded one. Added a parallel callout: skim a few rows first, and if none share a topic or restate another row's mechanism, skip straight to offering the seam-test split instead of spending a pass on light compression.

## 1.64.9

- **update-plugin**: Step 1's scan missed the very `read-summary` defect fixed in 1.64.8 — because that mistake happened several turns before `/update-plugin` was invoked, had already been corrected in-conversation (re-read the doc once the user pushed back), and so felt closed by the time the scan ran. The scan had implicitly scoped "the session" to the turn(s) immediately preceding invocation, not the full transcript. A corrected mistake still means the SKILL's wording let it happen — fixing the instance doesn't patch the skill, only logging it does. Step 1 now states explicitly: scan the whole transcript from the first message, not backward from the most recent tool call: a user correction anywhere is the strongest signal this skill exists to catch.

## 1.64.8

- **read-summary**: Step 3 said to "check the `Related:` field for other linked docs" — phrased as a lookup, not a mandate to read every entry. Live miss: `exam-taking/current.md`'s `Related:` listed `upload-redesign`, its title read as QC/extraction-scoped so it got silently skipped, and the user had to ask "no related task docs?" before it got read — at which point it turned out to share the same subsystem (same B11 fix, same pivot columns) and was the single most load-bearing related doc. Same failure Step 2 already guards against for split-doc `decisions/*.md` files ("don't rely on the generic field, read the actual files") — now applied to cross-domain `Related:` docs too: read every one, a title guess doesn't earn a "not relevant" conclusion, only an actual read does.

## 1.64.7

- **merge-task-docs**: Step 4's size-budget rule ("condense aggressively" to stay under 300 lines) and Step 7's validation gate ("no rows deleted, facts absorbed not dropped") directly contradicted each other whenever two merge candidates were each already dense and near 300 lines — condensing enough to fit one flat file meant deleting real facts. Hit live: `exam-taking` (297 lines) + `upload-redesign` (289 lines) shared the same subsystem (pivot config, B11 scoring) but combined to ~580 lines with nothing left to cut. Added the split-doc escape hatch (index `current.md` + `decisions/<theme>.md` files, already documented in `condense-task-doc`'s `templates.md` but never cross-referenced here) as Step 4.8, and reworded the "bad merge signal: >300 lines" line — size alone isn't a reason to keep a same-subsystem pair apart, it's a reason to split instead of forcing one file.
- **merge-task-docs**: Step 1 said to read all merge candidates but never checked whether one was already a dead redirect stub from a prior merge. Found two (`bi-format`, `exam-sets`) still on disk months after being "merged" — Step 5's "no redirect stubs" rule prevents creating new ones but did nothing about cleaning up old ones left by earlier sessions. Step 1 now flags stub docs on sight and routes them through the same delete + back-ref reconciliation as a real merge target.

## 1.64.6

- **done**: the Step-1 agent roll-call was a *speech act*, not a constraint — the skill said "state the roll-call out loud (N reviewers + N simplifiers + 1 product)", and saying it bound nothing. Stated the roll-call correctly ("1 + 1 + 1 = 3 agents"), then emitted a single `Agent` call in the very next block. Nothing errored: no mechanism ties the spoken number to the actual tool-call count, and the sentence itself manufactures a feeling of compliance ("I did the ritual") right before the violation. Same failure family as 1.64.5 — a loud existing rule broken anyway — so the fix is not to shout it louder: the roll-call is now a **count check on the block being composed** (write the literal list of `subagent_type` + role, one line each; if it's shorter than TOTAL, stop and add them *before* sending), plus an explicit "all agents in ONE tool-call block — a follow-up message to add the missing ones means the step already ran wrong."
- **done**: nothing forbade sending an agent a prompt whose ROLE didn't match its `subagent_type` — a product-review prompt went to `code-reviewer`, so neither the code review nor the product review actually ran, yet the agent still counted as "spawned". The Exit gate checked only that agents *were spawned*, which a mis-prompted agent satisfies, so the gate would have passed while the review never happened. Added the ❌/✅ row, and the Exit gate now requires each row be substantiated by the agent's **prompt matching its role**, not just the `subagent_type` you named.
- **done**: noted that `run_in_background: false` is most often dropped on the FIRST agent of a batch — i.e. exactly when composing calls one at a time instead of emitting the whole block.

## 1.64.5

- **merge-task-docs**: the back-reference scan shipped `rg -rn` in three literal copy-pasteable commands — and `rg` has **no recursive flag** (it always recurses), so `-r` is `--replace`: the command printed every match with the searched path *substituted away*, exit 0, no error. Step 3 scans for back-references and Step 6's exit condition is literally "zero results = done" — with the source docs already deleted by Step 5. A corrupted scan therefore reads as a clean all-clear and the merge finishes with dangling pointers. Switched to `grep -rn` (in ugrep, `-r` genuinely *is* recursive), and the final scan now carries a must-hit control line, because a zero from a broken search is indistinguishable from a genuinely clean tree.
- **read-summary**: discovery told the reader to use the `Grep`/`Glob` tools but said nothing about the fallback when those tools aren't available in a session — so the fallback became Bash `rg`, with the `-r` footgun and no warning. Found the hard way: a discovery search typed `rg -ril "payout"` intending *recursive, case-insensitive, list-files*, and got back output with every "payout" replaced by "il" (`-r` consumed the `l` as its replacement string). The rule already existed, loudly, in global CLAUDE.md — and was broken anyway, because at discovery time the mental frame is "I'm finding a doc", not "I'm writing a shell command". So the fix isn't another copy of the rule: it names `grep` as the mandated fallback (safe by construction) and gives the *tell* — your search term is missing from its own output.

## 1.64.4

- **update-plugin**: new rule — a fix to how one skill handles a shared mechanism (a field/table/convention several skills read or write) is a fix to all of them. The signal-scan in Step 1 is deliberately scoped to the current session, so nothing forced a check of sibling skills touching the same mechanism — until now that meant a fix could regress right back in the next time a sibling skill ran unchanged.
- **condense-task-doc** + **merge-task-docs**: both preserved the LLM-CONTEXT `Last updated` field literally ("keep all fields"), which would have silently reintroduced 1.64.3's fixed bug — a condense or merge pass copying forward a duplicated commit/deploy-status phrase instead of collapsing it to Quick Start. Both now state the same rule task-summary does: `Last updated` names the date + what changed, never commit/deploy status prose.

## 1.64.3

- **task-summary**: new Layer-1 rule — commit/deploy status ("uncommitted" / "committed, not yet deployed" / "LIVE in production") is one fact with one home, same as any other. Found the hard way: mid-ship, the status got written into LLM-CONTEXT's `Last updated:` line, Quick Start's state line, and `## Last Session` — three places restating a phrase that was about to go stale within minutes of the actual deploy completing. The user flagged it directly ("too bothersome to update, just update 1 place"). `Last updated:` now points at Quick Start instead of mirroring its wording.

## 1.64.2

- **read-summary**: a doc that **researched** a third-party tool no longer reads as license to **run** it. The authoritative-for table treated "what the doc knows" vs "what the running system knows" as the only two axes, so a doc summarizing an external tool felt like complete grounding — and its "go MEASURE the live system" antidote actively points the wrong way here: you can profile every byte of the disk and still not know what the tool deletes, because that answer lives in the tool's source, not your machine's state. Found the hard way: a research doc on a Mac cleanup CLI was read, the disk was measured, a cleanup was planned — and the user had to ask *"did you research the github first?"*. Reading the actual source then inverted the plan: the tool deliberately treats Laravel `vendor/` as unprotected, and had permanently deleted `~/.claude/projects/*/memory/` (the session's own working dir) for other users. New table row + callout, and the **Doc path** intent branch — whose "wait for the user's next instruction" reads as permission to stop reading — now flags that a path handed to you *alongside an action* is a doc path **plus** a task. The tell that you skipped it: you cannot name, from code you read, the exact paths the command touches.

## 1.64.1

- **done**: Step 5's gate now catches **the signal you CAUGHT**, which is the one it kept missing. If you declined to follow a skill's step, worked around it, or told the user "this step doesn't apply here" — that IS the defect, and it's the hardest one to notice precisely because it feels like a win ("I handled it"), so it gets filed as competence instead of a finding. The gate's wording was calibrated entirely against false positives (*"most runs have none"*, *"never manufacture one"*, *"a thin patch is worse than no patch"*) with nothing guarding the other direction. Found the hard way: this session hand-rolled a correct branch chain *because* `/ship`'s Step 3 was wrong, then skipped Step 5 anyway. New check: *did I deviate from any skill's written instructions this session, and why?* — a deviation with a good reason is a skill that needs the reason written into it.
- **ship**: Step 3 no longer assumes **`git push` on the current branch IS the deploy**. It isn't, on any project that promotes through a chain (`master → staging → production`), and the failure is silent in the worst way: pushing `master` can deploy *nothing* (production needs a forward-merge plus a manual CI gate) or deploy to *staging* while the skill reports "shipped to production" — CI goes green either way, so the verify step's green tick confirms the wrong thing. Worse, the deploy branch differs **per repo**: in a two-repo ship one repo's `master` was its staging branch while the other's deployed nothing at all, so a single `git push` per repo produced two different wrong outcomes at once. Step 3 now requires establishing the deploy branch first (from `CLAUDE.md`/`CLAUDE.local.md`, confirmed against the repo), recognising a forward-merge chain as a *merge*, not a push (`--ff-only` fails on diverged branches), checking for a manual gate, and surfacing any migrations riding along — a one-way door that belongs in the user's decision, not a silent side effect. If the chain isn't documented and can't be inferred: **ask**, because pushing to a deploy branch is outward-facing and hard to reverse.

## 1.64.0

- **done**: new **Infra-only mode** — the mirror image of docs-only. When the diff is entirely deploy/build/CI plumbing (CI workflows, `docker-compose*.yml`, `Dockerfile`, `next.config.*`, nginx/env), run the **reviewer only**: there's no logic to DRY and no user journey, but infra needs review *badly* because it fails **silently in production** (a `docker compose exec` swallowing a heredoc's stdin; `nginx -s reload` re-reading an orphaned inode; an undeclared `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` build arg dropped at compile time — all pass lint/typecheck/tests). Size-independent: the trigger is file KIND, not count. Also warns against blanket-applying a fix to every occurrence of a pattern — two call sites of the same command can need opposite treatments. Exception: a compose change that flips a feature flag ON is not infra-only (user-facing capability → product reviewer).
- **read-summary** — four fixes, all from this skill running and *still* letting the session get things wrong:
  - **Trigger now names deploy/go-live questions** ("if we deploy staging to production, what do we need to do?"). These were a blind spot: they name no feature and report no bug, so the old description didn't match — yet prerequisites, blockers, and env state live in task docs and *nowhere in the code*. Now a PRIME trigger, not an edge case.
  - **New: what the doc is authoritative FOR.** A task doc is authoritative for *decisions* and *gotchas*; it is **not a live-state oracle** (what's in prod's DB/`.env`/a bucket right now decays the moment anyone touches a server). The trap is that a well-read doc makes you feel fully grounded, so you answer a live-state question from a weeks-stale snapshot with total confidence. If the answer depends on a running system, **measure it** — the doc's claim about prod is a hypothesis to test, and when they disagree the live system wins.
  - **Never infer a REMOTE system's state from a LOCAL file's absence.** "Prod's `.env` has no `AWS_*`" means *this machine isn't wired to S3* — it says nothing about whether the bucket exists. Ask the remote system with a call that discriminates (`HeadBucket`: 403 exists-denied vs 404 absent).
  - **Sibling-repo rule promoted to its own Read Order step**, gated on the QUESTION rather than on which files you'll edit. It had been a sub-bullet of the CLAUDE.md tree-walk — which fires per-directory-you-edit — so a read-only planning question skipped it precisely when it mattered most. (Sibling `CLAUDE.md`/`CLAUDE.local.md` never auto-load: the harness walks from the working dir only, and it hides itself — the current repo's files *did* load, so context feels complete while you re-derive facts the sibling already documents.)
- **task-summary**: §6 back-reference reconciliation now also greps the **defect's own vocabulary**, not just the feature's. A doc describing a bug you just fixed is stale in a way a feature-name grep structurally cannot find — it may never name the feature, only the defect. Higher severity than a stale status: a stale *status* is merely out of date, a stale *gotcha* actively misleads the next session into re-fixing a solved bug or routing around a non-problem.
- **done** ⇄ **update-plugin**: `/done` gains a **conditional Step 5** that closes the loop — a session that exposes a skill defect now feeds it back into the plugin instead of losing it.
